“我走在一条小径上，朝着这片土地的领主城堡走去，两边是令人愉快但被禁止的草地，有着不同的名字。一个人和我同行，他喋喋不休地谈论着沉思的山脉和低垂的邻近云朵；谈论着西风和正在发芽的果树。他辩论着这些事物的意义，他迷路去采紫罗兰，而我走在踏出的小径上。

“他喋喋不休地谈论着宜人的葡萄酒和女人敏捷的嘴唇，摇摆的香炉和牧师平静的面容，以及面包和黄油那清晰可爱的色彩，他的心被这个充满美丽的世界所困扰。他跳过一道篱笆去与一只垂死的狗分享他分得的口粮，后来，饿了，就翻墙去偷苹果，而我走在踏出的小径上。

“他喋喋不休地谈论着秋天的破产和春天古老的谎言承诺；谈论着他自己想要安息的欲望；谈论着流水和腐烂的叶子。他喋喋不休地谈论着遥远的星星；他辩论着它们是上帝的眼睛还是燃烧的气体，他用逻辑证明两者都不存在。有时他绊倒了，一边环顾四周一边咀嚼着苹果，弄得浑身是泥，但我走在踏出的小径上。

“小径通向一座城堡的入口，穿过入口。‘我们不要进去，’他说，‘因为城堡是空的，而且，我非常害怕，此外，我还没有吃完所有的苹果。’他大声哭泣，但我不害怕，因为我一直走在踏出的小径上。”

===Original===
Again there was a silence. "You paint a dreary world, my Prince."

===Chinese===
又是一阵沉默。“您描绘了一个沉闷的世界，我的王子。”

===Original===
"My little Miguel, I paint the world as the Eternal Father made it. The laws of the place are written large, so that all may read them; and we know that every road, whether it be my trodden path or some byway through your gayer meadows, yet leads in the end to God. We have our choice﻿—or to come to Him as a laborer comes at evening for the day’s wages fairly earned, or to come as a roisterer haled before the magistrate."

===Chinese===
“我的小米格尔，我描绘的世界是天父创造的世界。这个地方的法则写得很大，让所有人都能读懂；我们知道每条路，无论它是我踏出的小径还是穿过你那更欢快草地的小径，最终都通向上帝。我们有选择——要么像工人在晚上来领取一天应得的工资那样来到他面前，要么像闹事者被带到法官面前那样来到他面前。”

===Original===
"I consider you to be in the right," the boy said, after a lengthy interval, "although I decline﻿—and decline emphatically﻿—to believe you."

===Chinese===
“我认为您是对的，”经过长时间的停顿后，男孩说，“虽然我拒绝——坚决拒绝——相信您。”

===Original===
The Prince laughed. "There spoke Youth," he said, and he sighed as though he were a patriarch. "But we have sung, we two, the Eternal Tenson of God’s will and of man’s desires. And I claim the prize, my Little Miguel."

===Chinese===
王子笑了。“那是青春在说话，”他说，他叹息着，仿佛自己是一位族长。“但我们两人唱了上帝意志和人类欲望的永恒争辩曲（Tenson）。我要求赢得奖品，我的小米格尔。”

===Original===
Suddenly the page kissed one huge hand. "You have conquered, my very dull and very glorious Prince. Concerning that Hawise﻿—" But Miguel de Rueda choked. "Oh, I do not understand! and yet in part I understand!" the boy wailed in the darkness.

===Chinese===
侍童突然亲吻了一只巨大的手。“您征服了，我非常迟钝又非常光荣的王子。关于哈维斯——”但米格尔·德·鲁埃达哽咽了。“哦，我不明白！然而我又部分明白！”男孩在黑暗中哀号。

===Original===
And the Prince laid one hand upon his page’s hair, and smiled in the darkness to note how soft was this hair, since the man was less a fool than at first view you might have taken him to be; and he said:

"One must play the game out fairly, my lad. We are no little people, she and I, the children of many kings, of God’s regents here on earth; and it was never reasonable, my Miguel, that gentlefolk should cheat at their dicing."

===Chinese===
王子把手放在侍童的头发上，在黑暗中微笑着，注意到这头发是多么柔软，因为这个人并不像初看时你可能认为的那样愚蠢；他说：

“一个人必须公平地把游戏玩到底，我的小伙子。她和我都不是小人物，我们是众多国王的子孙，是上帝在人间的摄政者；而上等人在掷骰子时作弊从来都是不合理的，我的米格尔。”

===Original===
The same night Miguel de Rueda repeated the prayer which Saint Theophilus made long ago to the Mother of God:

"Dame, je n’ose,
Flors d’aiglentier et lis et rose,
En qui li filz Diex se repose,"

and so on. Or, in other wording: "Hearken, O gracious Lady! thou that art more fair than any flower of the eglantine, more comely than the blossoming of the rose or of the lily! thou to whom was confided the very Son of God! Harken, for I am afraid! afford counsel to me that am ensnared by Satan and know not what to do! Never will I make an end of praying. O Virgin débonnaire! O honored Lady! Thou that wast once a woman﻿—!"

===Chinese===
当晚，米格尔·德·鲁埃达重复了圣狄奥菲卢斯（Saint Theophilus）很久以前向圣母（Mother of God）所做的祈祷：

“夫人，我不敢，
野蔷薇、百合和玫瑰的花，
上帝之子在其中安息，”

等等。或者，换一种措辞：“倾听吧，哦仁慈的夫人！你比任何野蔷薇的花都美丽，比玫瑰或百合的盛开更可爱！你曾被托付上帝之子本人！倾听吧，因为我害怕！给我建议，我被撒旦诱捕，不知所措！我永不会停止祈祷。哦温柔的圣母！哦尊贵的夫人！你曾是一个女人——！”

===Original===
So he prayed, and upon the next day as these two rode southward, he sang half as if in defiance.

===Chinese===
他如此祈祷，第二天当两人骑马南下时，他半是挑衅地唱着歌。

===Original===
Sang Miguel:

"And still﻿—whatever years impend
To witness Time a fickle friend,
And Youth a dwindling fire﻿—
I must adore till all years end
My first love, Heart’s Desire.

"I may not hear men speak of her
Unmoved, and vagrant pulses stir
To greet her passing-by,
And I, in all her worshipper
Must serve her till I die.

"For I remember: this is she
That reigns in one man’s memory
Immune to age and fret,
And stays the maid I may not see
Nor win to, nor forget."

===Chinese===
米格尔唱道：

“然而——无论岁月如何临近，
见证时光是个反复无常的朋友，
青春是渐弱的火焰——
我必须崇拜，直到岁月终结
我最初的爱，心灵的渴望。

“我不能听到人们谈论她
而无动于脉，流浪的脉搏悸动
去迎接她走过，
而我，在她所有的崇拜者中
必须侍奉她直到死亡。

“因为我记得：她就是她，
在一个人的记忆中统治，
免受岁月和烦恼的侵扰，
她是那个我无法看见
也无法赢得，也无法忘记的少女。”

===Original===
It was on the following day, near Bazas, that these two encountered Adam de Gourdon, a Provençal knight, with whom the Prince fought for a long while, without either contestant giving way; in consequence a rendezvous was fixed for the November of that year, and afterward the Prince and de Gourdon parted, highly pleased with each other.

===Chinese===
就在第二天，在巴扎（Bazas）附近，两人遇到了普罗旺斯骑士亚当·德·古尔东（Adam de Gourdon），王子与他战了很长时间，双方都没有让步；因此约定当年十一月再战，之后王子和德·古尔东分别，彼此非常满意。

===Original===
Thus the Prince and his attendant came, in late September, to Mauléon, on the Castilian frontier, and dined there at the Fir Cone. Three or four lackeys were about﻿—some exalted person’s retinue? Prince Edward hazarded to the swart little landlord, as the Prince and Miguel lingered over the remnants of their meal.

===Chinese===
于是王子和他的随从在九月底来到了卡斯蒂利亚边境的莫莱翁（Mauléon），在松果旅店（the Fir Cone）用餐。三四个仆人正在附近——是某位显贵的随从吗？爱德华王子向黝黑的小旅店老板试探道，他和米格尔还在慢慢享用他们的残羹冷炙。

===Original===
Yes, the fellow informed them: the Prince de Gâtinais had lodged there for a whole week, watching the north road, as circumspect of all passage as a cat over a mouse-hole. Eh, monseigneur expected someone, doubtless﻿—a lady, it might be﻿—the gentlefolk had their escapades like everyone else. The innkeeper babbled vaguely, for on a sudden he was very much afraid of his gigantic patron.

===Chinese===
是的，那人告诉他们：加蒂内亲王在那里住了一整周，守望着北方的道路，像猫守着老鼠洞一样警惕着所有通行。唉，大人无疑是在等什么人——可能是一位女士——上流人士和普通人一样也有他们的冒险。旅店老板含糊其辞地闲聊着，因为他突然非常害怕他这位巨大的主顾。

===Original===
"You will show me to his room," Prince Edward said, with a politeness that was ingratiating.

===Chinese===
“带我去他的房间，”爱德华王子说，带着一种讨好的礼貌。

===Original===
The host shuddered and obeyed.

===Chinese===
店主颤抖着服从了。

===Original===
Miguel de Rueda, left alone, sat quite silent, his fingertips drumming upon the table. He rose suddenly and flung back his shoulders, all resolution. On the stairway he passed the black little landlord, who was now in a sad twitter, foreseeing bloodshed. But Miguel de Rueda went on to the room above. The door was ajar. He paused there.

===Chinese===
米格尔·德·鲁埃达被独自留下，静静地坐着，指尖在桌上敲打着。他突然站起来，挺起肩膀，下定了决心。在楼梯上他经过了那个黝黑的小旅店老板，那人正惊恐万分，预见到流血事件。但米格尔·德·鲁埃达继续走向楼上的房间。门半开着。他停在那里。

===Original===
De Gâtinais had risen from his dinner and stood facing the door. He, too, was a blond man and the comeliest of his day. And at sight of him awoke in the woman’s heart all the old tenderness; handsome and brave and witty she knew him to be, as indeed the whole world knew him to be distinguished by every namable grace; and the innate weakness of de Gâtinais, which she alone suspected, made him now seem doubly dear. Fiercely she wanted to shield him, less from bodily hurt than from that self-degradation which she cloudily apprehended to be at the hand; the test was come, and Etienne would fail. Thus much she knew with a sick, illimitable surety, and she loved de Gâtinais with a passion which dwarfed comprehension.

===Chinese===
德·加蒂内已从晚餐中起身，面对着门站着。他也是个金发男子，是他那个时代最英俊的人。一看到他，女人心中就唤醒了所有旧日的温情；她知道他英俊、勇敢、机智，正如整个世界都知道他拥有所有可以命名的优雅；而德·加蒂内天生的弱点，只有她一人察觉，此刻使他显得更加可贵。她强烈地想要保护他，与其说是为了保护他免受身体伤害，不如说是为了保护他免受她隐约预感到的自我贬低；考验已经来临，艾蒂安会失败。她带着一种病态的、无边的确信知道这一点，她以一种让理解相形见绌的激情爱着德·加蒂内。

===Original===
"O Madame the Virgin!" prayed Miguel de Rueda, "thou that wast once a woman, even as I am now a woman! grant that the man may slay him quickly! grant that he may slay Etienne very quickly, honored Lady, so that my Etienne may die unshamed!"

===Chinese===
“哦圣母夫人！”米格尔·德·鲁埃达祈祷道，“你曾是一个女人，正如我现在是一个女人！愿那人能迅速杀死他！愿他能迅速杀死艾蒂安，尊敬的夫人，这样我的艾蒂安就能死得不蒙羞！”

===Original===
"I must question, messire," de Gâtinais was saying, "whether you have been well inspired. Yes, quite frankly, I do await the arrival of her who is your nominal wife; and your intervention at this late stage, I take it, can have no outcome save to render you absurd. So, come now! be advised by me, messire﻿—"

===Chinese===
“我必须质疑，阁下，”德·加蒂内正在说，“您是否得到了良好的启示。是的，坦率地说，我确实在等待您名义上的妻子的到来；而您在这最后阶段的干预，我认为，除了让您显得荒谬之外别无他用。所以，来吧！接受我的建议吧，阁下——”

===Original===
Prince Edward said, "I am not here to talk."

===Chinese===
爱德华王子说：“我来这里不是为了说话。”

===Original===
"—For, messire, I grant you that in ordinary disputation the cutting of one gentleman’s throat by another gentleman is well enough, since the argument is unanswerable. Yet in this case we have each of us too much to live for; you to govern your reconquered England, and I﻿—you perceive that I am candid﻿—to achieve in turn the kingship of another realm. Now to secure this realm, possession of the Lady Ellinor is to me essential; to you she is nothing."

===Chinese===
“——因为，阁下，我承认在普通争论中，一位绅士割断另一位绅士的喉咙是很好的，因为这个论点是无可辩驳的。然而在这种情况下，我们每个人都有太多活下去的理由；您要治理您收复的英格兰，而我——您看出我很坦率——要轮到我去实现另一个王国的王位。现在要确保这个王国，拥有埃莉诺女士对我至关重要；对您来说她什么都不是。”

===Original===
"She is a woman whom I have deeply wronged," Prince Edward said, "and to whom, God willing, I mean to make atonement. Ten years ago they wedded us, willy-nilly, to avert the impending war between Spain and England; today El Sabio intends to purchase Germany with her body as the price; you to get Sicily as her husband. Mort de Dieu! is a woman thus to be bought and sold like hog’s flesh! We have other and cleaner customs, we of England."

===Chinese===
“她是一个我深深伤害过的女人，”爱德华王子说，“而上帝保佑，我打算向她赎罪。十年前他们强行让我们结婚，以阻止西班牙和英格兰之间迫在眉睫的战争；今天智者阿方索打算用她的身体作为代价购买德国；您则想作为她的丈夫得到西西里。天杀的！一个女人就这样像猪肉一样被买卖！我们英格兰人有其他更干净的习俗。”

===Original===
"Eh, and who purchased the woman first?" de Gâtinais spat at him, viciously, for the Frenchman now saw his air-castle shaken to the cornerstone.

===Chinese===
“唉，是谁先买了这个女人？”德·加蒂内恶毒地向他吐口水，因为这个法国人现在看到他的空中楼阁被撼动到了根基。

===Original===
"They wedded me to the child in order that a great war might be averted. I acquiesced, since it appeared preferable that two people suffer inconvenience rather than many thousands be slain. And still this is my view of the matter. Yet afterward I failed her. Love had no clause in our agreement; but I owed her more protection than I have afforded. England has long been no place for women. I thought she would comprehend that much. But I know very little of women. Battle and death are more wholesome companions, I now perceive, than such folk as you and Alphonso. Woman is the weaker vessel﻿—the negligence was mine﻿—I may not blame her." The big and simple man was in an agony of repentance.

===Chinese===
“他们把我嫁给这个孩子，是为了避免一场大战。我默许了，因为两个人遭受不便似乎比成千上万人被杀要好。这仍然是我对这件事的看法。然而后来我辜负了她。爱在我们的协议中没有条款；但我欠她的保护比我能提供的要多得多。英格兰长期以来对女人来说不是个好地方。我以为她能理解这一点。但我对女人了解甚少。我现在明白，战斗和死亡比像您和阿方索这样的人是更健康的伴侣。女人是较弱的器皿——疏忽是我的——我不该责备她。”这个高大单纯的人陷入了痛苦的悔恨。

===Original===
On a sudden he strode forward, his sword now shifted to his left hand and his right hand outstretched. "One and all, we are weaklings in the net of circumstance. Shall one herring, then, blame his fellow if his fellow jostle him? We walk as in a mist of error, and Belial is fertile in allurements; yet always it is granted us to behold that sin is sin. I have perhaps sinned through anger, Messire de Gâtinais, more deeply than you have planned to sin through luxury and through ambition. Let us then cry quits, Messire de Gâtinais, and afterward part in peace, and in common repentance."

===Chinese===
突然他大步向前，剑已换到左手，右手伸了出来。“所有人，我们都是环境之网中的弱者。那么，一条鲱鱼会责怪它的同伴如果同伴撞到它吗？我们如同在错误的迷雾中行走，别西卜（Belial）擅长诱惑；然而总是允许我们看到罪恶就是罪恶。我也许因愤怒而犯罪，德·加蒂内阁下，比您计划因奢华和野心而犯的罪更深重。那么我们就算扯平吧，德·加蒂内阁下，然后和平分手，共同忏悔。”

===Original===
"And yield you Ellinor?" de Gâtinais said. "Oh no, messire, I reply to you with Arnaud de Marveil, that marvellous singer of eld, 'They may bear her from my presence, but they can never untie the knot which unites my heart to her; for that heart, so tender and so constant, God alone divides with my lady, and the portion which God possesses He holds but as a part of her domain, and as her vassal.'"

===Chinese===
“把埃莉诺让给您？”德·加蒂内说。“哦不，阁下，我以阿尔诺·德·马尔维勒（Arnaud de Marveil），那位古代奇妙歌手的话回答您，‘他们可以把她从我身边带走，但他们永远解不开将我的心与她连结的纽带；因为那颗心，如此温柔又如此忠诚，只有上帝与我的夫人分享，而上帝所拥有的部分，他不过是作为她领地的一部分，作为她的附庸来持有。’”

===Original===
"This is blasphemy," Prince Edward now retorted, "and for such observations alone you merit death. Will you always talk and talk and talk? I perceive that the devil is far more subtle than you, messire, and leads you, like a pig with a ring in his nose, toward gross iniquity. Messire, I tell you that for your soul’s health I doubly mean to kill you now. So let us make an end of this."

===Chinese===
“这是亵渎，”爱德华王子反驳道，“仅仅为这样的言论你就该死。你总是说个没完吗？我看出魔鬼比您狡猾得多，阁下，他像牵着猪鼻子上的环一样，引您走向严重的邪恶。阁下，我告诉您，为了您灵魂的健康，我现在加倍地想要杀死您。所以让我们结束这一切吧。”

===Original===
De Gâtinais turned and took up his sword. "Since you will have it," he rather regretfully said; "yet I reiterate that you play an absurd part. Your wife has deserted you, has fled in abhorrence of you. For three weeks she has been tramping God knows whither or in what company﻿—"

===Chinese===
德·加蒂内转身拿起他的剑。“既然您坚持如此，”他颇为遗憾地说；“然而我重申您扮演着一个荒谬的角色。您的妻子抛弃了您，在厌恶中逃跑了。三周以来她一直跋涉着，天知道去了哪里，和谁在一起——”

===Original===
He was here interrupted. "What the Lady Ellinor has done," Prince Edward crisply said, "was at my request. We were wedded at Burgos; it was natural that we should desire our reunion to take place at Burgos; and she came to Burgos with an escort which I provided."

===Chinese===
他在这里被打断了。“埃莉诺女士所做的一切，”爱德华王子干脆地说，“是应我的请求。我们在布尔戈斯结婚；我们自然希望我们的重逢在布尔戈斯举行；她带着我提供的护卫来到了布尔戈斯。”

===Original===
De Gâtinais sneered. "So that is the tale you will deliver to the world?"

===Chinese===
德·加蒂内讥讽道：“所以这就是您要向世人讲述的故事？”

===Original===
"After I have slain you," the Prince said, "yes."

===Chinese===
“在我杀死您之后，”王子说，“是的。”

===Original===
"The reservation is wise. For if I were dead, Messire Edward, there would be none to know that you risk all for a drained goblet, for an orange already squeezed﻿—quite dry, messire."

===Chinese===
“这个保留很明智。因为如果我死了，爱德华阁下，就没有人知道您为了一个喝干的酒杯，为了一个已经榨干的橙子——完全干了，阁下——而冒一切风险。”

===Original===
"Face of God!" the Prince said.

===Chinese===
“上帝的面容！”王子说。

===Original===
But de Gâtinais flung back both arms in a great gesture, so that he knocked a flask of claret from the table at his rear. "I am candid, my Prince. I would not see any brave gentleman slain in a cause so foolish. In consequence I kiss and tell. In effect, I was eloquent, I was magnificent, so that in the end her reserve was shattered like the wooden flask yonder at our feet. Is it worth while, think you, that our blood flow like this flagon’s contents?"

===Chinese===
但德·加蒂内双臂向后猛地一挥，碰倒了身后桌子上的一瓶红酒。“我很坦率，我的王子。我不希望任何勇敢的绅士为了如此愚蠢的原因被杀。因此我亲吻并讲述。实际上，我口才很好，我非常精彩，以至于最终她的矜持像我们脚边那个木瓶一样破碎了。您认为，值得让我们的血像这酒壶里的东西一样流淌吗？”

===Original===
"Liar!" Prince Edward said, very softly. "O hideous liar! Already your eyes shift!" He drew near and struck the Frenchman. "Talk and talk and talk! and lying talk! I am ashamed while I share the world with a thing as base as you."

===Chinese===
“骗子！”爱德华王子轻声说。“哦可怕的骗子！你的眼睛已经在躲闪了！”他走近打了那个法国人。“说个不停！而且说谎！与你这样卑鄙的东西共享世界，我感到羞耻。”

===Original===
De Gâtinais hurled upon him, cursing, sobbing in an abandoned fury. In an instant the place resounded like a smithy, for there were no better swordsmen living than these two. The eavesdropper could see nothing clearly. Round and round they veered in a whirl of turmoil. Presently Prince Edward trod upon the broken flask, smashing it. His foot slipped in the spilth of wine, and the huge body went down like an oak, his head striking one leg of the table.

===Chinese===
德·加蒂内扑向他，咒骂着，在放纵的愤怒中抽泣着。瞬间那个地方像铁匠铺一样响声大作，因为活着的剑客中没有人比他们俩更出色。偷听者什么也看不清楚。他们在混乱的漩涡中一圈又一圈地旋转。不久爱德华王子踩到了破碎的酒瓶，把它踩碎了。他的脚在洒出的葡萄酒上滑了一下，巨大的身体像橡树一样倒下，头撞到了桌子的一条腿。

===Original===
"A candle!" de Gâtinais cried, and he panted now﻿—"a hundred candles to the Virgin of Beaujolais!" He shortened his sword to stab the Prince of England.

===Chinese===
“一支蜡烛！”德·加蒂内喊道，他现在喘着气——“向博若莱（Beaujolais）的圣母献上一百支蜡烛！”他缩短了剑准备刺杀英格兰王子。

===Original===
The eavesdropper came through the doorway, and flung herself between Prince Edward and the descending sword. The sword dug deep into her shoulder, so that she shrieked once with the cold pain of this wound. Then she rose, ashen. "Liar!" she said. "Oh, I am shamed while I share the world with a thing as base as you!"

===Chinese===
偷听者从门口进来，扑在爱德华王子和落下的剑之间。剑深深地刺入了她的肩膀，她因这伤口的冰冷疼痛而尖叫了一声。然后她站起来，脸色苍白。“骗子！”她说。“哦，与你这样卑鄙的东西共享世界，我感到羞耻！”

===Original===
In silence de Gâtinais regarded her. There was a long interval before he said, "Ellinor!" and then again, "Ellinor!" like a man bewildered.

===Chinese===
德·加蒂内沉默地看着她。过了好一会儿他才说：“埃莉诺！”然后又说：“埃莉诺！”像个困惑的人。

===Original===
" 'I was eloquent, I was magnificent' " she said, " 'so that in the end her reserve was shattered!' Certainly, messire, it is not your death which I desire, since a man dies so very, very quickly. I desire for you﻿—I know not what I desire for you!" the girl wailed.

===Chinese===
“‘我口才很好，我非常精彩，’”她说，“‘以至于最终她的矜持破碎了！’当然，阁下，我渴望的不是您的死，因为一个人死得非常非常快。我渴望给您——我不知道我渴望给您什么！”女孩哀号道。

===Original===
"You desire that I should endure this present moment," de Gâtinais replied; "for as God reigns, I love you, of whom I have spoken infamy, and my shame is very bitter."

===Chinese===
“您渴望我忍受此时此刻，”德·加蒂内回答；“因为上帝作证，我爱您，而我刚才说了您的坏话，我的羞耻非常苦涩。”

===Original===
She said: "And I, too, loved you. It is strange to think of that."

===Chinese===
她说：“我也爱过您。想起这个很奇怪。”

===Original===
"I was afraid. Never in my life have I been afraid before today. But I was afraid of this terrible and fair and righteous man. I saw all hope of you vanish, all hope of Sicily﻿—in effect, I lied as a cornered beast spits out his venom."

===Chinese===
“我害怕了。在我今天之前，我一生中从未害怕过。但我害怕这个可怕、公正而正义的人。我看到对您的一切希望都消失了，对西西里的一切希望都消失了——实际上，我撒了谎，就像一头被逼入绝境的野兽吐出毒液一样。”

===Original===
"I know," she answered. "Give me water, Etienne." She washed and bound the Prince’s head with a vinegar-soaked napkin. Ellinor sat upon the floor, the big man’s head upon her knee. "He will not die of this, for he is of strong person. Look you, Messire de Gâtinais, you and I are not strong. We are so fashioned that we can enjoy only the pleasant things of life. But this man can enjoy﻿—enjoy, mark you﻿—the commission of any act, however distasteful, if he think it to be his duty. There is the difference. I cannot fathom him. But it is now necessary that I become all which he loves﻿—since he loves it﻿—and that I be in thought and deed all which he desires. For I have heard the Tenson through."

===Chinese===
“我知道，”她回答。“给我水，艾蒂安。”她用浸过醋的餐巾为王子清洗并包扎了头部。埃莉诺坐在地板上，那个大个子男人的头枕在她的膝盖上。“他不会因此而死，因为他身体强壮。看着，德·加蒂内阁下，您和我不够坚强。我们的构造使我们只能享受生活中愉快的事物。但这个人可以享受——注意——任何行为的实施，无论多么令人不快，只要他认为这是他的责任。这就是区别。我看不透他。但现在我必须成为他所爱的一切——因为他爱它——并且在思想和行动上成为他所渴望的一切。因为我已经听完了这首争辩曲。”

===Original===
"You love him!" said de Gâtinais.

===Chinese===
“您爱他！”德·加蒂内说。

===Original===
She glanced upward with a pitiable smile. "No, it is you whom I love, my Etienne. You cannot understand how at this very moment every fibre of me﻿—heart, soul, and body﻿—may be longing just to comfort you, and to give you all which you desire, my Etienne, and to make you happy, my handsome Etienne, at however dear a cost. No; you will never understand that. And since you may not understand, I merely bid you go and leave me with my husband."

===Chinese===
她带着可怜的微笑向上瞥了一眼。“不，我爱的是您，我的艾蒂安。您不明白此时此刻我的每一根纤维——心灵、灵魂和身体——都在渴望安慰您，给予您您所渴望的一切，我的艾蒂安，并让您快乐，我英俊的艾蒂安，无论代价多么高昂。不；您永远不会明白。既然您不明白，我只要求您离开，把我留给我的丈夫。”

===Original===
And then there fell between these two an infinite silence.

===Chinese===
接着两人之间陷入了无尽的沉默。

===Original===
"Listen," de Gâtinais said; "grant me some little credit for what I do. You are alone; the man is powerless. My fellows are within call. A word secures the Prince’s death; a word gets me you and Sicily. And I do not speak that word, for you are my lady as well as his, and your will is my one law."

===Chinese===
“听着，”德·加蒂内说；“对我所做的，请给我一点信任。您独自一人；那个人无力反抗。我的同伴就在附近。一句话就能确保王子的死；一句话就能得到您和西西里。而我没有说那句话，因为您既是他的夫人也是我的夫人，您的意志是我唯一的法律。”

===Original===
But there was no mercy in the girl, no more for him than for herself. The big head lay upon her breast; she caressed the gross hair of it ever so lightly. "These are tinsel oaths," she crooned, as if rapt with incurious content; "these are the old empty protestations of all you strutting poets. A word gets you what you desire! Then why do you not speak that word? Why do you not speak many words, and become again as eloquent and as magnificent as you were when you contrived that adultery about which you were just now telling my husband?"

===Chinese===
但女孩心中没有怜悯，对他没有，对自己也没有。那颗巨大的头枕在她的胸膛上；她极其轻柔地抚摸着它粗糙的头发。“这些都是华而不实的誓言，”她低声说，仿佛沉浸在漠不关心的满足中；“这些都是你们所有趾高气扬的诗人们古老的空洞宣言。一句话就能得到你想要的东西！那你为什么不说话？你为什么不说许多话，再次变得像你刚才告诉我丈夫你策划那次通奸时那样口才很好、那样精彩？”

===Original===
De Gâtinais raised clenched hands. "I am shamed," he said; and then he said, "It is just."

===Chinese===
德·加蒂内举起紧握的双手。“我感到羞耻，”他说；然后他说，“这是公正的。”

===Original===
He left the room and presently rode away with his men. I say that, here at last, he had done a knightly deed, but she thought little of it, never raised her head as the troop clattered from Mauléon, with a lessening beat which lapsed now into the blunders of an aging fly who doddered about the window yonder.

===Chinese===
他离开了房间，很快和他的手下骑马离开了。我说，他终于在这里做了一件骑士般的事，但她对此毫不在意，当队伍从莫莱翁咔嗒咔嗒地离开时，她从未抬起头，那逐渐减弱的节奏现在变成了远处一只衰老的苍蝇在窗户附近蹒跚的嗡嗡声。

===Original===
She stayed thus, motionless, her meditations adrift in the future; and that which she foreread left her not all sorry nor profoundly glad, for living seemed by this, though scarcely the merry and colorful business which she had esteemed it, yet immeasurably the more worth while.

===Chinese===
她就这样一动不动地待着，思绪飘向未来；她预见的一切既没有让她完全悲伤，也没有让她深感快乐，因为生活似乎到这时，虽然不再是她曾经认为的那样愉快而多彩的事业，却无限地更加值得过下去。

## III / 第三章

===Original===

The Story of the Rattrap

“Leixant a part le stil dels trobados,

Dos grans dezigs ban combatut ma pensa,

Mas lo voler vers un seguir dispensa:

Yo l’vos publich, amar dretament vos.”

The third novel.﻿—Meregrett of France, thinking to preserve a hoodwinked gentleman, annoys a spider; and by the grace of destiny the web of that cunning insect entraps a butterfly, a wasp, and then a god; who shatters it.

In the year of grace 1298, a little before Candlemas (thus Nicolas begins), came letters to the first King Edward of England from his kinsman and ambassador to France, Earl Edmund of Lancaster. It was perfectly apparent, the Earl wrote, that the French King meant to surrender to the Earl’s lord and brother neither the duchy of Guienne nor the Lady Blanch. This lady, I must tell you, was now affianced to King Edward, whose first wife, Dame Ellinor, had died eight years before this time.

The courier found Sire Edward at Ipswich, midway in celebration of his daughter’s marriage to the Count of Holland. The King read the letters through and began to laugh; and presently broke into a rage such as was possible (men whispered) only to the demon-tainted blood of Oriander’s descendants. Next day the keeper of the privy purse entered upon the household-books a considerable sum “to make good a large ruby and an emerald lost out of his coronet when the King’s Grace was pleased to throw it into the fire”; and upon the same day the King recalled Lancaster. The King then despatched yet another embassy into France to treat about Sire Edward’s marriage. This last embassy was headed by the Earl of Aquitaine: his lieutenant was Lord Pevensey, the King’s natural son by Hawise Bulmer.

===Chinese===

捕鼠器的故事

“抛却游吟诗人的陈词滥调，

两大欲望交战于我心间，

唯愿追随本心，无须矫饰：

我将公之于众，坦荡去爱。”

第三个故事——法兰西的梅勒格蕾特（Meregrett），欲保全一位被蒙蔽的绅士，却惹恼了一只蜘蛛；而命运的恩宠，让那狡黠之虫的罗网先后困住一只蝴蝶、一只黄蜂，继而困住一位神祇；最终将其撕碎。

主恩一二九八年，圣烛节（Candlemas）前夕（尼古拉斯如此开篇），英格兰国王爱德华一世收到了其堂兄兼驻法兰西大使埃德蒙·兰开斯特伯爵（Earl Edmund of Lancaster）的来信。伯爵在信中明确写道，法兰西国王既无意将吉耶纳公国（duchy of Guienne）归还，也无意将布兰奇小姐（Lady Blanch）交予其主上兼兄弟。必须告知您，这位小姐目前已与爱德华国王订婚，国王的首任妻子埃莉诺夫人（Dame Ellinor）已于八年前辞世。

信使在伊普斯维奇（Ipswich）找到了爱德华陛下（Sire Edward），他正忙于操办女儿与荷兰伯爵的婚礼。国王读完信函，先是大笑，随即勃然大怒——据人们低语，唯有被恶灵血脉污染的奥里安德（Oriander）后裔方能如此暴怒。次日，掌管王室内帑的官员在账簿上记下一大笔款项，用于“赔偿陛下盛怒之下将冠冕投入火中时损失的一颗大红宝石与一颗祖母绿”；同日，国王召回了兰开斯特。随后，国王又派遣使团前往法兰西，商谈爱德华陛下的婚事。此次使团由阿基坦伯爵（Earl of Aquitaine）领衔，其副手是佩文西勋爵（Lord Pevensey），乃国王与霍维斯·布尔默（Hawise Bulmer）所生的私生子。

===Original===

The Earl got audience of the French King at Mezelais. Walking alone came this Earl of Aquitaine, with a large retinue, into the hall where the barons of France stood according to their rank; in unadorned russet were the big Earl and his attendants, but upon the scarlets and purples of the French lords many jewels shone: it was as though through a corridor of gayly painted sunlit glass that the grave Earl came to the dais where sat King Philippe.

The King had risen at close sight of the new envoy, and had gulped once or twice, and without speaking, had hurriedly waved his lords out of earshot. The King’s perturbation was very extraordinary.

“Fair cousin,” the Earl now said, without any prelude, “four years ago I was affianced to your sister, Dame Blanch. You stipulated that Gascony be given up to you in guaranty, as a settlement on any children I might have by that incomparable lady. I assented, and yielded you the province, upon the understanding, sworn to according to the faith of loyal kings, that within forty days you assign to me its seignory as your vassal. And I have had of you since then neither my province nor my betrothed wife, but only excuses, Sire Philippe.”

With eloquence the Frenchman touched upon the emergencies to which the public weal so often drives men of high station, and upon his private grief over the necessity﻿—unavoidable, alas!﻿—of returning a hard answer before the council; and became so voluble that Sire Edward merely laughed in that big-lunged and disconcerting way of his, and afterward lodged for a week at Mezelais, nominally passing by his minor title of Earl of Aquitaine, and as his own ambassador.

===Chinese===

伯爵在梅泽莱（Mezelais）获得了觐见法兰西国王的机会。阿基坦伯爵孤身一人步入大厅，身后跟随着庞大的随从队伍，法兰西的男爵们按品级排列；伯爵及其侍从身着朴素的褐色衣袍，而法兰西领主们猩红与紫色的衣袍上却珠光宝气：这位庄重的伯爵仿佛穿过一条阳光斑斓的彩绘玻璃长廊，走向坐在高台上的腓力国王（King Philippe）。

国王在新使节走近时站起身来，哽咽了一两次，未发一言，匆匆挥手示意领主们退避至听不见之处。国王的不安极为罕见。

“堂兄，”伯爵此刻毫不寒暄地开口道，“四年前，我与令妹布兰奇夫人订婚。您要求将加斯科涅（Gascony）作为担保交予您，作为我与那位绝代佳人所生子女的产业。我应允了，并将该省让予您，约定——以忠诚国王的信仰立誓——您在四十天内将其领主权作为附庸授予我。然而自那时起，我既未得到我的省份，也未得到我的未婚妻，唯得借口而已，腓力陛下（Sire Philippe）。”

这位法兰西人雄辩地谈及公共福祉常将身居高位者推向的紧急关头，谈及他在议会上不得不（唉，无可避免！）给出严苛答复时的私人悲痛；他变得如此滔滔不绝，以至于爱德华陛下只是以他那洪亮而令人不安的大笑回应，随后在梅泽莱住了整整一周，名义上是以其阿基坦伯爵的次等头衔，作为他自己的大使。

===Original===

Negotiations became more swift of foot, since a man serves himself with zeal. In addition, the French lords could make nothing of a politician so thick-witted that he replied to every consideration of expediency with a parrot-like reiteration of the circumstance that already the bargain was signed and sworn to: in consequence, while daily they fumed over his stupidity, daily he gained his point. During this period he was, upon one pretext or another, very often in the company of his affianced wife, Dame Blanch.

This lady, I must tell you, was the handsomest of her day; there could nowhere be found a creature more agreeable to every sense; and she compelled the adoring regard of men, it is recorded, not gently but in an imperious fashion. Sire Edward, who, till this, had loved her merely by report, and, in accordance with the high custom of old, through many perusals of her portrait, now appeared besotted. He was an aging man, near sixty, huge and fair, with a crisp beard, and the bright unequal eyes of Manuel of Poictesme. The better-read at Mezelais began to liken this so candidly enamored monarch and his Princess to Sieur Hercules at the feet of Queen Omphale.

The court hunted and slew a stag of ten in the woods of Ermenoueïl, which stand thick about the château; and at the hunt’s end, these two had dined at Rigon the forester’s hut, in company with Dame Meregrett, the French King’s younger sister. She sat a little apart from the betrothed, and stared through the hut’s one window. We know, nowadays, it was not merely the trees she was considering.

===Chinese===

谈判进展更为迅速，因为人总为自己效力而格外卖力。此外，法兰西的领主们无法理解这位如此愚钝的政治家——他对每一项权宜之计都以鹦鹉学舌般的固执重申旧约已签已誓：因此，尽管他们日日为其愚蠢而恼怒，他却日日得偿所愿。在此期间，他常以各种借口与未婚妻布兰奇夫人相伴。

必须告诉您，这位夫人是当时最美丽的女子；举世无双，令感官皆悦；据载，她并非温柔，而是以专横的方式迫使男人们对她倾慕不已。爱德华陛下此前仅凭传闻爱慕她，且依循古老习俗，通过多次端详她的肖像，如今竟如痴如醉。他年近六旬，身材魁伟，金发碧眼，蓄着卷曲的胡须，眼神明亮而深邃，一如普瓦捷的曼努埃尔（Manuel of Poictesme）。梅泽莱饱学之士开始将这位坦率示爱的君主及其公主比作跪在翁法勒王后（Queen Omphale）脚下的赫拉克勒斯老爷（Sieur Hercules）。

宫廷在埃梅努埃尔（Ermenoueïl）的森林中猎杀了一头十叉雄鹿，城堡周围林木葱郁；狩猎结束后，两人在猎人里贡（Rigon）的小屋中共进晚餐，法兰西国王的幼妹梅勒格蕾特夫人（Dame Meregrett）作陪。她与订婚二人稍隔而坐，凝视着小屋唯一的窗户。如今我们知晓，她所思量的远不止树木。

===Original===

Dame Blanch seemed undisposed to mirth. “We have slain the stag, beau sire,” she said, “and have made of his death a brave diversion. Today we have had our sport of death﻿—and presently the gay years wind past us, as our cavalcade came toward the stag, and God’s incurious angel slays us, much as we slew the stag. And we shall not understand, and we will wonder, as the stag did, in helpless wonder. And Death will have his sport of us, as if in atonement.” Her big eyes shone, as when the sun glints upon a sand-bottomed pool. “Ohé, I have known such happiness of late, beau sire, that I am hideously afraid to die.”

The King answered, “I too have been very happy of late.”

“But it is profitless to talk about death thus drearily. Let us flout him, instead, with some gay song.” And thereupon she handed Sire Edward a lute.

The King accepted it. “Death is not reasonably mocked by any person,” Sire Edward said, “since in the end he conquers, and of the lips that gibed at him remains but a little dust. Rather should I, who already stand beneath a lifted sword, make for my destined and inescapable conqueror a Sirvente, which is the Song of Service.”

===Chinese===

布兰奇夫人似乎无心欢愉。“我们已杀死雄鹿，吾主（beau sire），”她说，“并以其死为一场壮丽的消遣。今日我们以死亡为乐——而欢乐的岁月将如我们的马队奔向雄鹿般从我们身边疾驰而过，上帝那漠不关心的天使将杀死我们，正如我们杀死雄鹿。我们将不解，我们将困惑，如雄鹿般无助地困惑。死亡将玩弄我们，仿佛赎罪。”她的大眼睛闪烁着光芒，如同阳光映照在沙底池塘上。“唉，我近来如此幸福，吾主，以至于恐惧死亡至极。”

国王答道：“我近来亦甚幸福。”

“然而如此忧郁地谈论死亡毫无益处。不如以欢快的歌谣嘲弄他。”说着，她将一把鲁特琴递给爱德华陛下。

国王接过琴。“死亡不容任何人戏弄，”爱德华陛下说，“因他终将获胜，而嘲弄他的嘴唇只剩一撮尘土。我——已立于悬剑之下——倒该为我那命定而无可逃避的征服者作一首《服务之歌》（Sirvente）。”

===Original===

Sang Sire Edward:3

“I sing of Death, that comes unto the king,

And lightly plucks him from the cushioned throne;

And drowns his glory and his warfaring

In unrecorded dim oblivion;

And girds another with the sword thereof;

And sets another in his stead to reign;

And ousts the remnant, nakedly to gain

Styx’ formless shore and nakedly complain

Midst twittering ghosts lamenting life and love.

“For Death is merciless: a crackbrained king

He raises in the place of Prester John,

Smites Priam, and mid-course in conquering

Bids Caesar pause; the wit of Salomon,

The wealth of Nero and the pride thereof,

And battle-prowess﻿—or of Tamburlaine

Darius, Jeshua, or Charlemaigne﻿—

Wheedle and bribe and surfeit Death in vain,

And get no grace of him nor any love.

“Incuriously he smites the armored king

And tricks his counsellors﻿—”

“True, O God!” murmured the tiny woman, who sat beside the window yonder. With that, Dame Meregrett rose, and passed from the room.

The two lovers started, and laughed, and afterward paid little heed to her outgoing. Sire Edward had put aside the lute and sat now regarding the Princess. His big left hand propped the bearded chin; his grave countenance was flushed, and his intent eyes shone under their shaggy brows, very steadily, although the left eye was now so nearly shut as to reveal the merest spark.

===Chinese===

爱德华陛下吟唱道：3

我歌咏死亡，他降临君王身畔，
轻轻将他拽离锦缎宝座；
淹没他的荣耀与征伐，
湮没于无录的幽暗遗忘之中；
为他人佩上那柄剑；
扶植他人登基为王；
驱逐残存者，使其赤裸裸地
抵达斯提克斯河（Styx）那无形的岸边，赤裸裸地
在啾啾鬼魂中哀叹生之爱恋与死亡。

“因死亡冷酷无情：他扶植一位痴愚的国王
取代祭司王约翰（Prester John），
击溃普里阿摩斯（Priam），在征服途中
命恺撒停步；所罗门（Salomon）的智慧，
尼禄（Nero）的财富及其骄奢，
以及战功——或帖木儿（Tamburlaine）的、
大流士（Darius）、耶书亚（Jeshua）或查理曼（Charlemaigne）的——
皆徒然哄骗、贿赂、餍足死亡，
却得不到他的恩宠或丝毫爱意。

“他漠然击溃披甲的国王，
戏弄他的谋臣——”

“确实如此，上帝啊！”坐在窗边的那位娇小女子喃喃道。梅勒格蕾特夫人随即起身，离室而去。

两位恋人惊了一跳，相视而笑，对她的离去未再多留意。爱德华陛下搁下鲁特琴，此刻凝望着公主。他巨大的左手托着蓄须的下巴；庄重的面容涨红，专注的眼神在浓眉下闪烁，异常坚定，尽管左眼已几乎闭合，仅余一线微光。

===Original===

Irresolutely, Dame Blanch plucked at her gown; then rearranged a fold of it, and with composure awaited the ensuing action, afraid at bottom, but not at all ill-pleased; and she looked downward.

The King said: “Never before were we two alone, madame. Fate is very gracious to me this morning.”

“Fate,” the lady considered, “has never denied much to the Hammer of the Scots.”

“She has denied me nothing,” he sadly said, “save the one thing that makes this business of living seem a rational proceeding. Fame and power and wealth fate has accorded me, no doubt, but never the common joys of life. And, look you, my Princess, I am of aging person now. During some thirty years I have ruled England according to my interpretation of God’s will as it was anciently made manifest by the holy Evangelists; and during that period I have ruled England not without odd by-ends of commendation: yet behold, today I forget the world-applauded, excellent King Edward, and remember only Edward Plantagenet﻿—hot-blooded and desirous man!﻿—of whom that much-commended king has made a prisoner all these years.”

“It is the duty of exalted persons,” Blanch unsteadily said, “to put aside such private inclinations as their breasts may harbor﻿—”

He said, “I have done what I might for the happiness of every Englishman within my realm saving only Edward Plantagenet; and now I think his turn to be at hand.” Then the man kept silence; and his hot appraisal daunted her.

===Chinese===

布兰奇夫人犹豫不决地拉扯着她的长袍；随后重新整理好一道褶皱，镇静地等待着接下来的举动，内心深处虽感恐惧，却并无不悦；她垂下了目光。

国王说道：“我们二人从未单独相处过，夫人。今晨命运对我格外垂青。”

“命运，”这位夫人思忖道，“从未亏待过‘苏格兰的锤子’（Hammer of the Scots）。”

“她未曾亏待我任何，”他忧伤地说，“唯独亏待了那使生存这桩事显得理所当然的唯一之物。命运无疑赐予我名声、权力与财富，却从未赐予我凡俗的生之欢愉。且看，我的公主，我已垂垂老矣。约三十载以来，我依据上帝的旨意——由神圣福音书早已昭示——统治英格兰；在此期间，我统治英格兰并非毫无建树，亦非全无赞誉：然而瞧啊，今日我忘却了那举世赞誉、卓越的爱德华国王，只记得爱德华·金雀花（Edward Plantagenet）——那个热血而渴望的男人！——这位备受推崇的国王已将他囚禁多年。”

“身居高位者的责任，”布兰奇不稳地说道，“便是摒弃胸中可能怀有的私欲——”

他说：“我已为我王国内每一位英格兰人的幸福竭尽所能，唯独遗忘了爱德华·金雀花；如今我想他的时机已到。”男人随即陷入沉默；他那炽热的审视令她畏惧。

===Original===

He said, “I have done what I might for the happiness of every Englishman within my realm saving only Edward Plantagenet; and now I think his turn to be at hand.” Then the man kept silence; and his hot appraisal daunted her.

“Lord,” she presently faltered, “lord, you know that we are already betrothed, and, in sober verity, Love cannot extend his laws between husband and wife, since the gifts of love are voluntary, and husband and wife are but the slaves of duty﻿—”

“Troubadourish nonsense!” Sire Edward said; “yet it is true that the gifts of love are voluntary. And therefore﻿—Ha, most beautiful, what have you and I to do with all this chaffering over Guienne?” The two stood very close to each other now. Blanch said, “It is a high matter﻿—” Then on a sudden the full-veined girl was aglow. “It is a trivial matter.” He took her in his arms, since already her cheeks flared in scarlet anticipation of the event.

Thus holding her, he wooed the girl tempestuously. Here, indeed, was Sieur Hercules enslaved, burned by a fiercer fire than that of Nessus, and the huge bulk of the unconquerable visibly shaken by his adoration. In a disordered tapestry of verbiage, aflap in winds of passion, she presently beheld herself prefigured by Balkis, the Judean’s lure, and by that Princess of Cyprus who reigned in Aristotle’s time, and by Nicolete, the King’s daughter of Carthage﻿—since the first flush of morning was as a rushlight before her resplendency, the man swore; and in conclusion, he likened her to a modern Countess of Tripolis, for love of whom he, like Rudel, had cleft the seas, and losing whom he must inevitably die as did Rudel. Sire Edward snapped his fingers now over any consideration of Guienne. He would conquer for her all Muscovy and all Cataia, too, if she desired mere acreage. Meanwhile he wanted her, and his hard and savage passion beat down opposition as if with a bludgeon.

===Chinese===

“大人，”她此刻怯懦地说，“大人，您知道我们已订婚，且，诚然，爱神无法将其律法延伸至夫妻之间，因爱之馈赠乃自愿，而夫妻仅为义务之奴——”

“游吟诗人的胡言乱语！”爱德华陛下说，“然爱之馈赠确属自愿。因此——哈，最美丽的人儿，你我何必为吉耶纳这般争执？”此刻两人已站得极近。布兰奇说：“这是大事——”旋即，血管贲张的少女双颊绯红。“这是小事。”他将她拥入怀中，因她的面颊已因期待此事而涨得通红。

他如此拥着她，以狂风骤雨般的方式追求少女。此情此景，确是赫拉克勒斯老爷沦为奴隶，被比涅乌斯（Nessus）之火更猛烈的火焰焚烧，那位不可征服者的庞大身躯因爱慕而明显颤抖。在一片纷乱的言语织锦中，在激情之风中翻飞，她旋即见自己被比作巴尔基斯（Balkis），犹太人的诱惑者，以及亚里士多德时代统治塞浦路斯的那位公主，还有妮可蕾特（Nicolete），迦太基的公主——因晨曦初露的光泽在她面前如同微弱的灯烛，男人如此起誓；最终，他将她比作特里波利斯（Tripolis）的现代伯爵夫人，为爱她，他如鲁代尔（Rudel）般劈波斩浪，失去她则必如鲁代尔般死去。爱德华陛下此刻对吉耶纳的任何考量嗤之以鼻。若她渴望土地，他愿为她征服整个莫斯科公国（Muscovy）乃至震旦（Cataia）。而此刻他想要她，他那狂野而凶猛的激情如棍棒般击溃了一切反对。

===Original===

“Heart’s emperor,” the trembling girl replied, “I think that you were cast in some larger mould than we of France. Oh, none of us may dare resist you! and I know that nothing matters, nothing in all the world, save that you love me. Then take me, since you will it﻿—and take me not as King, since you will otherwise, but as Edward Plantagenet. For listen! by good luck you have this afternoon despatched Rigon for Chevrieul, where tomorrow we were to hunt the great boar. So tonight this hut will be unoccupied.”

The man was silent. He had a gift that way when occasion served.

“Here, then, beau sire! here, then, at nine, you are to meet me with my chaplain. Behold, he marries us, as glibly as though we two were peasants. Poor king and princess!” cried Dame Blanch, and in a voice which thrilled him, “shall ye not, then, dare to be but man and woman?”

“Ha!” the King said. “So the chaplain makes a third! Well, the King is pleased to loose his prisoner, that long-imprisoned Edward Plantagenet: and I will do it.”

So he came that night, without any retinue, and habited as a forester, with a horn swung about his neck, into the unlighted hut of Rigon the forester, and he found a woman there, though not the woman whom he had expected.

“Treachery, beau sire! Horrible treachery!” she wailed.

“I have encountered it before this,” the big man said.

“Presently will come to you not Blanch but Philippe, with many men to back him. And presently they will slay you. You have been trapped, beau sire. Ah, for the love of God, go! Go, while there is yet time!” Sire Edward reflected. Undoubtedly, to light on Edward Longshanks alone in a forest would appear to King Philippe, if properly attended, a tempting chance to settle divers difficulties, once for all; and Sire Edward knew the conscience of his old opponent to be invulnerable. The act would violate the core of hospitality and knighthood, no doubt, but its outcome would be a very definite gain to France, and for the rest, merely a dead body in a ditch. Not a monarch in Christendom, Sire Edward reflected, but feared and in consequence hated the Hammer of the Scots, and in further consequence would not lift a finger to avenge him; and not a being in the universe would rejoice more heartily at the success of Philippe’s treachery than would Sire Edward’s son and immediate successor, the young Prince Edward of Caernarvon. Taking matters by and large, Philippe had all the powers of common sense to back him in contriving an assassination.

===Chinese===

“心之君主，”颤抖的少女答道，“我想您的气度远胜我等法兰西人。哦，无人敢抗拒您！且我知晓，世间万物皆无足轻重，唯您爱我一事至关重要。那么，请带走我，因您愿意——莫以国王之身带我，因您另有所图，而以爱德华·金雀花之身。听好！今日午后您遣里贡前往舍夫勒（Chevrieul），明日我们本欲猎杀那头巨熊。故今夜此屋将无人。”

男人沉默了。遇事时他常如此沉默。

“那么，吾主！九点时，请携我的牧师来此相会。瞧，他将为我们证婚，如同我们不过是农夫农妇般轻易。可怜的国王与公主！”布兰奇夫人呼道，声音令他战栗，“难道您不敢仅为男人与女人？”

“哈！”国王说。“如此牧师成了第三人！好，国王乐于释放他的囚徒，那被囚禁多年的爱德华·金雀花：我将照办。”

于是他当夜前来，未带任何随从，身着猎人装束，颈悬号角，步入猎人里贡那未点灯的小屋，却发现一个女人在内，虽非他所期待之人。

“背叛，吾主！可怕的背叛！”她哀号道。

“我此前亦遭遇过，”大块头男人说。

“稍后到来的非布兰奇，而是腓力，带众多部众助阵。他们旋即会杀死您。您已中计，吾主。啊，看在上帝的份上，快走！趁还有时间！”爱德华陛下思忖道。毫无疑问，在森林中撞见孤身一人的爱德华·长腿（Edward Longshanks），若腓力陛下布置得当，将是解决诸多难题的诱人良机；而爱德华陛下深知其老对手的良心坚不可摧。此举无疑违背了待客与骑士精神的核心，但其结果对法兰西将是确凿无疑的收益，至于其余，不过沟渠中一具尸骸。爱德华陛下思忖，基督教世界无一位君主不惧怕并因而憎恨“苏格兰的锤子”，进而无人愿伸出一根手指为他复仇；而宇宙中亦无一人比爱德华陛下的儿子、直系继承人——年轻的卡那封亲王（Prince Edward of Caernarvon）——更乐见腓力的阴谋得逞。总而言之，腓力在策划这场刺杀时，完全有常识的力量作后盾。

===Original===

What Sire Edward said was, “Dame Blanch, then, knew of this?” But Meregrett’s pitiful eyes had already answered him, and he laughed a little.

“In that event, I have tonight enregistered my name among the goodly company of Love’s Lunatics﻿—as yokefellow with Dan Merlin in his thornbush, and with wise Salomon when he capered upon the high places of Chemosh, and with Duke Ares sheepishly agrin in the net of Mulciber. Rogues all, madame! fools all! yet always the flesh trammels us, and allures the soul to such sensual delights as bar its passage toward the eternal life wherein alone lies the empire and the heritage of the soul. And why does this carnal prison so impede the soul? Because Satan once ranked among the sons of God, and the Eternal Father, as I take it, has not yet forgotten the antique relationship﻿—and hence it is permitted even in our late time that always the flesh rebel against the spirit, and that always these so tiny and so thin-voiced tricksters, these highly tinted miracles of iniquity, so gracious in demeanor and so starry-eyed﻿—”

Then he turned and pointed, no longer the orotund zealot but the expectant captain now. “Look, my Princess!” In the pathway from which he had recently emerged stood a man in full armor like a sentinel. “Mort de Dieu, we can but try to get out of this,” Sire Edward said.

“You should have tried without talking so much,” replied Meregrett. She followed him. And presently, in a big splash of moonlight, the armed man’s falchion glittered across their way. “Back,” he bade them, “for by the King’s orders, I can let no man pass.”

“It would be very easy now to strangle this herring,” Sire Edward reflected.

“But it is not easy to strangle a whole school of herring,” the fellow retorted. “Hoh, Messire d’Aquitaine, the bushes of Ermenoueïl are alive with my associates. The hut yonder, in effect, is girdled by them﻿—and we have our orders to let no man pass.”

“Have you any orders concerning women?” the King said.

===Chinese===

爱德华陛下说道：“那么，布兰奇夫人知晓此事？”但梅勒格蕾特那哀怜的眼神已替她回答，他微微一笑。

“若果真如此，我今夜已将己名登入‘爱神之疯人’的光荣名册——与荆棘丛中的梅林大师（Dan Merlin）为伍，与在基抹（Chemosh）高处狂舞的睿智所罗门为伍，与在伏尔甘（Mulciber）网中赧然狞笑的阿瑞斯公爵（Duke Ares）为伍。皆是恶棍，夫人！皆是傻瓜！然肉身总羁绊我们，引诱灵魂耽于感官之乐，阻碍其通往永恒生命——唯此中蕴藏着灵魂的帝国与遗产。这肉体的囚笼为何如此阻碍灵魂？因撒旦（Satan）曾位列上帝之子，且依我之见，永恒的圣父尚未忘却那古老的关联——因而即使在我们这迟暮之世，肉身仍被允许反叛精神，且这些如此微小、如此细声细语的骗子，这些色彩浓艳的邪僻奇迹，举止如此优雅、眼神如此星光闪烁——”

他旋即转身指向，不再是那浮夸的热忱者，而是期待中的指挥官。“看，我的公主！”他刚走过的路径上站着一个全身铠甲、如哨兵般的人。“天主（Mort de Dieu），我们只能试着逃离此地，”爱德华陛下说。

“您本该少说废话、早点尝试，”梅勒格蕾特答道。她跟随他。旋即，在一大片月光中，那武装者的弯刀（falchion）寒光一闪，拦住去路。“退后，”他命令道，“奉国王之命，我不得放任何人通行。”

“此刻勒死这条鲱鱼（herring）易如反掌，”爱德华陛下思忖道。

“但勒死一整群鲱鱼可不易，”那家伙反驳道。“吼，阿基坦老爷（Messire d’Aquitaine），埃梅努埃尔的灌木丛里满是我的同伴。那间小屋，实则已被他们包围——我们奉命不得放任何人通行。”

“你们有关于女人的命令吗？”国王说。

===Original===

The man deliberated. Sire Edward handed him three gold pieces. “There was assuredly no specific mention of petticoats,” the soldier now recollected, “and in consequence I dare to pass the Princess, against whom certainly nothing can be planned.”

“Why, in that event,” Sire Edward said, “we two had as well bid each other adieu.”

But Meregrett only said, “You bid me go?”

He waved his hand. “Since there is no choice. For that which you have done﻿—however tardily﻿—I thank you. Meantime I return to Rigon’s hut to rearrange my toga as King Caesar did when the assassins fell upon him, and to encounter with due decorum whatever Dame Luck may prefer.”

She said, “You go to your death.”

He shrugged his broad shoulders. “In the end we necessarily die.”

Dame Meregrett turned, and without faltering passed back into the hut.

When he had lighted the inefficient lamp which he found there, Sire Edward wheeled upon her in half-humorous vexation. “Presently come your brother and his tattling lords. To be discovered here with me at night, alone, means trouble for you. If Philippe chances to fall into one of his Capetian rages it means death.”

She answered, as though she were thinking about other matters, “Yes.”

Now, for the first time, Sire Edward regarded her with profound consideration. To the fingertips this so-little lady showed a descendant of the holy Lewis whom he had known and loved in old years. Small and thinnish she was, with soft and profuse hair that, for all its blackness, gleamed in the lamp-light with stray ripples of brilliancy, as you may see sparks shudder to extinction over burning charcoal. She had the Valois nose, long and delicate in form, and overhanging a short upper-lip; yet the lips were glorious in tint, and the whiteness of her skin would have matched the Hyperborean snows tidily enough. As for her eyes, the customary similes of the court poets were gigantic onyxes or ebony highly polished and wet with May dew. These eyes were too big for her little face: they made of her a tiny and desirous wraith which nervously endured each incident of life, like a foreigner uneasily acquiescent to the custom of the country.

===Chinese===

男人迟疑着。爱德华陛下递给他三枚金币。“军中确无明确提及裙钗之事，”那士兵此刻记起，“因此我敢放公主通行，她想必无事。”

“若果真如此，”爱德华陛下说，“我们二人不如互道珍重。”

但梅勒格蕾特只说：“您要我走？”

他挥了挥手。“因别无选择。为您所做之事——无论多迟——我感谢您。此刻我返回里贡的小屋，如恺撒王遭刺客袭击时那般整装，以应有的礼仪面对运气夫人（Dame Luck）的任何安排。”

她说：“您去赴死。”

他耸了耸宽阔的肩膀。“我们终将一死。”

梅勒格蕾特夫人转身，毫不犹豫地回到了小屋。

当他点亮屋内那盏昏暗的油灯后，爱德华陛下半是恼怒半是好笑地转向她。“你兄弟和他那些饶舌的领主即将到来。与我在此彻夜独处被发现，对你意味着麻烦。若腓力碰巧大发卡佩（Capet）王朝的雷霆之怒，那便意味着死亡。”

她答道，仿佛在想别的事：“是的。”

此刻，爱德华陛下第一次以深沉的审视望着她。从发梢到指尖，这位娇小的女子都透着圣路易（holy Lewis）后裔的气韵，那是他早年所识所爱之人。她身材娇小纤弱，乌发柔密，虽黑却在灯光下闪烁着零星的波光，如同你见到炭火上颤抖渐灭的火星。她有着瓦卢瓦（Valois）式的鼻子，修长精致，悬于短上唇之上；双唇色泽明艳，肌肤的白皙足以与极北（Hyperborean）之雪相映成趣。至于她的眼睛，宫廷诗人惯用的比喻是巨大的玛瑙石或抛光乌木，缀以五月的露珠。这对眼睛对她的小脸而言过大：使她成为一缕微小而渴望的幽魂，紧张地忍受着生活的每一桩事件，如同异乡人不安地顺应着当地的风俗。

===Original===

Sire Edward moved one step toward this tiny lady and paused. “Madame, I do not understand.”

Dame Meregrett looked up into his face unflinchingly. “It means that I love you, sire. I may speak without shame now, for presently you die. Die bravely, sire! Die in such fashion as may hearten me to live.”

The little Princess spoke the truth, for always since his coming to Mezelais she had viewed the great conqueror as through an awful haze of forerunning rumor, twin to that golden vapor which enswathes a god and transmutes whatever in corporeal man would have been a defect into some divine and hitherto unguessed-at excellence. I must tell you in this place, since no other occasion offers, that even until the end of her life it was so. For to her what in other persons would have seemed flagrant dullness showed somehow, in Sire Edward, as the majestic deliberation of one that knows his verdict to be decisive, and therefore appraises cautiously; and if sometimes his big, irregular calm eyes betrayed no apprehension of the jest at which her lips were laughing, and of which her brain approved, always within the instant her heart convinced her that a god is not lightly moved to mirth.

And now it was a god﻿—*O deus certè*!﻿—who had taken a woman’s paltry face between his hands, half roughly. “And the maid is a Capet!” Sire Edward mused.

“Blanch has never desired you any ill, beau sire. But she loves the Archduke of Austria. And once you were dead, she might marry him. One cannot blame her,” Meregrett considered, “since he wishes to marry her, and she, of course, wishes to make him happy.”

“And not herself, save in some secondary way!” the big King said. “In part I comprehend, madame. Now I too hanker after this same happiness, and my admiration for the cantankerous despoiler whom I praised this morning is somewhat abated. There was a Tenson once﻿—Lord, Lord, how long ago! I learn too late that truth may possibly have been upon the losing side﻿—” Thus talking incoherencies, he took up Rigon’s lute.

===Chinese===

爱德华陛下朝这位娇小的女子迈出一步，停下。“夫人，我不明白。”

梅勒格蕾特夫人毫不畏缩地仰视他的脸庞。“这意味着我爱您，陛下。此刻我可以无愧地诉说，因您即将死去。勇敢地死，陛下！以令我有勇气活下去的方式死。”

小公主说的是真话，因自他来到梅泽莱，她总是透过那可怕的、先于传闻的迷雾审视这位伟大的征服者，如同包裹神祇的金色蒸汽，将肉身凡人本可能显露的缺陷转化为某种神圣而迄今未被猜到的卓越。我必须在此告知您，因别无机会，即使直到她生命的终点，情形依然如此。对她而言，他人身上可能显得昭彰愚钝之处，在爱德华陛下身上，却以某种方式呈现为一种庄严的审慎——他深知自己的判决具有决定性，因而审慎评估；即便他那双硕大、不规则的平静眼眸有时未能显露对他唇间笑意、内心认可的玩笑的理解，她的心总在瞬间确信，神祇不会轻易被逗乐。

而此刻，是一位神祇——*O deus certè*！——用略显粗鲁的双手捧住一个女人卑微的脸庞。“而这少女是卡佩家的！”爱德华陛下沉吟道。

“布兰奇从未对您怀有恶意，吾主。但她爱慕奥地利大公（Archduke of Austria）。一旦您死去，她或可嫁给他。不能怪她，”梅勒格蕾特思忖道，“因他欲娶她，而她，自然，欲令他幸福。”

“而非她自身，至多是次要的！”大国王说。“我部分理解了，夫人。如今我也渴望同样的幸福，我今晨所赞美的那位好战的掠夺者在我心中的敬意已稍减。曾有一场辩论（Tenson）——天啊，天啊，多久以前！我为时过晚地领悟，真理或许曾在败方——”他如此语无伦次地说着，拿起了里贡的鲁特琴。

===Original===

Sang Sire Edward:

“Incuriously he smites the armored king

And tricks his counsellors﻿—

yes, the jingle ran thus. Now listen, madame﻿—listen, the while that I have my singing out, whatever any little cutthroats may be planning in corners.”

Sang Sire Edward:

“As, later on,

Death will, half-idly, still our pleasuring,

And change for fevered laughter in the sun

Sleep such as Merlin’s﻿—and excess thereof﻿—

Whence we, divorceless Death our Viviaine

Implacable, may never more regain

The unforgotten rapture, and the pain

And grief and ecstasy of life and love.

“For, presently, as quiet as the king

Sleeps now that planned the keeps of Ilion,

We, too, will sleep, whilst overhead the spring

Rules, and young lovers laugh﻿—as we have done﻿—

And kiss﻿—as we, that take no heed thereof,

But slumber very soundly, and disdain

The worldwide heralding of winter’s wane

And swift sweet ripple of the April rain

Running about the world to waken love.

“We shall have done with Love, and Death be king

And turn our nimble bodies carrion,

Our red lips dusty;﻿—yet our live lips cling

Despite that age-long severance and are one

Despite the grave and the vain grief thereof﻿—

Which we will baffle, if in Death’s domain

Fond memories may enter, and we twain

May dream a little, and rehearse again

In that unending sleep our present love.

“Speed forth to her in halting unison,

My rhymes: and say no hindrance may restrain

Love from his aim when Love is bent thereon;

And that were love at my disposal lain﻿—

All mine to take!﻿—and Death had said, ‘Refrain,

Lest I, even I, exact the cost thereof,’

I know that even as the weathervane

Follows the wind so would I follow Love.”

Sire Edward put aside the lute. “Thus ends the Song of Service,” he said, “which was made not by the King of England but by Edward Plantagenet﻿—hot-blooded and desirous man!﻿—in honor of the one woman who within more years than I care to think of has at all considered Edward Plantagenet.”

“I do not comprehend,” she said. And, indeed, she dared not.

But now he held both tiny hands in his. “At best, your poet is an egotist. I must die presently. Meantime I crave largesse, madame, and a great almsgiving, so that in his unending sleep your poet may rehearse our present love.” And even in Rigon’s dim light he found her kindling eyes not niggardly.

===Chinese===

爱德华陛下吟唱道：

他漠然击溃披甲的国王，
戏弄他的谋臣——

是的，歌词如此。现在听好，夫人——听好，容我唱完，任凭角落里的小恶棍们盘算什么。”

爱德华陛下吟唱道：

“因稍后，
死亡将漫不经心地止息我们的欢愉，
以发热的笑声换取
如同梅林般的沉睡——乃至过度的沉睡——
从此我们，与无情死亡——我们的薇维安（Viviaine）——永诀，
永难再寻回那难忘的狂喜，以及
生与爱之痛苦、悲伤与狂喜。

“因，不久，如同静卧的国王
此刻安睡，他曾规划伊利翁（Ilion）的堡垒，
我们亦将安睡，而头顶之上春天
主宰，年轻的恋人欢笑——如我们曾欢笑——
亲吻——如我们，而我们
不予理会，却酣然沉睡，
蔑视那昭示冬去的寰宇宣告，
以及四月细雨的迅疾甜润涟漪
流遍世界以唤醒爱。

“我们将与爱告别，死亡成为君王
将我们敏捷的躯体化作腐肉，
我们红唇化作尘土；——然而我们鲜活的唇却紧贴
尽管长久分离而合一
尽管坟墓及其徒然的悲伤——
我们将挫败它，若在死亡的领地
柔情记忆可进入，我俩
或可稍作梦寐，重续
在那无尽睡梦中我们当下的爱。

“去吧，我的诗行，以蹒跚的合音
向她疾驰：说无碍能阻
爱神达成其志，当爱神决意于此；
且若爱神为我所有——
尽归我取！——而死亡曾说，‘止步，
免得我，唯我，索取其代价，’
我知纵如风向标
追随风向，我亦将追随爱。”

爱德华陛下搁下鲁特琴。“《服务之歌》至此终了，”他说，“此歌非英格兰国王所作，而是爱德华·金雀花——那个热血而渴望的男人！——为那唯一一位在我不愿多想的岁月里曾考虑过爱德华·金雀花的女子所作。”

“我不明白，”她说。她确实不敢明白。

但他此刻将那双小手握在自己手中。“归根结底，您的诗人是个自我主义者。我即刻将死。此刻我恳求赏赐，夫人，恳求一份厚赠，以便您的诗人在无尽睡梦中能重续我们当下的爱。”即使在里贡那昏暗的灯光下，他也发现她那燃烧的双眸并不吝啬。

===Original===

Sire Edward strode to the window and raised big hands toward the spear-points of the aloof stars. “Master of us all!” he cried; “O Father of us all! the Hammer of the Scots am I! the Scourge of France, the conqueror of Llewellyn and of Leicester, and the flail of the accursed race that slew Thine only Son! the King of England am I, who have made of England an imperial nation, and have given to Thy Englishmen new laws! And tonight I crave my hire. Never, O my Father, have I had of any person aught save reverence or hatred! never in my life has any person loved me! And I am old, my Father﻿—I am old, and presently I die. As I have served Thee﻿—as Jacob wrestled with Thee at the ford of Jabbok﻿—at the place of Peniel﻿—” Against the tremulous blue and silver of the forest the Princess saw how horribly the big man was shaken. “My hire! my hire!” he hoarsely said. “Forty long years, my Father! And now I will not let Thee go except Thou hear me, and grant me life and this woman’s love.”

He turned, stark and black in the rearward splendor of the moon. “*As a prince hast thou power with God*,” he calmly said, “*and thou hast prevailed*. For the King of kings was never obdurate, my dear, to them that have deserved well of Him. So He will attend to my request, and will get us out of this pickle somehow.”

Even as he said this, Philippe the Handsome came into the room, and at the heels of the French King were seven lords, armed cap-à-pie.

The French King was an odd man. Subtly smiling, he came forward through the twilight, with soft, long strides, and he made no outcry at recognition of his sister. “Take the woman away, Victor,” he said, disinterestedly, to de Montespan. Afterward he sat down beside the table and remained silent for a while, intently regarding Sire Edward and the tiny woman who clung to Sire Edward’s arm; and in the flickering gloom of the hut Philippe smiled as an artist may smile who gazes on the perfected work and knows it to be adroit.

“You prefer to remain, my sister?” he said presently. “*Hé bien!* it happens that tonight I am in a mood for granting almost any favor. A little later and I will attend to your merits.” The fleet disorder of his visage had lapsed again into the meditative smile which was that of Lucifer watching a toasted soul. “And so it ends,” he said, “and England loses tonight the heir that Manuel the Redeemer provided. Conqueror of Scotland, Scourge of France! O unconquerable king! and will the worms of Ermenoueïl, then, pause tomorrow to consider through what a glorious turmoil their dinner came to them?”

“Do you design to murder me?” Sire Edward said.

===Chinese===

爱德华陛下大步走到窗前，将巨掌举向那疏离星辰的矛尖。“我们的主宰！”他呼喊；“哦，我们的天父！我是‘苏格兰的锤子’！法兰西的灾殃，卢埃林（Llewellyn）与莱斯特（Leicester）的征服者，那屠戮您独生子的被诅咒种族的鞭笞者！我是英格兰国王，将英格兰缔造为帝国之邦，并赋予您的英格兰人新法！今夜我恳求我的酬劳。哦，我的天父，我从未从任何人处得到过尊敬或仇恨之外的任何东西！我一生从未有人爱过我！而我已老迈，我的天父——我已老迈，即将死去。正如我侍奉过您——正如雅各（Jacob）在雅博渡口（Jabbok）与您角力——在毗努伊勒（Peniel）之地——”在森林那颤动的蓝银光辉中，公主看见那个大块头男人如何可怕地颤抖。“我的酬劳！我的酬劳！”他嘶哑地说。“四十载漫长岁月，我的天父！如今我不会放您走，除非您垂听我，赐予我生命与这女子的爱。”

他转身，在月光后退的辉煌中显得漆黑而僵直。“*你如王子般有权力与上帝摔跤*，”他平静地说，“*并且你已得胜*。因万王之王对他们——那些曾善待他的人——从不固执，亲爱的。因此他将垂听我的请求，设法将我们从这困境中解救出来。”

他正如此说时，美男子腓力（Philippe the Handsome）走进房间，法兰西国王身后跟随着七位全副武装的领主。

法兰西国王是个古怪的人。他微妙地微笑着，穿过暮色向前走来，步伐轻柔而悠长，认出妹妹时并未惊呼。“把这女人带走，维克多（Victor），”他漠不关心地对德·蒙特斯潘（de Montespan）说。随后他在桌边坐下，沉默了片刻，专注地凝视着爱德华陛下和那个紧抓着陛下手臂的娇小女子；在小屋摇曳的昏暗中，腓力微笑着，如同一个艺术家凝视着完成的作品，深知其精巧。

“你宁愿留下，我的妹妹？”他稍后说道。“*Hé bien!*（好吧！）今夜我心情颇佳，几乎任何恩惠都可给予。稍后我会处理你的功劳。”他面容上迅疾的紊乱已再次转为沉思的微笑，那是路西法（Lucifer）凝视一个被烘烤灵魂时的微笑。“如此终结了，”他说，“英格兰今夜失去了救世主曼努埃尔（Manuel the Redeemer）提供的继承人。苏格兰的征服者，法兰西的灾殃！哦，不可征服的国王！那么埃梅努埃尔的蛆虫明日会暂停下来，思量它们的晚餐是经由何等辉煌的骚乱才得来的吗？”

“你意在谋杀我？”爱德华陛下说。

===Original===

The French King shrugged. “I design that within this moment my lords shall slay you while I sit here and do not move a finger. Is it not good to be a king, my cousin, and to sit quite still, and to see your bitterest enemy hacked and slain﻿—and all the while to sit quite still, quite unruffled, as a king should always be? Eh, eh! I never lived until tonight!”

“Now, by Heaven,” said Sire Edward, “I am your kinsman and your guest, I am unarmed﻿—”

Philippe bowed his head. “Undoubtedly,” he assented, “the deed is foul. But I desire Gascony very earnestly, and so long as you live you will never permit me to retain Gascony. Hence it is quite necessary, you conceive, that I murder you. What!” he presently said, “will you not beg for mercy? I had hoped,” the French King added, somewhat wistfully, “that you might be afraid to die, O huge and righteous man! and would entreat me to spare you. To spurn the weeping conqueror of Llewellyn, say﻿ ﻿… But these sins which damn one’s soul are in actual performance very tedious affairs; and I begin to grow aweary of the game. *Hé bien!* now kill this man for me, messieurs.”

The English King strode forward. “Shallow trickster!” Sire Edward thundered. “*Am I not afraid?* You grimacing baby, do you think to ensnare a lion with such a flimsy rattrap? Wise persons do not hunt lions with these contraptions: for it is the nature of a rattrap, fair cousin, to ensnare not the beast which imperiously desires and takes in daylight, but the tinier and the filthier beast that covets meanly and attacks under the cover of darkness﻿—as do you and your seven skulkers!” The man was rather terrible; not a Frenchman within the hut but had drawn back a little.

“Listen!” Sire Edward said, and he came yet farther toward the King of France and shook at him one forefinger; “when you were in your cradle I was leading armies. When you were yet unbreeched I was lord of half Europe. For thirty years I have driven kings before me as did Fierabras. Am I, then, a person to be hoodwinked by the first big-bosomed hussy that elects to waggle her fat shoulders and to grant an assignation in a forest expressly designed for stabbings? You baby, is the Hammer of the Scots the man to trust for one half moment a Capet? Ill-mannered infant,” the King said, with bitter laughter, “it is now necessary that I summon my attendants and remove you to a nursery which I have prepared in England.” He set the horn to his lips and blew three blasts. There came many armed warriors into the hut, bearing ropes. Here was the entire retinue of the Earl of Aquitaine. Cursing, Sire Philippe sprang upon the English King, and with a dagger smote at the impassive big man’s heart. The blade broke against the mail armor under the tunic. “Have I not told you,” Sire Edward wearily said, “that one may never trust a Capet? Now, messieurs, bind these carrion and convey them whither I have directed you. Nay, but, Roger﻿—” He conversed apart with his son, the Earl of Pevensey, and what Sire Edward commanded was done. The French King and seven lords of France went from that hut trussed like chickens ready for the oven.

===Chinese===

法兰西国王耸了耸肩。“我意在即刻，诸位大人杀死你，而我坐在此处不动一指。堂兄，身为国王岂不美哉，端坐不动，眼见你最死敌被砍杀——同时始终端坐不动，泰然自若，如国王应有的模样？嗯，嗯！我今夜方真正活着！”

“如今，天主在上，”爱德华陛下说，“我是你的亲族兼客人，我手无寸铁——”

腓力低下头。“诚然，”他附和道，“此行卑劣。但我极其渴望加斯科涅，只要你活着，你便永不允许我保有加斯科涅。因此，你理解，我谋杀你是完全必要的。什么！”他此刻说道，“你竟不求饶？我原期望，”法兰西国王略带惆怅地补充道，“你会惧怕死亡，哦，高大而正义的人！并恳求我饶你一命。去唾弃那哭泣的卢埃林征服者，说吧……但这些令灵魂遭诅咒的罪孽，实际执行时实为乏味之事；我开始厌倦这场游戏了。*Hé bien!*（好吧！）诸位大人，为我杀了此人。”

英格兰国王大步上前。“浅薄的骗子！”爱德华陛下吼道。“*难道我不怕吗？*你这龇牙咧嘴的婴孩，竟想用如此简陋的捕鼠器（rattrap）诱捕雄狮？智者不用这些装置猎狮：因捕鼠器的本性，堂兄，是诱捕那在白昼贪婪攫取、耀武扬威的野兽，而非那在暗夜卑鄙觊觎、伺机偷袭的更小、更污秽的野兽——正如你与你那七个潜伏者！”此人相当可怕；小屋内无人不稍退一步。

“听着！”爱德华陛下说着，又向法兰西国王走近一步，冲他摇晃着一根食指；“你尚在摇篮时，我已统率大军。你尚穿开裆裤时，我已是半个欧洲的领主。三十年来，我如费拉布拉斯（Fierabras）般驱策诸王。那么，我难道是会因第一个选择摇晃肥臀、在专为刺杀设计的森林中应允幽会的荡妇而受骗之人吗？你这婴孩，‘苏格兰的锤子’难道会信任卡佩家的人半刻吗？无礼的婴孩，”国王苦笑道，“此刻我需召来随从，将你送往我在英格兰备好的育婴室。”他将号角置于唇边，吹了三声。众多武装战士涌入小屋，手执绳索。此乃阿基坦伯爵的全部随从。腓力陛下咒骂着扑向英格兰国王，以匕首刺向那面无表情的大汉的心脏。刀刃在束腰外衣下的锁子甲上折断。“我岂未告诉过你，”爱德华陛下疲倦地说，“永远不可信任卡佩家的人？诸位大人，将这些腐肉绑好，送往我指定之处。不，不过，罗杰（Roger）——”他与儿子佩文西伯爵私下交谈，爱德华陛下的命令得到执行。法兰西国王和七位法兰西领主如同待烤的鸡般被捆缚着走出小屋。

===Original===

And now Sire Edward turned toward Meregrett and chafed his big hands gleefully. “At every tree-bole a tethered horse awaits us; and a ship awaits our party at Fécamp. Tomorrow we sleep in England﻿—and, Mort de Dieu! do you not think, madame, that once within my very persuasive Tower of London, your brother and I may come to some agreement over Guienne?”

She had shrunk from him. “Then the trap was yours? It was you that lured my brother to this infamy!”

“In effect, I planned it many months ago at Ipswich yonder,” Sire Edward gayly said. “Faith of a gentleman! your brother has cheated me of Guienne, and was I to waste eternity in begging him to give me back my province? Oh, no, for I have many spies in France, and have for some two years known your brother and your sister to the bottom. Granted that I came hither incognito, to forecast your kinfolk’s immediate endeavors was none too difficult; and I wanted Guienne﻿—and, in consequence, the person of your brother. Hah, death of my life! does not the seasoned hunter adapt his snare to the qualities of his prey, and take the elephant through his curiosity, as the snake through his notorious treachery?” Now the King of England blustered.

But the little Princess wrung her hands. “I am this night most hideously shamed. Beau sire, I came hither to aid a brave man infamously trapped, and instead I find an alert spider, snug in his cunning web, and patiently waiting until the gnats of France fly near enough. Eh, the greater fool was I to waste my labor on the shrewd and evil thing which has no more need of me than I of it! And now let me go hence, sire, unmolested, for the sake of chivalry. Could I have come to the brave man I had dreamed of, I would have come cheerily through the murkiest lane of hell; as the more artful knave, as the more judicious trickster”﻿—and here she thrust him from her﻿—“I spit upon you. Now let me go hence.”

He took her in his brawny arms. “Fit mate for me,” he said. “Little vixen, had you done otherwise I would have devoted you to the devil.”

Still grasping her, and victoriously lifting Dame Meregrett, so that her feet swung clear of the floor, Sire Edward said, again with that queer touch of fanatic gravity: “My dear, you are perfectly right. I was tempted, I grant you. But it was never reasonable that gentlefolk should cheat at their dicing. Therefore I whispered Roger Bulmer my final decision; and he is now loosing all my captives in the courtyard of Mezelais, after birching the tails of every one of them as soundly as these infants’ pranks tonight have merited. So you perceive that I do not profit by my trick; and that I lose Guienne, after all, in order to come to you with hands﻿—well! not intolerably soiled.”

“Oh, now I love you!” she cried, a-thrill with disappointment to find him so unthriftily high-minded. “Yet you have done wrong, for Guienne is a king’s ransom.”

He smiled whimsically, and presently one arm swept beneath her knees, so that presently he held her as one dandles a baby; and presently his stiff and graying beard caressed her burning cheek. Masterfully he said: “Then let Guienne serve as such and ransom for a king his glad and common manhood. Now it appears expedient that I leave France without any unwholesome delay, because these children may resent being spanked. More lately﻿—hé, already I have in my pocket the Pope’s dispensation permitting me to marry, in spite of our cousinship, the sister of the King of France.”

Very shyly Dame Meregrett lifted her little mouth. She said nothing because talk was not necessary.

===Chinese===

此刻爱德华陛下转向梅勒格蕾特，欣喜地搓着他的大手。“每棵树干旁都有一匹拴好的马等着我们；费康（Fécamp）有船只等候我们一行。明日我们将在英格兰安眠——天主啊！夫人，难道您不认为，一旦进入我那极具说服力的伦敦塔（Tower of London），您兄弟与我或可在吉耶纳问题上达成某种协议？”

她曾畏缩过。“那么陷阱是您的？是您诱使我兄弟行此卑劣之事！”

“诚然，我数月前在那边的伊普斯维奇便策划了此事，”爱德华陛下快活地说。“以绅士的信誉担保！你兄弟骗取了我的吉耶纳，难道我要耗尽永恒乞求他归还我的省份？哦，不，我在法兰西有许多间谍，已深知你兄弟和你妹妹的底细长达两年。我微服来此，预判你亲族的近期图谋并非难事；我想要吉耶纳——因而，想要你兄弟本人。哈，天主啊！经验丰富的猎手难道不将陷阱设为适应猎物的特性，以好奇心诱捕大象，以臭名昭著的背叛诱捕毒蛇吗？”此刻英格兰国王咆哮起来。

但小公主绞着双手。“我今夜蒙受了奇耻大辱。吾主，我来此欲助一位英勇之人脱困于卑鄙的陷阱，却发现一只警觉的蜘蛛，安然蜷伏在其狡诈的罗网中，耐心等待法兰西的蠓虫（gnats）飞近。唉，我真是大愚特愚，竟将精力耗费在这精明邪恶、与我两不相需之物上！如今请放我走，陛下，莫加阻拦，以骑士之名。若我能来到我梦寐以求的勇士身边，我将欣然穿越地狱最阴暗的巷道；而作为更狡猾的恶棍，更精明的骗子”——她此刻将他推开——“我唾弃你。现在放我走。”

他用强健的臂膀拥抱她。“与我相配的佳偶，”他说。“小泼妇，若你别有举动，我定将你献给魔鬼。”

他仍紧拥着她，胜利般地将梅勒格蕾特夫人举起，使她双脚离地，爱德华陛下再次带着那种奇异的狂热庄重说：“亲爱的，你完全正确。我承认我曾受诱惑。但贵族在掷骰子时作弊从来都不合情理。因此我将最终决定低声告知罗杰·布尔默（Roger Bulmer）；此刻他正在梅泽莱庭院中释放我所有俘虏，并如这些婴孩今夜的胡闹所应得的，狠狠地鞭打他们的屁股。所以你明白我并未从我的诡计中获利；且我终究失去了吉耶纳，为的是能以双手——嗯！虽未污秽不堪——来到你身边。”

“哦，此刻我爱上你了！”她呼道，因发现他如此挥霍无度的高尚而激动不已。“但你已铸成大错，因吉耶纳是一位国王的赎金。”

他古怪地微笑着，随即一只手臂扫过她的膝弯，将她如婴孩般抱起；他那僵硬灰白的胡须抚触着她发烫的面颊。他威严地说：“那么让吉耶纳充当国王的赎金，赎回他欢愉而平凡的男子气概。此刻我似乎应毫无滞碍地离开法兰西，因这些孩子可能怨恨挨打。更甚者——嘿，我口袋里已有教皇的特许状，允许我——尽管有堂亲关系——娶法兰西国王的妹妹。”

梅勒格蕾特夫人羞怯地抬起小口。她无须言语。

===Original===

In consequence, after a deal of political tergiversation (Nicolas concludes), in the year of grace 1299, on the day of our Lady’s nativity, and in the twenty-seventh year of King Edward’s reign, came to the British realm, and landed at Dover, not Dame Blanch, as would have been in consonance with seasoned expectation, but Dame Meregrett, the other daughter of King Philippe the Bold; and upon the following day proceeded to Canterbury, whither on the next Thursday after came Edward, King of England, into the Church of the Trinity at Canterbury, and therein espoused the above-mentioned Dame Meregrett.

===Chinese===

结果，历经一番政治反复（尼古拉斯总结道），主恩一二九九年，圣母诞辰日（the day of our Lady’s nativity），国王爱德华在位第二十七年，抵达不列颠王国并在多佛（Dover）登陆的，并非布兰奇夫人——此乃世人久已期待之事——而是梅勒格蕾特夫人，勇者腓力（Philippe the Bold）的另一位女儿；次日她启程前往坎特伯雷（Canterbury），又过一个周四，英格兰国王爱德华来到坎特伯雷的三位一体教堂（Church of the Trinity），在教堂内迎娶了上述梅勒格蕾特夫人。

## IV / 抉择的故事

===Original===

"Sest fable es en aquest mon

Semblans al homes que i son;

Que el mager sen qu'om pot aver

So es amar Dieu et sa mer,

E gardar sos comendamens."

The fourth novel.﻿—Ysabeau of France, desirous of distraction, looks for recreation in the torment of a certain knight, whom she proves to be no more than human; but in the outcome of her holiday he confounds this queen by the wit of his reply.

In the year of grace 1327 (thus Nicolas begins) you could have found in all England no couple more ardent in affection or in despair more affluent than Rosamund Eastney and Sir Gregory Darrell. She was Lord Berners' only daughter, a brown beauty, of extensive repute, thanks to a retinue of lovers who were practitioners of the Gay Science, and who had scattered broadcast innumerable Canzons in her honor; and Lord Berners was a man to accept the world as he found it.

===Chinese===

"此世间之寓言

宛若其中芸芸众生；

人间至智莫过于

敬爱上帝及其圣母，

谨守其诫命。"

第四个故事——法兰西的伊莎博（Ysabeau），欲求消遣，遂以折磨某位骑士为乐，却发觉他不过一介凡人；然而在她这场假日的结局中，他以机智的回答令这位王后大为愕然。

主恩一三二七年（尼古拉斯如此开篇），举英格兰全境，你找不到比罗莎蒙德·伊斯特尼（Rosamund Eastney）与格雷戈里·达雷尔爵士（Sir Gregory Darrell）更炽热的情侣，或更富绝望之人。她是伯纳斯勋爵（Lord Berners）的独女，一位肤色微褐的美人，声名远播——多亏一批以风流之学（Gay Science）为业的爱慕者，他们广为散布了无数歌谣（Canzons）以颂其芳名；而伯纳斯勋爵则是个随遇而安之人。

===Original===

"Dompnedex!" the Earl was wont to say; "in sincerity I am fond of Gregory Darrell, and if he chooses to make love to my daughter that is none of my affair. The eyes and the brain preserve a proverbial warfare, which is the source of all amenity, for without lady-service there would be no songs and tourneys, no measure and no good breeding; and a man delinquent in domnei is no more to be valued than an ear of corn without the grain. No, I am so profoundly an admirer of Love that I can never willingly behold him slain, of a surfeit, by Matrimony; besides, this rapscallion Gregory could not to advantage exchange purses with Lazarus in the parable; and, moreover, Rosamund is to marry the Earl of Sarum a little after All Saints' day."

"Sarum!" people echoed. "Why, the old goat has had four wives already!"

And the Earl would spread his hands. "These redundancies are permissible to one of the wealthiest persons in England," he was used to submit.

===Chinese===

"天主啊！"伯爵惯常说；"我真心喜爱格雷戈里·达雷尔，若他执意追求我的女儿，那可不关我的事。眼睛与头脑素来不和——这倒是万般温雅的源头，因若无对淑女的效劳，便没有歌谣与比武，没有风度与教养；一个不懂得效忠淑女的人，便如同无实之穗，不值一提。不，我如此深挚地敬慕爱神，绝不愿眼见他因餍足而被婚姻扼杀；况且，这个无赖格雷戈里连同寓言中的拉撒路（Lazarus）互换钱袋都嫌寒碜；再者，罗莎蒙德将在万圣节后不久嫁给索尔兹伯里伯爵（Earl of Sarum）。"

"索尔兹伯里！"人们附和道。"唉，那老山羊已有四任妻子了！"

伯爵便摊开双手。"这些冗余对英格兰最富有的人之一而言是可以容忍的，"他惯常如此辩解。

===Original===

Thus it fell out that Sir Gregory came and went at his own discretion as concerned Lord Berners' fief of Ordish, all through those choppy times of warfare between Sire Edward and Queen Ysabeau. Lord Berners, for one, vexed himself not inordinately over the outcome, since he protested the King's armament to consist of fools and the Queen's of rascals; and had with entire serenity declined to back either Dick or the devil.

But at last the Queen got resistless aid from Count William of Hainault (in a way to be told about hereafter), and the King was captured by her forces, and was imprisoned in Berkeley Castle. There they held the second Edward to reign in England, who was the unworthy son of Dame Ellinor and of that first squinting King Edward about whom I have told you in the two tales preceding this tale. It was in the September of this year, a little before Michaelmas, that they brought Sir Gregory Darrell to be judged by the Queen; notoriously the knight had been her husband's adherent. "Death!" croaked Adam Orleton, who sat to the right hand, and, "Young de Spencer's death!" amended the Earl of March, with wild laughter; but Ysabeau leaned back in her great chair﻿—a handsome woman, stoutening now from gluttony and from too much wine﻿—and regarded her prisoner with lazy amiability.

===Chinese===

如是，在爱德华陛下（Sire Edward）与伊莎博王后之间战事不断的那些动荡岁月里，格雷戈里爵士在伯纳斯勋爵的封地奥迪什（Ordish）来去自如。伯纳斯本人对战局的结局并不十分挂怀，因他断言国王的军队尽是蠢货，王后的军队尽是恶棍；他泰然自若地拒绝支持任何一方——无论是迪克（Dick）还是魔鬼。

然而王后终于获得了海诺特伯爵威廉（Count William of Hainault）的不可抗拒之援（此事容后详叙），国王被她的军队俘获，囚禁于伯克利城堡（Berkeley Castle）。在那里，他们扶植了爱德华二世（the second Edward）统治英格兰——他是埃莉诺夫人（Dame Ellinor）之子，便是那位我在本故事之前的两个故事中向您讲述过的斜眼国王爱德华一世的不肖之子。就在这一年的九月，米迦勒节（Michaelmas）前夕，他们将格雷戈里·达雷尔爵士带来听候王后发落；众所周知，这位骑士一直是她丈夫的拥护者。"处死！"坐于右侧的亚当·奥尔顿（Adam Orleton）嘶声喊道，"处死小德·斯宾塞（young de Spencer）！"马奇伯爵（Earl of March）狂笑着补了一句；但伊莎博向后倚坐在她的大椅中——一个因饕餮和酗酒而日渐发福的美貌妇人——以慵懒的和蔼审视着她的囚犯。

===Original===

"And what was your errand in Figgis Wood?" she demanded﻿—"or are you mad, then, Gregory Darrell, that you dare ride past my gates alone?"

He curtly said, "I rode for Ordish."

Followed silence. "Roger," the Queen ordered, "give me the paper which I would not sign."

The Earl of March had drawn an audible breath. The Bishop of London somewhat wrinkled his shaggy brows, like a person in shrewd and epicurean amusement, while the Queen subscribed the parchment, with a great scrawling flourish.

"Take, in the devil's name, the hire of your dexterities," said Ysabeau. She pushed this document with her wet pen-point toward March. "So! get it over with, that necessary business with my husband at Berkeley. And do the rest of you withdraw, saving only my prisoner."

===Chinese===

"你在菲吉斯森林（Figgis Wood）做什么？"她质问道——"抑或你疯了不成，格雷戈里·达雷尔，竟敢独自骑马经过我的大门？"

他简短地答道："我骑马前往奥迪什。"

随之是一阵沉默。"罗杰，"王后命令道，"把那份我不愿签署的文书拿来。"

马奇伯爵倒吸了一口听得见的气息。伦敦主教（Bishop of London）微微皱起他浓密的眉毛，一副精明而好逸恶劳的愉快神情，而王后以潦草飞扬的笔触在羊皮纸上签下了名字。

"以魔鬼之名，拿去你们那些诡计的报酬吧，"伊莎博说。她用沾湿的笔尖将这份文件推向马奇。"好了！去办那桩与我丈夫在伯克利的必要事务吧。其余的人退下，只留下我的囚犯。"

===Original===

Followed another silence. Queen Ysabeau lolled in her carven chair, considering the comely gentleman who stood before her, fettered, at the point of shameful death. There was in the room a little dog which had come to the Queen, and now licked the palm of her left hand, and the soft lapping of its tongue was the only sound you heard. "So at peril of your life you rode for Ordish, then, messire?"

The tense man had flushed. "You have harried us of the King's party out of England﻿—and in reason I might not leave England without seeing the desire of my heart."

"My friend," said Ysabeau, as if half in sorrow, "I would have pardoned anything save that." She rose. Her face was dark and hot. "By God and all His saints! you shall indeed leave England tomorrow and the world also! but not without a final glimpse of this same Rosamund. Yet listen: I, too, must ride with you to Ordish﻿—as your sister, say﻿—Gregory, did I not hang, last April, the husband of your sister? Yes, Ralph de Belomys, a thin man with eager eyes, the Earl of Farrington he was. As his widow I will ride with you to Ordish, upon condition you disclose to none at Ordish, saving only, if you will, this quite immaculate Rosamund, any hint of our merry carnival. And tomorrow (you will swear according to the nicest obligations of honor) you must ride back with me to encounter﻿—that which I may devise. For I dare to trust your naked word in this, and, moreover, I shall take with me a sufficiency of retainers to leave you no choice."

===Chinese===

随之又是一阵沉默。伊莎博王后慵懒地斜倚在她雕刻华美的座椅中，审视着站在她面前那位英俊的绅士——身带镣铐，濒于可耻的死地。房中有一只小狗来到王后身旁，此刻正舔着她左手的掌心，它舌头轻柔的舔舐声是唯一能听到的声响。"那么你是冒着性命之危骑马前往奥迪什了，老爷（messire）？"

这位紧张的男人涨红了脸。"您将我们这些国王党人驱逐出了英格兰——我不离开英格兰而去见一见我心中的挚爱，于情于理都说得过去。"

"我的朋友，"伊莎博说道，仿佛带着几分忧伤，"我什么都可以宽恕，唯独此事不可。"她站起身来。她的面容阴沉而滚烫。"以天主及其所有圣徒之名！你明日确实要离开英格兰，也离开人世！但在那之前，你将最后一次见一见这位罗莎蒙德。且听我说：我也必须骑马与你同往奥迪什——权作你的妹妹，如何？——格雷戈里，去年四月我绞死了你妹妹的丈夫，不是吗？是的，拉尔夫·德·贝洛米斯（Ralph de Belomys），一个瘦削而眼神热切的人，他曾是法林顿伯爵（Earl of Farrington）。作为他的遗孀，我将骑马与你同往奥迪什，条件是你不得向奥迪什的任何人——只消你愿意的话，除了这位纯洁无瑕的罗莎蒙德——透露我们这场欢乐嘉年华的任何痕迹。而明日（你须以最严格的荣誉义务起誓），你必须骑马随我返回，面对——我可能设计的一切。因为在此事上我敢信赖你赤裸的诺言，而且，我还会带上足够多的随从，使你别无选择。"

===Original===

Darrell knelt before her. "I can do no homage to Queen Ysabeau; yet the prodigal hands of her who knows that I must die tomorrow and cunningly contrives, for old time's sake, to hearten me with a sight of Rosamund, I cannot but kiss." This much he did. "And I swear in all things to obey your will."

"O comely fool!" the Queen said, not ungently, "I contrive, it may be, but to demonstrate that many tyrants of antiquity were only bunglers. And, besides, I must have other thoughts than those which I have known too long: I must this night take holiday from thinking them, lest I go mad."

Thus did the Queen arrange her holiday.

===Chinese===

达雷尔跪在她面前。"我无法向伊莎博王后效忠；然而那慷慨的双手——明知我明日必死，却因旧日情谊巧妙安排让我见上罗莎蒙德一面——我不能不亲吻。"他如此做了。"我发誓一切服从您的意志。"

"哦，俊美的傻瓜！"王后说道，并非不温柔，"我或许在谋划，不过是想证明古代许多暴君不过是笨拙的庸才。而且，我必须有些不同于那些我已熟知太久的念头：今夜我须暂时放下那些念头度个假，免得我发疯。"

王后如是安排了她的假日。

===Original===

"Either I mean to torture you tomorrow," Dame Ysabeau said, presently, to Darrell, as these two rode side by side, "or else I mean to free you. In sober verity I do not know. I am in a holiday humor, and it is as the whim may take me. But do you indeed love this Rosamund Eastney? And of course she worships you?"

"It is my belief, madame, that when I see her I tremble visibly, and my weakness is such that a child has more intelligence than I﻿—and toward such misery any lady must in common reason be a little compassionate."

Her hands had twitched so that the astonished palfrey reared. "I design torture," the Queen said; "ah, I perfect exquisite torture, for you have proven recreant, you have forgotten the maid Ysabeau﻿—*Le Desir du Cuer*, was it not, my Gregory, that you were wont to call her, as nowadays this Rosamund is the desire of your heart. You lack inventiveness."

His palms clutched at heaven. "That Ysabeau is dead! and all true joy is destroyed, and the world lies under a blight from which God has averted an unfriendly face in displeasure! yet of all wretched persons existent I am he who endures the most grievous anguish, for daily I partake of life without any relish, and I would in truth deem him austerely kind who slew me now that the maiden Ysabeau is dead."

She shrugged wearily. "I scent the raw stuff of a Planh," the Queen observed; "*benedicite!* it was ever your way, my friend, to love a woman chiefly for the verses she inspired." And she began to sing, as they rode through Baverstock Thicket.

===Chinese===

"我明日要么想折磨你，"伊莎博夫人（Dame Ysabeau）此刻对并肩骑行的达雷尔说道，"要么想放了你。说实话我不知道。我正值假日心情，全凭兴致而定。但你是否真心爱这位罗莎蒙德·伊斯特尼？她自然也崇拜你？"

"夫人，我深信，当我见到她时我会明显地颤抖，我的软弱以至于孩童都比我明智——而面对如此可怜的境况，任何一位女士都理当稍有怜悯。"

她的手猛地一抖，惊得那匹温驯的乘马扬起了前蹄。"我设计的正是折磨，"王后说；"啊，我要施展精妙绝伦的折磨，因为你证明了自己是个变心之人，你已忘记了少女伊莎博——你不是惯常称她为*心之所欲*（*Le Desir du Cuer*）吗，我的格雷戈里，正如如今这位罗莎蒙德是你心中的渴望。你缺乏创造力。"

他的双手向天抓去。"那个伊莎博已死！一切真正的欢乐都已毁灭，这世界笼罩在一片枯萎之中，上帝已转过脸去，不愿目睹这不幸！然而在所有现存的悲苦之人中，我是承受最剧烈痛苦的那个，因我日日苟活而全无滋味，我真心认为那此刻将我杀死的人是严厉的善人，因为少女伊莎博已死了。"

她疲倦地耸了耸肩。"我嗅到一曲挽歌（Planh）的雏形了，"王后评道；"*Benedicite!*（天佑！）你一贯如此，我的朋友，你爱一个女人主要是因为她激发了你写诗的灵感。"她开始吟唱，两人骑马穿过巴弗斯托克灌木丛（Baverstock Thicket）。

===Original===

Sang Ysabeau:

"Man's love hath many prompters,

But a woman's love hath none;

And he may woo a nimble wit

Or hair that shames the sun,

Whilst she must pick of all one man

And ever brood thereon﻿—

And for no reason,

And not rightly﻿—

"Save that the plan was foreordained

(More old than Chalcedon,

Or any tower of Tarshish

Or of gleaming Babylon),

That she must love unwillingly

And love till life be done﻿—.

He for a season,

And more lightly."

So to Ordish in that twilight came the Countess of Farrington, with a retinue of twenty men-at-arms, and her brother Sir Gregory Darrell. Lord Berners received the party with boisterous hospitality.

===Chinese===

伊莎博吟唱道：

"男人的爱有许多驱策，
但女人的爱无所依凭；
他可追求伶俐的才智，
或令日光羞惭的秀发，
而她须从万千人中择定一人，
永远沉溺其中——
无缘由，
亦非出于本心——

"只因那计划乃天命所定
（比卡尔西顿（Chalcedon）更古老，
或任何塔尔西斯（Tarshish）的塔楼，
或闪耀的巴比伦（Babylon）的塔楼），
她必须不情愿地去爱，
且爱至生命终结——
他只消一季，
且爱得更轻。"

于是，在暮色中，法林顿伯爵夫人（Countess of Farrington）来到了奥迪什，随行二十名武士，以及她的兄弟格雷戈里·达雷尔爵士。伯纳斯勋爵以喧闹的殷勤接待了一行人。

===Original===

"Age has not blinded Father to the fact that your sister is a very handsome woman," was Rosamund Eastney's comment. The period appears to have been after supper, and the girl sat with Gregory Darrell in not the most brilliant corner of the main hall.

The wretched man leaned forward, bit his nether-lip, and then with a tumbling rush of speech told of the sorry masquerade. "The she-devil designs some horrible and obscure mischief, she plans I know not what."

"Yet I﻿—" said Rosamund. The girl had risen, and she continued with an odd inconsequence: "You have told me you were Pembroke's squire when long ago he sailed for France to fetch this woman into England﻿—"

"﻿—Which you never heard!" Lord Berners shouted at this point. "Jasper, a lute!" And then he halloaed, "Gregory, Madame de Farrington demands that racy song you made against Queen Ysabeau during your last visit." Thus did the Queen begin her holiday.

===Chinese===

"岁月并未蒙蔽父亲的双眼，看不出您的妹妹是位非常美丽的女子，"罗莎蒙德·伊斯特尼如此评论。时间似乎是在晚饭之后，少女与格雷戈里·达雷尔坐在大厅中最不显眼的角落里。

这可怜的男人倾身向前，咬着下唇，随后以纷乱而急速的言语道出了这场悲惨的伪装。"那女妖设计了某种可怕而隐秘的恶行，她图谋什么我不得而知。"

"然而我——"罗莎蒙德说。少女已站起身，她以一种奇特的不连贯继续说道："你曾告诉我，当年彭布罗克（Pembroke）远赴法兰西迎娶这个女人来英格兰时，你是他的侍从——"

"——这你从未听说过！"伯纳斯勋爵此时高喊道。"贾斯珀（Jasper），拿一把鲁特琴来！"接着他又喊道，"格雷戈里，法林顿夫人（Madame de Farrington）要求你演唱上次来访时写的那首讽刺伊莎博王后的尖刻歌谣。"王后的假日如是开始了。

===Original===

It was a handsome couple which came forward, with hand quitting hand tardily, and with blinking eyes yet rapt: these two were not overpleased at being disturbed, and the man was troubled, as in reason he well might be, by the task assigned him.

"Is it, indeed, your will, my sister," he said, "that I should sing﻿—this song?"

"It is my will," the Countess said.

And the knight flung back his comely head and laughed. "A truth, once spoken, may not be disowned in any company. It is not, look you, of my own choice that I sing, my sister. Yet if Queen Ysabeau herself were to bid me sing this song, I could not refuse, for, Christ aid me! the song is true."

Sang Sir Gregory:

"Dame Ysabeau, la prophécie

Que li sage dit ne ment mie,

Que la royne sut ceus grever

Qui tantost laquais sot aymer﻿—"4

and so on. It was a lengthy ditty, and in its wording not oversqueamish; the Queen's career in England was detailed without any stuttering, and you would have found the catalogue unhandsome. Yet Sir Gregory delivered it with an incisive gusto, desperately countersigning his own death warrant. Her treacheries, her adulteries and her assassinations were rendered in glowing terms whose vigor seemed, even now, to please their contriver. Yet the minstrel added a new peroration.

===Chinese===

走上前来的是一对璧人，手牵手迟迟不愿松开，眼睛虽眨动却仍痴迷：两人对被打扰并不十分高兴，而男人因被赋予的任务而烦恼——这烦恼合情合理。

"妹妹，这确是您的意思吗，"他说，"要我唱——这首歌？"

"正是我的意思，"伯爵夫人说。

骑士将英俊的头向后一仰，笑了起来。"一旦说出口的真理，无论在任何场合都无法否认。您瞧，这首歌并非我自愿所唱，我的妹妹。然而即便伊莎博王后本人命我唱此歌，我亦无法拒绝，因为基督助我！这首歌说的是真话。"

格雷戈里爵士吟唱道：

"伊莎博夫人，那预言
智者所言丝毫不虚，
王后懂得如何伤害那些
不久便沦为仆役的恋人——"4

余下如此。这是一首冗长的小曲，措辞并不十分文雅；王后在英格兰的行径被不加掩饰地详述，你若读来会觉得目录不堪入目。然而格雷戈里爵士以犀利的激情演唱，绝望地签署了他自己的死刑令。她的背叛、她的奸情与暗杀被以灼热的词句渲染，其力度即便此刻似乎仍令其策划者愉悦。然而这位吟游诗人添加了一段新的终曲。

===Original===

Sang Sir Gregory:

"Ma voix mocque, mon cuer gémit﻿—

Peu pense à ce que la voix dit,

Car me membre du temps jadis

Et d'ung garson, d'amour surpris,

Et d'une fille﻿—et la vois si﻿—

Et grandement suis esbahi."
