And when Darrell had ended, the Countess of Farrington, without speaking, swept her left hand toward her cheek and by pure chance caught between thumb and forefinger the autumn-numbed fly that had annoyed her. She drew the little dagger from her girdle and meditatively cut the buzzing thing in two. She cast the fragments from her, and resting the dagger's point upon the arm of her chair, one forefinger upon the summit of the hilt, considerately twirled the brilliant weapon.

"This song does not err upon the side of clemency," she said at last, "nor by ordinary does Queen Ysabeau."

"That she-wolf!" said Lord Berners, comfortably. "Hoo, Madame Gertrude! since the Prophet Moses wrung healing waters from a rock there has been no such miracle recorded."

===Chinese===

格雷戈里爵士吟唱道：

"我的声音嘲弄，我的心呻吟——
我的心很少顾及声音所言，
因我忆起往昔的时光，
忆起一个少年，被爱袭扰，
忆起一个少女——我望见她——
我深感惊异。"

达雷尔唱毕，法林顿伯爵夫人未发一言，左手拂向面颊，纯粹出于巧合，用拇指和食指夹住了那只秋日迟钝的烦人飞蝇。她从腰带上抽出小匕首，若有所思地将嗡嗡之物一斩为二。她将碎片弹开，将匕首尖搁在椅臂上，一根食指按在刀柄顶端，从容地转动着这柄寒光闪闪的武器。

"这首歌在宽厚一面可没犯什么错，"她终于说道，"而伊莎博王后平素也不会。"

"那只母狼！"伯纳斯勋爵舒坦地说道。"嗬，格特鲁德夫人（Madame Gertrude）！自从先知摩西（Moses）从岩石中拧出治水以来，就没有记载过这样的奇迹了。"

===Original===

"We read, Messire de Berners, that when the she-wolf once acknowledges a master she will follow him as faithfully as any dog. My brother, I do not question your sincerity, yet everybody knows you sing with the voice of an unhonored courtier. Suppose Queen Ysabeau had heard your song all through as I have heard it, and then had said﻿—for she is not as the run of women﻿—'Messire, I had thought until this that there was no thorough man in England save tall Roger Mortimer. I find him tawdry now, and﻿—I remember. Come you, then, and rule the England that you love as you may love no woman, and rule me, messire, since I find even in your cruelty﻿—For we are no pygmies, you and I! Yonder is squabbling Europe and all the ancient gold of Africa, ready for our taking! and past that lies Asia, too, and its painted houses hung with bells, and cloud-wrapt Tartary, where we two may yet erect our equal thrones, upon which to receive the tributary emperors! For we are no pygmies, you and I.' She paused. She shrugged. "Suppose Queen Ysabeau, who is not as the run of women, had said this much, my brother?"

Darrell was more pallid (as the phrase is) than a sheet, and the lute had dropped unheeded, and his hands were clenched.

"I would answer, my sister, that as she has found in England but one man, I have found in England but one woman﻿—the rose of all the world." His eyes were turned at this toward Rosamund Eastney. "And yet," the man stammered, "because I, too, remember﻿—"

"Hah, in God's name! I am answered," the Countess said. She rose, in dignity almost a queen. "We have ridden far today, and tomorrow we must travel a deal farther﻿—eh, my brother? I am going to bed, Messire de Berners."

So the men and women parted. Madame de Farrington kissed her brother at leaving him, as was natural; and under her caress his stalwart person shuddered, but not in repugnance; and the Queen went away singing hushedly.

===Chinese===

"我们读到，伯纳斯老爷（Messire de Berners），母狼一旦认定了主人，便如任何忠犬般追随。我的兄弟，我不质疑你的真诚，但人人都知道你唱歌的声音像个不得宠的朝臣。假若伊莎博王后如我这般听完了你的歌，然后说道——她与寻常女子不同——'老爷，我此前一直以为英格兰除了高个子罗杰·莫蒂默（Roger Mortimer）再无真正的男子汉。如今我觉得他俗不可耐，而且——我想起来了。那么来吧，统治你所爱的英格兰——你对英格兰的爱无人可比——也统治我，老爷，因为我甚至在你的残酷中也发现了——因为我们不是侏儒，你和我！那里是争吵不休的欧洲和非洲的全部古老黄金，等着我们去取！越过那里还有亚洲，它那挂满铃铛的彩绘屋宇，以及云雾缠绕的鞑靼（Tartary），在那里我们二人尚可建立平等的王座，接受纳贡的帝王！因为我们不是侏儒，你和我。'她停顿了。她耸了耸肩。"假若伊莎博王后——她与寻常女子不同——说了这些话，我的兄弟？"

达雷尔面色比一张纸还要苍白（俗语如此），鲁特琴不知不觉间滑落，双手紧握。

"我会回答她，我的妹妹：既然她只在英格兰找到了一个男人，我便只在英格兰找到了一个女人——世间所有玫瑰中的那一朵。"他的目光此刻转向罗莎蒙德·伊斯特尼。"然而，"这男人结巴着说，"因为我同样记得——"

"哈，以上帝之名！我得到了回答，"伯爵夫人说。她站起身来，仪态之尊贵宛若王后。"我们今日骑了很远的路，明日还要走更远的路——嗯，我的兄弟？我要去睡了，伯纳斯老爷。"

男男女女就此散去。法林顿夫人（Madame de Farrington）在离去时亲吻了她的兄弟，这是自然不过的事；在他的拥抱下，他那魁伟的身躯颤抖了，但并非出于厌恶；王后低声吟唱着离去了。

===Original===

Sang Ysabeau:

"Were the All-Mother wise, life (shaped anotherwise)

Would be all high and true;

Could I be otherwise I had been otherwise

Simply because of you,﻿…

With whom I have naught to do,

And who are no longer you!

"Life with its pay to be bade us essay to be

What we became﻿—I believe

Were there a way to be what it was play to be

I would not greatly grieve﻿…

Hearts are not worn on the sleeve.

Let us neither laugh nor grieve!"

Ysabeau would have slept that night within the chamber of Rosamund Eastney had either slept. As concerns the older I say nothing. The girl, though soon aware of frequent rustlings near at hand, lay quiet, half-forgetful of the poisonous woman yonder. The girl was now fulfilled with a great blaze of exultation: tomorrow Gregory must die, and then perhaps she might find time for tears; meanwhile, before her eyes, the man had flung away a kingdom and life itself for love of her, and the least nook of her heart ached to be a shade more worthy of the sacrifice.

===Chinese===

伊莎博吟唱道：

"若万物之母睿智，生命（若塑造得不同）
本该崇高而真实；
若我能成为另一个人，我本该成为另一个人，
只因有你……
与你我毫无瓜葛，
而你也不再是你！

"生命以它应得的代价命我们尝试成为
我们所变成的人——我相信
若有法成为本该轻松成为的人
我不会太悲伤……
心不外露于衣袖。
让我们既不欢笑也不悲伤！"

那一夜，伊莎博本欲睡在罗莎蒙德·伊斯特尼的寝室中，假如两人中有谁入睡了的话。关于年长的那位我无话可说。少女虽很快察觉到身旁频繁的窸窣声响，却安静地躺着，几乎忘记了远处那个有毒的女人。少女此刻心中充盈着巨大的欢腾：明日格雷戈里必死，然后她或许能找到哭泣的时间；而与此同时，在她眼前，这个男人为爱她而抛弃了一个王国和他自己的生命，她心中最隐秘的角落都因渴望配得上这份牺牲而疼痛。

===Original===

After it might have been an hour of this excruciate ecstasy the Countess came to Rosamund's bed. "Ay," the woman began, "it is indisputable that his hair is like spun gold and that his eyes resemble sun-drenched waters in June. It is certain that when this Gregory laughs God is more happy. Girl, I was familiar with the routine of your meditations before you were born."

Rosamund said, quite simply: "You have known him always. I envy the circumstance, Madame Gertrude﻿—you alone of all women in the world I envy, since you, his sister, being so much older, must have known him always."

"I know him to the core, my girl," the Countess answered. For a while she sat silent, one bare foot jogging restlessly. "Yet I am two years his junior﻿—Did you hear nothing, Rosamund?"

"No, Madame Gertrude, I heard nothing."

"Strange!" the Countess said; "let us have lights, since I can no longer endure this overpopulous twilight." She kindled, with twitching fingers, three lamps. "It is as yet dark yonder, where the shadows quiver very oddly, as though they would rise from the floor﻿—do they not, my girl?﻿—and protest vain things. But, Rosamund, it has been done; in the moment of death men's souls have travelled farther and have been visible; it has been done, I tell you. And he would stand before me, with pleading eyes, and would reproach me in a voice too faint to reach my ears﻿—but I would see him﻿—and his groping hands would clutch at my hands as though a dropped veil had touched me, and with the contact I would go mad!"

===Chinese===

在这酷烈的狂喜中过了约莫一个时辰，伯爵夫人来到罗莎蒙德的床前。"唉，"她开口道，"无可争辩的是，他的头发如纺金丝，他的眼睛像六月里阳光浸透的水潭。确然无疑，当这位格雷戈里欢笑时，上帝也更快乐。姑娘，你的心思我早在你出生之前就已熟知。"

罗莎蒙德十分坦率地说："您一直认识他。我羡慕这种境况，格特鲁德夫人——全世界的女人中我只羡慕您，因为您是他的妹妹，年长许多，必然一直认识他。"

"我了解他至骨髓，我的姑娘，"伯爵夫人答道。她沉默了一会儿，一只光脚不安地晃动着。"然而我比他小两岁——你什么都没听到吗，罗莎蒙德？"

"没有，格特鲁德夫人，我什么都没听到。"

"奇怪！"伯爵夫人说；"我们点灯吧，因为我再也忍受不了这人影绰绰的暮色了。"她用颤抖的手指点燃了三盏灯。"那边仍然黑暗，那里的影子诡异地颤动，仿佛要从地板上升起——难道不是吗，我的姑娘？——并且徒然地抗议。但是，罗莎蒙德，这事已经发生过；在死亡的那一刻，人们的灵魂曾走得很远，且曾被看见；我告诉你，这是真的。他会站在我面前，以恳求的目光，以微弱得无法传入我耳的声音责备我——但我会看见他——他摸索着的双手会抓住我的双手，仿佛一方坠落的面纱触碰了我，而一旦触碰，我就会发疯！"

===Original===

"Madame Gertrude!" the girl stammered, in communicated terror.

"Poor innocent fool!" the woman said, "I am Ysabeau of France." And when Rosamund made as though to rise, in alarm, Queen Ysabeau caught her by the shoulder. "Bear witness when he comes that I never hated him. Yet for my quiet it was necessary that it suffer so cruelly, the scented, pampered body, and no mark be left upon it! Eia! even now he suffers! No, I have lied. I hate the man, and in such fashion as you will comprehend when you are Sarum's wife."

"Madame and Queen!" the girl said, "you will not murder me!"

"I am tempted!" the Queen answered. "O little slip of girlhood, I am tempted, for it is not reasonable you should possess everything that I have lost. Innocence you have, and youth, and untroubled eyes, and quiet dreams, and the fond graveness of a child, and Gregory Darrell's love﻿—" Now Ysabeau sat down upon the bed and caught up the girl's face between two fevered hands. "Rosamund, this Darrell perceives within the moment, as I do, that the love he bears for you is but what he remembers of the love he bore a certain maid long dead. Eh, you might have been her sister, Rosamund, for you are very like her. And she, poor wench﻿—why, I could see her now, I think, were my eyes not blurred, somehow, almost as though Queen Ysabeau might weep! But she was handsomer than you, since your complexion is not overclear, praise God!"

===Chinese===

"格特鲁德夫人！"少女结巴着，被传递的恐惧所攫住。

"可怜而无辜的傻瓜！"那女人说，"我是法兰西的伊莎博。"当罗莎蒙德惊恐地欲起身时，伊莎博王后抓住了她的肩膀。"等他来时，你可作证我从未恨过他。然而为了我的安宁，那芬芳的、被娇惯的肉身必须遭受如此残酷的折磨，且不留下任何痕迹！Eia！即便此刻他仍在受苦！不，我说了谎。我恨那个男人，恨的方式你成为索尔兹伯里的妻子时自会明白。"

"夫人！王后！"少女说，"您不会杀了我吧！"

"我正受着诱惑！"王后答道。"哦，你这娇嫩的小丫头，我正受着诱惑，因为你拥有一切我已失去的东西，这不合理。你有纯真，有青春，有无忧的眼睛，有安宁的梦境，有孩童般的稚拙端庄，还有格雷戈里·达雷尔的爱——"伊莎博此刻坐在床上，用一双滚烫的手捧起少女的脸。"罗莎蒙德，这位达雷尔此刻与我一样清楚地意识到，他对你的爱不过是他对一位早已死去的少女所怀旧爱的记忆。唉，你本可以做她的妹妹，罗莎蒙德，因为你与她非常相像。而她，可怜的姑娘——唉，我此刻还能看见她，我想，若我的眼睛没有莫名地模糊——仿佛伊莎博王后可能哭泣！但她比你更美，因为你的肤色并不十分白皙，感谢上帝！"

===Original===

Woman against woman they were. "He has told me of his intercourse with you," the girl said, and this was a lie flatfooted. "Nay, kill me if you will, madame, since you are the stronger, yet, with my dying breath, I protest that Gregory has loved no woman truly in all his life except me."

The Queen laughed bitterly. "Do I not know men? He told you nothing. And tonight he hesitated, and tomorrow, at the lifting of my finger, he will supplicate. Since boyhood Gregory Darrell has loved me, O white, palsied innocence! and he is mine at a whistle. And in that time to come he will desert you, Rosamund﻿—bidding farewell with a pleasing Canzon﻿—and they will give you to the gross Earl of Sarum, as they gave me to the painted man who was of late our King! and in that time to come you will know your body to be your husband's makeshift when he lacks leisure to seek out other recreation! and in that time to come you will long for death, and presently your heart will be a flame within you, my Rosamund, an insatiable flame! and you will hate your God because He made you, and hate Satan because in some desperate hour he tricked you, and hate all men because, poor fools, they scurry to obey your whims! and chiefly you will hate yourself because you are so pitiable! and devastation only will you love in that strange time which is to come. It is adjacent, my Rosamund."

===Chinese===

这是女人与女人之间的对峙。"他已告诉过我他与你的交往，"少女说，这是一句赤裸裸的谎言。"不，您若要杀我便杀吧，夫人，因为您更强势，然而以我临终的气息，我抗议格雷戈里一生中从未真心爱过任何女人，除了我。"

王后苦笑着。"我岂不知男人？他什么也没告诉你。而今晚他犹豫了，明日只需我抬起手指，他便会乞求。自少年时代起格雷戈里·达雷尔便爱着我，哦，你这苍白而战栗的纯真！他一声口哨便属于我。而将来，他会抛弃你，罗莎蒙德——以一首动听的歌谣（Canzon）告别——他们会把你交给粗鄙的索尔兹伯里伯爵，正如他们把我交给那个涂脂抹粉的如今成了我们国王的男人！而将来你会知道，当你的丈夫无暇寻找其他消遣时，你的肉体不过是他的权宜之计！而将来你会渴望死亡，不久你的心中将燃起一团火焰，我的罗莎蒙德，一团欲壑难填的火焰！你将恨你的上帝因为祂创造了你，恨撒旦因为在某个绝望的时刻他欺骗了你，恨所有男人因为——可怜的傻瓜们——他们争相服从你的心血来潮！而你尤其会恨自己因为你如此可悲！而在那个即将来临的奇异时光中，你唯一会爱的便是毁灭。它近在咫尺，我的罗莎蒙德。"

===Original===

The girl kept silence. She sat erect in the tumbled bed, her hands clasping her knees, and she appeared to deliberate what Dame Ysabeau had said. Plentiful brown hair fell about this Rosamund's face, which was white and shrewd. "A part of what you say, madame, I understand. I know that Gregory Darrell loves me, yet I have long ago acknowledged he loves me as one pets a child, or, let us say, a spaniel which reveres and amuses one. I lack his wit, you comprehend, and so he never speaks to me all that he thinks. Yet a part of it he tells me, and he loves me, and with this I am content. Assuredly, if they give me to Sarum I shall hate Sarum even more than I detest him now. And then, I think, Heaven help me! that I would not greatly grieve﻿—Oh, you are all evil!" Rosamund said; "and you thrust into my mind thoughts which I may not understand!"

"You will comprehend them," the Queen said, "when you know yourself a chattel, bought and paid for."

The Queen laughed. She rose, and her hands strained toward heaven. "You are omnipotent, yet have You let me become that into which I am transmuted," she said, very low.

She began to speak as though a statue spoke through lips that seemed motionless. "Men have long urged me, Rosamund, to a deed which by one stroke would make me mistress of these islands. Today I looked on Gregory Darrell, and knew that I was wise in love﻿—and I had but to crush a lewd soft worm to come to him. Eh, and I was tempted﻿—!"

===Chinese===

少女沉默着。她在凌乱的床上端坐，双手抱膝，似乎在思索伊莎博夫人所说的话。浓密的褐发垂落在罗莎蒙德的脸上，那张脸苍白而精明。"夫人，您的话我部分理解。我知道格雷戈里·达雷尔爱着我，但很早以前我便承认他爱我如同溺爱一个孩童，或者，我们这样说吧，如同溺爱一条既崇敬又逗人欢喜的西班牙猎犬。我不够聪明，您明白，所以他从不对我倾吐他的全部思想。但他会告诉我一部分，而且他爱我，对此我已心满意足。诚然，若他们把我嫁给索尔兹伯里，我将比现在更憎恨索尔兹伯里。而那时，我想，上帝助我！我不会太悲伤——哦，你们都是邪恶的！"罗莎蒙德说；"你们把我无法理解的念头强塞进我的脑海！"

"你会理解它们的，"王后说，"当你意识到自己不过是一件买来的、付过钱的物品时。"

王后笑了。她站起身来，双手向天空伸去。"您是全能的，但您却让我变成了现在的模样，"她极低声地说。

她开始说话，仿佛一尊雕像通过看似纹丝不动的嘴唇在说话。"男人们长久以来敦促我，罗莎蒙德，去做一件一举使我成为这些岛屿之主的事。今日我端详了格雷戈里·达雷尔，深知我在爱情中是明智的——我只需碾碎一条淫邪柔软的虫子便能得到他。唉，而我曾受诱惑——！"

===Original===

The girl said: "Let us grant that Gregory loves you very greatly, and me just when his leisure serves. You may offer him a cushioned infamy, a colorful and brief delirium, and afterward demolishment of soul and body; I offer him contentment and a level life, made up of small events, it may be, and lacking both in abysses and in skyey heights. Yet is love a flame wherein the lover's soul must be purified; it is a flame which assays high queens just as it does their servants: and thus, madame, to judge between us I dare summon you."

"Child, child!" the Queen said, tenderly, and with a smile, "you are brave; and in your fashion you are wise; yet you will never comprehend. But once I was in heart and soul and body all that you are today; and now I am Queen Ysabeau﻿—Did you in truth hear nothing, Rosamund?"

"Why, nothing save the wind."

"Strange!" said the Queen; "since all the while that I have talked with you I have been seriously annoyed by shrieks and imprecations! But I, too, grow cowardly, it may be﻿—Nay, I know," she said, and in a resonant voice, "that by this I am mistress of broad England, until my son﻿—my own son, born of my body, and in glad anguish, Rosamund﻿—knows me for what I am. For I have heard﻿—Coward! O beautiful sleek coward!" the Queen said; "I would have died without lamentation and I was but your plaything!"

===Chinese===

少女说："让我们假设格雷戈里非常爱你，而对我只在他闲暇时才顾及。你可以给他一桩锦垫上的耻辱，一场绚烂而短暂的迷醉，以及随之而来的灵魂与肉体的毁灭；我给他满足和平稳的生活，由琐碎事件构成，或许，既无深渊亦无高天。然而爱是一团火焰，恋人的灵魂须在其中净化；它考验高贵的王后正如考验她们的仆人：因此，夫人，为裁决你我之间的事，我敢召唤你。"

"孩子，孩子！"王后温柔地说，带着微笑，"你很勇敢；以你的方式你很明智；但你永远无法理解。然而曾经，我在心中、灵魂上和肉体上就是你现在的一切；而如今我是伊莎博王后——你真的什么也没听到吗，罗莎蒙德？"

"没有，只有风声。"

"奇怪！"王后说；"因我与你交谈的整个过程中，一直被尖叫声和诅咒声严重困扰！但我也可能变得胆怯了——不，我知道，"她以洪亮的声音说，"凭借此我已是辽阔英格兰的主人，直到我的儿子——我亲生的儿子，从我体内诞生，伴随着甜蜜的痛苦，罗莎蒙德——认清我的真面目。因为我听到——懦夫！哦，美丽的、圆滑的懦夫！"王后说；"我本会毫无怨言地死去，而我只是你的玩物！"

===Original===

"Madame Ysabeau﻿—!" the girl answered vaguely, for she was puzzled and was almost frightened by the other's strange talk.

"To bed!" said Ysabeau; "and put out the lights lest he come presently. Or perhaps he fears me now too much to come tonight. Yet the night approaches, none the less, when I must lift some arras and find him there, chalk-white, with painted cheeks, and rigid, and smiling very terribly, or look into some mirror and behold there not myself but him﻿—and in that instant I shall die. Meantime I rule, until my son attains his manhood. Eh, Rosamund, my only son was once so tiny, and so helpless, and his little crimson mouth groped toward me, helplessly, and save in Bethlehem, I thought, there was never any child more fair﻿—But I must forget all that, for even now he plots. Hey, God orders matters very shrewdly, my Rosamund."

Timidly the girl touched Ysabeau's shoulder. "In part, I understand, madame and Queen."

"You understand nothing," said Ysabeau; "how should you understand whose breasts are yet so tiny? So let us put out the light! though I dread darkness, Rosamund﻿—For they say that hell is poorly lit﻿—and they say﻿—" Then Queen Ysabeau shrugged. Pensively she blew out each lamp.

===Chinese===

"伊莎博夫人——！"少女含糊地应道，因对方怪异的言辞令她困惑且几近恐惧。

"去睡吧！"伊莎博说；"把灯熄了，免得他此刻过来。抑或他如今太怕我，今夜不会来了。然而那夜晚终将降临，届时我须掀开某幅挂毯，在那里找到他——面色如粉笔般苍白，双颊涂着脂粉，僵硬地站着，笑得极为可怖；或照某面镜子，在镜中看见的不是自己而是他——而在那一瞬间我将死去。此刻我统治着一切，直到我的儿子长大成人。唉，罗莎蒙德，我唯一的儿子曾经那么幼小，那么无助，他那小小的绯红嘴唇向我摸索着，无助地，我以为除了伯利恒（Bethlehem），从未有过更美的孩童——但我必须忘记这一切，因为即便此刻他已在谋划。嘿，上帝安排事务何等精明，我的罗莎蒙德。"

少女怯怯地碰了碰伊莎博的肩膀。"我部分理解了，夫人，王后。"

"你什么也不理解，"伊莎博说；"你的胸脯还那么小，如何能理解？让我们把灯熄了吧！尽管我惧怕黑暗，罗莎蒙德——因为他们说地狱的照明很差——他们说——"伊莎博王后耸了耸肩。她若有所思地吹灭了每一盏灯。

===Original===

"We know this Gregory Darrell," the Queen said in the darkness, "ah, to the marrow we know him, however steadfastly we blink, and we know the present turmoil of his soul; and in common sense what chance have you of victory?"

"None in common sense, madame, and yet you go too fast. For man is a being of mingled nature, we are told by those in holy orders, and his life here is one unending warfare between that which is divine in him and that which is bestial, while impartial Heaven attends as arbiter of the tourney. Always a man's judgment misleads him and his faculties allure him to a truce, however brief, with iniquity. His senses raise a mist about his goings, and there is not an endowment of the man but in the end plays traitor to his interest, as of God's wisdom God intends; so that when the man is overthrown, the Eternal Father may, in reason, be neither vexed nor grieved if only the man takes heart to rise again. And when, betrayed and impotent, the man elects to fight out the allotted battle, defiant of common sense and of the counsellors which God Himself accorded, I think that the Saints hold festival in heaven."

"A very pretty sermon," said the Queen. "Yet I do not think that our Gregory could very long endure a wife given over to such high-minded talking. He prefers to hear himself do the fine talking."

Followed a silence, vexed only on the purposeless September winds; but I believe that neither of these two slept with profundity.

===Chinese===

"我们了解这个格雷戈里·达雷尔，"王后在黑暗中说，"啊，我们了解他至骨髓，无论我们如何坚定地回避，我们了解他此刻灵魂的动荡；而以常理而论，你有何胜算？"

"以常理而言毫无胜算，夫人，然而您言之过早。因据圣职者们所言，人是混合本性的存在，他在此世的生命是他身上神性与兽性之间永无止息的战争，而公正的天堂作为这场比武的裁判在旁观照。人的判断总在误导他，他的感官总引诱他与罪恶达成短暂的休战。他的感官在他行进间升腾起迷雾，人身上没有哪样天赋最终不背叛他的利益——正如上帝的智慧所意欲的；因此当人被击倒时，永恒的圣父既不恼怒也不悲伤——只要人鼓起勇气重新站起来。而当人被背叛、被击溃，却选择战斗到底——藐视常理与上帝亲自赐予的谋臣们——我认为圣徒们会在天堂举行庆典。"

"一篇非常漂亮的小布道，"王后说。"然而我不认为我们的格雷戈里能长久忍受一个沉溺于如此高谈阔论的妻子。他更喜欢听自己做那些漂亮的演说。"

随之是一阵沉默，唯有无目的的九月秋风在搅扰；但我相信两人中没有谁睡得安稳。

===Original===

About dawn one of the Queen's attendants roused Sir Gregory Darrell and conducted him into the hedged garden of Ordish, where Ysabeau walked in tranquil converse with Lord Berners. The old man was in high good-humor.

"My lad," said he, and clapped Sir Gregory upon the shoulder, "you have, I do protest, the very phoenix of sisters. I was never happier." And he went away chuckling.

The Queen said in a toneless voice, "We ride for Blackfriars now."

Darrell responded, "I am content, and ask but leave to speak, briefly, with Dame Rosamund before I die."

Then the woman came more near to him. "I am not used to beg, but within this hour you encounter death, and I have loved no man in all my life saving only you, Sir Gregory Darrell. Nor have you loved any person as you loved me once in France. Oh, today, I may speak freely, for with you the doings of that boy and girl are matters overpast. Yet were it otherwise﻿—eh, weigh the matter carefully! for I am mistress of England now, and England would I give you, and such love as that slim, white innocence has never dreamed of would I give you, Gregory Darrell﻿—No, no! ah, Mother of God, not you!" The Queen clapped one hand upon his lips.

"Listen," she quickly said; "I spoke to tempt you. But you saw, and you saw clearly, that it was the sickly whim of a wanton, and you never dreamed of yielding, for you love this Rosamund Eastney, and you know me to be vile. Then have a care of me! The strange woman am I, of whom we read that her house is the way to hell, going down to the chambers of death. Hoh, many strong men have been slain by me, and in the gray time to come will many others be slain by me, it may be; but never you among them, my Gregory, who are more wary, and more merciful, and who know that I have need to lay aside at least one comfortable thought against eternity."

===Chinese===

约莫黎明时分，王后的一名随从唤醒了格雷戈里·达雷尔爵士，将他领入奥迪什的篱笆花园，伊莎博正与伯纳斯勋爵在园中闲适地漫步。老人心情极好。

"我的孩子，"他说着，拍了拍格雷戈里爵士的肩膀，"我敢说，你有一个举世无双的妹妹。我从未如此快乐过。"他咯咯笑着走开了。

王后以平淡无波的声音说："我们现在骑马去黑衣修士区（Blackfriars）。"

达雷尔回答："我心甘情愿，只求在临死前与罗莎蒙德夫人说几句话。"

那女人走近了些。"我不习惯恳求，但此刻你即将赴死，而我一生中从未爱过任何男人，唯独你，格雷戈里·达雷尔爵士。你也不曾爱过任何人如同你曾经在法兰西爱我那样。哦，今日我可以畅所欲言，因为对你而言，那少年少女的往事已成过去。然而假若不然——嗯，仔细思量！因我如今是英格兰的主人，我愿将英格兰赐予你，我愿将那个纤弱白皙的纯真女子从未梦想过的爱赐予你，格雷戈里·达雷尔——不，不！啊，上帝之母，不是你！"王后将一只手拍在他的嘴唇上。

"听好，"她迅速说；"我说那些话是为了诱惑你。但你看见了，你清楚地看见了，那不过是放荡女人的病态幻想，你从未想过屈服，因为你爱这位罗莎蒙德·伊斯特尼，你知道我卑劣。那么请当心我！我就是那个奇异的女人，我们读到她的房子是通往地狱的路，直入死亡的居室。嗬，许多强壮的男人已被我杀死，在灰暗的未来也许还会有更多人被我杀死；但你永远不会在其中，我的格雷戈里，因为你更为谨慎，更为慈悲，你知道我至少需要保留一个安慰的念头以对抗永恒。"

===Original===

"I concede you to have been unwise﻿—" he hoarsely began.

About them fell the dying leaves, of many glorious colors, but the air of this new day seemed raw and chill.

Then Rosamund came through the opening in the hedge. "Now, choose," she said; "the woman offers life and high place and wealth, and it may be, a greater love than I am capable of giving you. I offer a dishonorable death within the moment."

And again, with that peculiar and imperious gesture, the man flung back his head, and he laughed. Said Gregory Darrell:

"I am I! and I will so to live that I may face without shame not only God, but also my own scrutiny." He wheeled upon the Queen and spoke henceforward very leisurely. "I love you; all my life long I have loved you, Ysabeau, and even now I love you: and you, too, dear Rosamund, I love, though with a difference. And every fibre of my being lusts for the power that you would give me, Ysabeau, and for the good which I would do with it in the England which I or blustering Roger Mortimer must rule; as every fibre of my being lusts for the man that I would be could I choose death without debate. And I think also of the man that you would make of me, my Rosamund.

"The man! And what is this man, this Gregory Darrell, that his welfare should be considered?﻿—an ape who chatters to himself of kinship with the archangels while filthily he digs for groundnuts! This much I know, at bottom.

"Yet more clearly do I perceive that this same man, like all his fellows, is a maimed god who walks the world dependent upon many wise and evil counsellors. He must measure, to a hair's-breadth, every content of the world by means of a bloodied sponge, tucked somewhere in his skull, a sponge which is ungeared by the first cup of wine and ruined by the touch of his own finger. He must appraise all that he judges with no better instruments than two bits of colored jelly, with a bungling makeshift so maladroit that the nearest horologer's apprentice could have devised a more accurate device. In fine, each man is under penalty condemned to compute eternity with false weights, to estimate infinity with a yardstick: and he very often does it, and chooses his own death without debate. For though, 'If then I do that which I would not I consent unto the law,' saith even an Apostle; yet a braver Pagan answers him, 'Perceive at last that thou hast in thee something better and more divine than the things which cause the various effects and, as it were, pull thee by the strings.'

"There lies the choice which every man must face﻿—whether rationally, as his reason goes, to accept his own limitations and make the best of his allotted prison-yard? or stupendously to play the fool and swear even to himself (while his own judgment shrieks and proves a flat denial), that he is at will omnipotent? You have chosen long ago, my poor proud Ysabeau; and I choose now, and differently: for poltroon that I am! being now in a cold drench of terror, I steadfastly protest I am not very much afraid, and I choose death without any more debate."

===Chinese===

"我承认你曾不够明智——"他嘶哑地开始说。

纷纷扬扬的落叶色彩绚丽，飘落在他们周围，但这个新日子的空气似乎阴冷刺骨。

这时罗莎蒙德穿过篱笆的缺口走来。"现在，请选择，"她说；"那个女人给你生命、高位和财富，或许，还有一份我无力给予你的更伟大的爱。我给你的是此刻即至的不光彩的死亡。"

那男人再次以那种奇特而威严的姿态将头向后一仰，笑了。格雷戈里·达雷尔说道：

"我就是我！我要如此活着，以便不仅面对上帝时毫无愧色，面对自己的审视时亦然。"他转向王后，此后说话极为从容。"我爱你；我一生都爱你，伊莎博，即便此刻我仍爱你：而你，亲爱的罗莎蒙德，我也爱你，尽管有所不同。我全身心的每一根纤维都渴望你——伊莎博——将给予我的权力，以及我将在这片我或暴躁的罗杰·莫蒂默必须统治的英格兰以这权力行的善事；正如我全身心的每一根纤维都渴望那个我若能毫无争议地选择死亡便可成为的那个人。而我也想到了你——我的罗莎蒙德——将使我成为的那个人。

"那个人！而这个人，这个格雷戈里·达雷尔，他的福祉何足挂齿？——一只猿猴，一边喋喋不休地自称与大天使有亲缘关系，一边卑鄙地刨挖花生！这是我心底深知的。

"然而我更清楚地察觉到，这个人，如同他所有的同类，是一个残缺的神，行走于世间，依赖于众多明智而邪恶的谋臣。他必须以一块藏于颅骨某处的血渍海绵，精确到一丝不苟地衡量世间万物——这块海绵被第一杯酒便破坏了功能，被他自己的手指一触便毁坏了。他必须以不过两块有色凝胶——笨拙的权宜之物，其拙劣程度连最近处钟表匠的学徒都能设计出更精确的装置——来评估他所判断的一切。总之，每个人都被惩罚性地判决以假砝码计算永恒，以码尺估算无限：他常常如此做了，毫无争议地选择自己的死亡。因为虽然使徒（Apostle）说过：'我所不愿意做的，我若去做，就是我认同了律法'；然而一位更勇敢的异教徒回答他说：'最终认识到你身上有某种比那些产生各种效果、仿佛以线牵引你的东西更美好、更神圣的东西。'

"这便是每个人必须面对的选择——是以理性——依照其理性所能——接受自身的局限，充分利用他被分配的狱院？还是壮丽地扮演傻瓜，甚至对自己发誓（在他自己的判断尖叫着、证明着彻底否定的同时），他是随心所欲的全能者？你早已选择了，我可怜而骄傲的伊莎博；而我现在选择，且选择不同：因为我虽是个懦夫！此刻浸透在恐惧的冷水中，但我坚定地声明我并非非常害怕，我选择死亡，不再有任何争议。"

===Original===

It was toward Rosamund that the Queen looked, and smiled a little pitifully. "Should Queen Ysabeau be angry or vexed or very cruel now, my Rosamund? for at bottom she is glad."

And the Queen said also: "I give you back your plighted word. I ride homeward to my husks, but you remain. Or rather, the Countess of Farrington departs for the convent of Ambresbury, disconsolate in her widowhood and desirous to have done with worldly affairs. It is most natural she should relinquish to her beloved and only brother all her dower-lands﻿—or so at least Messire de Berners acknowledges. Here, then, is the grant, my Gregory, that conveys to you those lands of Ralph de Belomys which last year I confiscated. And this tedious Messire de Berners is willing now﻿—he is eager to have you for a son-in-law."

About them fell the dying leaves, of many glorious colors, but the air of this new day seemed raw and chill, while, very calmly, Dame Ysabeau took Sir Gregory's hand and laid it upon the hand of Rosamund Eastney. "Our paladin is, in the outcome, a mortal man, and therefore I do not altogether envy you. Yet he has his moments, and you are capable. Serve, then, not only his desires but mine also, dear Rosamund."

There was a silence. The girl spoke as though it was a sacrament. "I will, madame and Queen."

Thus did the Queen end her holiday.

A little later the Countess of Farrington rode from Ordish with all her train save one; and riding from that place, where love was, she sang very softly.

===Chinese===

王后望向罗莎蒙德，微微苦笑。"伊莎博王后此刻该愤怒、该苦恼、该非常残忍吗，我的罗莎蒙德？因她内心是高兴的。"

王后又说："我将你的誓言归还给你。我骑马回到我的壳中去，但你留下。或者更确切地说，法林顿伯爵夫人启程前往安布里斯伯里修道院（convent of Ambresbury），在丧偶之痛中郁郁寡欢，渴望了断尘世之事。她将全部嫁妆地产让给她唯一的爱弟，是再自然不过的——至少伯纳斯老爷如此承认。那么，格雷戈里，这就是转让文书，将去年我没收的拉尔夫·德·贝洛米斯的那些土地授予你。而这位令人厌烦的伯纳斯老爷如今愿意了——他渴望你做他的女婿。"

纷纷扬扬的落叶色彩绚丽，飘落在他们周围，但这个新日子的空气似乎阴冷刺骨，而伊莎博夫人十分从容地拿起格雷戈里爵士的手，放在罗莎蒙德·伊斯特尼的手上。"我们的勇士终归是个凡人，因此我不完全羡慕你。但他有他的光芒时刻，而你有能力。那么，亲爱的罗莎蒙德，不仅服侍他的愿望，也服侍我的。"

随之是一阵沉默。少女说话，仿佛在说一段圣事。"我愿意，夫人，王后。"

王后的假日如是结束了。

不久之后，法林顿伯爵夫人骑马离开了奥迪什，随行之人唯少了一位；她骑马离开那个有爱的地方，轻声吟唱着。

===Original===

Sang Ysabeau:

"As with her dupes dealt Circe

Life deals with hers, for she

Reshapes them without mercy,

And shapes them swinishly,

To wallow swinishly,

And for eternity;

"Though, harder than the witch was,

Life, changing not the whole,

Transmutes the body, which was

Proud garment of the soul,

And briefly drugs the soul,

Whose ruin is her goal;

"And means by this thereafter

A subtler mirth to get,

And mock with bitterer laughter

Her helpless dupes' regret,

Their swinish dull regret

For what they half forget."

And within the hour came Hubert Frayne to Ordish, on a foam-specked horse, as he rode to announce to the King's men the King's barbaric murder overnight, at Berkeley Castle, by Queen Ysabeau's order.

"Ride southward," said Lord Berners, and panted as they buckled on his disused armor; "but harkee, Frayne! if you pass the Countess of Farrington's company, speak no syllable of your news, since it is not convenient that a lady so thoroughly and so praiseworthily﻿—Lord, Lord, how I have fattened!﻿—so intent on holy things, in fine, should have her meditations disturbed by any such unsettling tidings. Hey, son-in-law?"

Sir Gregory Darrell laughed, very bitterly. "He that is without blemish among you﻿—" he said. Then they armed completely, and went forth to battle against the murderous harlot.

===Chinese===

伊莎博吟唱道：

"正如喀耳刻（Circe）对待她的受骗者，
生命对待她的受害者，因她
无情地重塑他们，
将他们塑成猪猡的形状，
使他们如猪猡般打滚，
永生永世；

"尽管比女巫更为严酷，
生命并未改变整体，
只是将肉体转化——
那原是灵魂的华服——
并短暂地麻醉灵魂，
其毁灭正是她的目标；

"她以此为手段，
日后获取更微妙的欢愉，
以更苦涩的嘲讽
讥笑她无助的受骗者的悔恨，
他们如猪猡般迟钝的悔恨，
为那些他们半已遗忘的事。"

不到一个时辰，休伯特·弗雷恩（Hubert Frayne）骑着一匹口吐白沫的马来到奥迪什，他奉命向国王的人通报国王于昨夜在伯克利城堡被伊莎博王后下令野蛮谋杀的消息。

"骑马向南去，"伯纳斯勋爵说，他们为他系上久未穿着的铠甲时他气喘吁吁；"但听好，弗雷恩！若你遇到法林顿伯爵夫人的队伍，半个字也别说你的消息，因为让一位如此彻底且值得赞颂的——天哪，天哪，我胖了多少！——一心专注于神圣事务的女士被如此令人不安的消息扰乱沉思，是很不合适的。嘿，女婿？"

格雷戈里·达雷尔爵士非常苦涩地笑了。"你们中间没有瑕疵的人——"他说。随后他们全副武装，出征讨伐那个杀人的荡妇。

## V / 家主妇的故事

"Selh que m blasma vostr' amor ni m defen

Non podon far en re mon cor mellor,

Ni'l dous dezir qu'ieu ai de vos major,

Ni l'enveya' ni'l dezir, ni'l talen."

===Original===

The fifth novel.—Philippa of Hainault dares to love unthriftily, and with the prodigality of her affection shames treachery, and common sense, and high romance, quite stolidly; but, as loving goes, is overtopped by her more stolid squire.

===Chinese===

第五篇传奇——菲利帕·德·埃诺（Philippa of Hainault）敢于倾心去爱，以那不计得失的深情令背信弃义、理智权谋与骑士浪漫统统黯然失色，而且做得何其平淡；然而若论爱之深沉，她的忠仆却比她更胜一筹。

===Original===

In the year of grace 1326, upon Walburga's Eve, some three hours after sunset (thus Nicolas begins), had you visited a certain garden on the outskirts of Valenciennes, you might there have stumbled upon a big, handsome boy, prone on the turf, where by turns he groaned and vented himself in sullen curses. His profanity had its palliation. Heir to England though he was, you must know that this boy's father in the flesh had hounded him from England, as more recently had the lad's uncle Charles the Handsome driven him from France. Now had this boy and his mother (the same Queen Ysabeau about whom I have told you in the preceding tale) come as suppliants to the court of that stalwart nobleman Sire William (Count of Hainault, Holland, and Zealand, and Lord of Friesland), where their arrival had evoked the suggestion that they depart at their earliest convenience. Tomorrow, then, these footsore royalties, the Queen of England and the Prince of Wales, would be thrust out-of-doors to resume the weary beggarship, to knock again upon the obdurate gates of this unsympathizing king or that deaf emperor.

===Chinese===

公元一三二六年，圣瓦尔堡节前夜，日落后约三个时辰——尼古拉斯（Nicolas）如是开篇——倘若你当时凑巧游经瓦朗谢讷（Valenciennes）城郊的一座花园，便会撞见一个高大俊美的少年俯卧在草皮上，时而呻吟，时而愤愤地咒骂。他的粗鄙言语情有可原。虽是英格兰的继承人，但须知这少年的生父已将他逐出英格兰，而他那位美貌的舅舅查理（Charles the Handsome）不久前也将他逐出了法兰西。如今这少年和他母亲——便是我在前篇里提到的那位伊莎博王后（Queen Ysabeau）——前来向那位刚毅的贵人威廉老爷（Sire William）求援，他是埃诺（Hainault）、荷兰（Holland）和泽兰（Zealand）的伯爵，弗里斯兰（Friesland）的领主，而他们的到来只换来一句逐客令：请趁早动身。如此，明日这两位足底磨破的王室贵人——英格兰王后与威尔士亲王——便要被逐出门外，重拾那疲惫不堪的乞讨生涯，再去叩响这位冷漠国王或那位聋聩皇帝的铁石心门。

===Original===

Accordingly the boy aspersed his destiny. At hand a nightingale carolled as though an exiled prince were the blithest spectacle the moon knew.

There came through the garden a tall girl, running, stumbling in her haste. "Hail, King of England!" she said.

===Chinese===

于是那少年咒骂起自己的命运。近旁一只夜莺唱得正欢，仿佛一位流亡的王子乃是月亮所见过的最欢快的景象。

一个高挑的少女穿过花园跑来，踉踉跄跄，步履匆忙。"英格兰国王，万福！"她说道。

===Original===

"Do not mock me, Philippa!" the boy half-sobbed. Sulkily he rose to his feet.

"No mockery here, my fair sweet friend. No, I have told my father all which happened yesterday. I pleaded for you. He questioned me very closely. And when I had ended, he stroked his beard, and presently struck one hand upon the table. 'Out of the mouth of babes!' he said. Then he said: 'My dear, I believe for certain that this lady and her son have been driven from their kingdom wrongfully. If it be for the good of God to comfort the afflicted, how much more is it commendable to help and succor one who is the daughter of a king, descended from royal lineage, and to whose blood we ourselves are related!' And accordingly he and your mother have their heads together yonder, planning an invasion of England, no less, and the dethronement of your wicked father, my Edward. And accordingly—hail, King of England!" The girl clapped her hands gleefully. The nightingale sang.

===Chinese===

"莫要取笑我，菲利帕！"少年哽咽着说。他闷闷不乐地站起身来。

"我绝无取笑之意，我亲爱的好友。我把昨日发生的一切都告诉了父亲。我为你们求情。他问得很细。待我说完，他抚了抚胡须，随即一掌拍在桌上。'婴孩之口亦有真言！'他这样说道。接着他又说：'亲爱的，我确信这母子二人是被无辜逐出他们的王国的。既然安慰受苦之人合乎上帝的旨意，那么帮助和庇护一位国王之女、王族血脉的后裔——而且与我们自己也有血缘之亲——又当是多么值得称道的事！'于是他和你母亲正在那边商议，要入侵英格兰呢，要把你那邪恶的父亲——我的爱德华（Edward）——赶下王位。因此——英格兰国王，万福！"少女兴高采烈地拍起了手。夜莺还在歌唱。

===Original===

But the boy kept momentary silence. Not even in youth were the men of his race handicapped by excessively tender hearts; yesterday in the shrubbery the boy had kissed this daughter of Count William, in part because she was a healthy and handsome person, and partly because great benefit might come of an alliance with her father. Well! the Prince had found chance-taking not unfortunate. With the episode as foundation, Count William had already builded up the future queenship of England. The strong Count could do—and, as it seemed, was now in train to do—indomitable deeds to serve his son-in-law; and now the beggar of five minutes since foresaw himself, with this girl's love as ladder, mounting to the high habitations of the King of England, the Lord of Ireland, and the Duke of Aquitaine. Thus they would herald him.

===Chinese===

然而少年沉默了片刻。这个家族的男儿即便年少，也从不被过分柔软的心肠所累；昨日在灌木丛中，这少年已亲吻过威廉伯爵的女儿，一半因为她是个健康漂亮的姑娘，另一半则因为与她父亲结盟可获莫大的好处。好得很！亲王发现冒险并非全无收获。以此事为基石，威廉伯爵已经在构建英格兰未来的王后之位。这位强有力的伯爵能够——而且此刻似乎正在——做出不屈不挠的壮举来辅佐他未来的女婿；而那个五分钟前还在乞讨的少年，此刻已预见自己以这少女的爱为阶梯，登临英格兰国王、爱尔兰领主和阿基坦公爵（Duke of Aquitaine）的崇高宝座。人们将如此传颂他的名号。

===Original===

So he embraced the girl. "Hail, Queen of England!" said the Prince; and then, "If I forget—" His voice broke awkwardly. "My dear, if ever I forget—!" Their lips met now. The nightingale discoursed as if on a wager.

===Chinese===

于是他拥抱了少女。"英格兰王后，万福！"亲王说道。接着他又说："若我忘却——"他的声音尴尬地哽住了。"我亲爱的，若我竟会忘却——！"此刻他们的双唇相遇了。夜莺唱得仿佛在与人赌赛一般。

===Original===

Presently was mingled with the bird's descant another kind of singing. Beyond the yew-hedge as these two stood silent, breast to breast, passed young Jehan Kuypelant, one of the pages, fitting to the accompaniment of a lute his paraphrase of the song which Archilochus of Sicyon very anciently made in honor of Venus Melaenis, the tender Venus of the Dark.

===Chinese===

不一会儿，另一种歌声混入了夜莺的吟唱。两人默然相对、胸口相贴之际，年轻的侍从让·库佩朗（Jehan Kuypelant）从紫杉篱笆的那一边走过，伴着里拉琴的乐声，吟唱着西锡安的阿尔基洛科斯（Archilochus of Sicyon）在远古时代为黑暗中温柔的维纳斯——梅拉埃尼斯（Venus Melaenis）——所作的歌谣。

===Original===

At a gap in the hedge the young Brabanter paused. His singing ended, gulped. These two, who stood heart hammering against heart, saw for an instant Jehan Kuypelant's lean face silvered by the moonlight, his mouth a tiny abyss. Followed the beat of lessening footfalls, while the nightingale improvised an envoi.

===Chinese===

年轻的布拉班特人在篱笆的一处豁口停了下来。他唱完了，咽了口唾沫。这两个心贴着心站立的人，一瞬间瞥见让·库佩朗清瘦的面庞被月光染成银白，嘴巴张成了一个小小的深渊。随后是渐渐远去的脚步声，夜莺即兴吟了一段送别曲。

===Original===

But earlier Jehan Kuypelant also had sung, as though in rivalry with the bird.

===Chinese===

但早在之前，让·库佩朗也曾歌唱，仿佛在与那只夜莺争鸣。

===Original===

Sang Jehan Kuypelant:

"Hearken and heed, Melaenis!

For all that the litany ceased

When Time had pilfered the victim,

And flouted thy pale-lipped priest,

And set astir in the temple

Where burned the fires of thy shrine

The owls and wolves of the desert—

Yet hearken, (the issue is thine!)

And let the heart of Atys,

At last, at last, be mine!

"For I have followed, nor faltered—

Adrift in a land of dreams

Where laughter and pity and terror

Commingle as confluent streams,

I have seen and adored the Sidonian,

Implacable, fair and divine—

And bending low, have implored thee

To hearken, (the issue is thine!)

And let the heart of Atys,

At last, at last, be mine!"

===Chinese===

让·库佩朗唱道：

"倾听吧，梅拉埃尼斯，听我诉说！

尽管那祷词早已沉寂——

当岁月偷走了祭品，

嘲弄你苍白唇瓣的祭司，

令荒漠的枭与狼出没于

你那圣火长燃的庙宇——

请倾听吧，（成败由你裁夺！）

让阿提斯（Atys）的心，

终归、终归属于我！

"我曾追随，从未踟蹰——

飘荡在一片梦幻之境，

那里笑声与怜悯与恐惧

如交汇的河流般浑融，

我曾仰望、崇拜那西顿女子（Sidonian），

冷峻、美艳而神圣——

俯身恳求你

倾听吧，（成败由你裁夺！）

让阿提斯的心，

终归、终归属于我！"

===Original===

It is time, however, that we quit this subject and speak of other matters. Just twenty years later, on one August day in the year of grace 1346, Master John Copeland—as men now called Jehan Kuypelant, now secretary to the Queen of England—brought his mistress the unhandsome tidings that David Bruce had invaded her realm with forty thousand Scots to back him. The Brabanter found plump Queen Philippa with the kingdom's arbitress—Dame Catherine de Salisbury, whom King Edward, third of that name to reign in Britain, and now warring in France, very notoriously adored and obeyed.

===Chinese===

然而，我们该搁下这个话题，转说别的事情了。恰在二十年后，公元一三四六年八月的一天，约翰·科佩兰大师（Master John Copeland）——人们如今这样称呼让·库佩朗，他已成为英格兰王后的秘书——为女主人带来了一个令人不快的消息：大卫·布鲁斯（David Bruce）率四万苏格兰大军入侵了她的王国。这位布拉班特人找到丰腴的菲利帕王后时，她正与王国的实际掌权者凯瑟琳·德·索尔兹伯里夫人（Dame Catherine de Salisbury）在一起。爱德华国王——不列颠第三个以此名号登基的君主，此刻正在法兰西征战——对她倾慕有加、言听计从，此事早已尽人皆知。

===Original===

This king, indeed, had been despatched into France chiefly, they narrate, to release the Countess' husband, William de Montacute, from the French prison of the Châtelet. You may appraise her dominion by this fact: chaste and shrewd, she had denied all to King Edward, and in consequence he could deny her nothing; so she sent him to fetch back her husband, whom she almost loved. That armament had sailed from Southampton on Saint George's day.

===Chinese===

据说这位国王被派往法兰西，主要就是为了将伯爵夫人的丈夫威廉·德·蒙塔丘特（William de Montacute）从法国的沙特莱监狱（Châtelet）中解救出来。你由此便能掂量她的权势：她端庄而精明，对爱德华国王百般拒绝，结果国王反倒对她有求必应；于是她差遣他去把自己的丈夫领回来——那个她差不多还算爱着的人。那支舰队是在圣乔治日从南安普顿（Southampton）扬帆出发的。

===Original===

These two women, then, shared the Brabanter's execrable news. Already Northumberland, Westmoreland, and Durham were the broken meats of King David.

===Chinese===

于是这两个女人一同听到了这位布拉班特人带来的噩耗。诺森伯兰（Northumberland）、威斯特摩兰（Westmoreland）和达勒姆（Durham）此时已沦为戴维国王（King David）嘴边的残羹。

===Original===

The Countess presently exclaimed: "Let them weep for this that must! My place is not here."

Philippa said, half hopefully, "Do you forsake Sire Edward, Catherine?"

"Madame and Queen," the Countess answered, "in this world every man must scratch his own back. My lord has entrusted to me his castle of Wark, his fiefs in Northumberland. These, I hear, are being laid waste. Were there a thousand men-at-arms left in England I would say fight. As it is, our men are yonder in France and the island is defenceless. Accordingly I ride for the north to make what terms I may with the King of Scots."

===Chinese===

伯爵夫人随即高声说道："让他们为这不可避免之事哭泣去吧！我不该留在此处。"

菲利帕半带期望地问道："你要抛弃爱德华陛下吗，凯瑟琳？"

"夫人、王后，"伯爵夫人答道，"这世上每个人都得顾好自己。我的夫君将瓦克城堡（Wark）和他在诺森伯兰的封地托付给我照管。我听说那些地方正遭蹂躏。倘若英格兰还剩一千名甲士，我定说抵抗。可如今我们的将士远在法兰西，这座岛国毫无防备。因此我要北上，尽可能与苏格兰国王谈妥条件。"

===Original===

Now you might have seen the Queen's eye brighten. "Undoubtedly," said she, "in her lord's absence it is the wife's part to defend his belongings. And my lord's fief is England. I bid you Godspeed, Catherine." And when the Countess was gone, Philippa turned, her round face somewhat dazed and flushed. "She betrays him! she compounds with the Scot! Mother of Christ, let me not fail!"

"A ship must be despatched to bid Sire Edward return," said the secretary. "Otherwise all England is lost."

===Chinese===

你可以看到王后的眼睛亮了起来。"毫无疑问，"她说，"丈夫不在身边时，妻子理当保卫他的产业。而我丈夫的封地便是英格兰。祝你一路平安，凯瑟琳。"伯爵夫人走后，菲利帕转过身来，圆圆的面庞有些茫然，泛着红晕。"她背叛了他！她要与苏格兰人媾和！圣母基督，万勿让我有负使命！"

"须派一艘船去请爱德华陛下回师，"秘书说道，"否则整个英格兰就完了。"

===Original===

"Not so, John Copeland! We must let Sire Edward complete his overrunning of France, if such be the Trinity's will. You know perfectly well that he has always had a fancy to conquer France; and if I bade him return now he would be vexed."

"The disappointment of the King," John Copeland considered, "is a smaller evil than allowing all of us to be butchered."

"Not to me, John Copeland," the Queen said.

===Chinese===

"不可如此，约翰·科佩兰！若这是三位一体（Trinity）的旨意，我们须让爱德华陛下完成征服法兰西的大业。你很清楚他一直梦想着征服法兰西；若我此刻令他班师，他会恼怒的。"

"国王的失望，"约翰·科佩兰思量道，"总比我们通通被屠戮要好。"

"对我来说却不是这样，约翰·科佩兰，"王后说道。

===Original===

Now came many lords into the chamber, seeking Madame Philippa. "We must make peace with the Scottish rascal!—England is lost!—A ship must be sent entreating succor of Sire Edward!" So they shouted.

"Messieurs," said Queen Philippa, "who commands here? Am I, then, some woman of the town?"

===Chinese===

此时众多贵族涌入议事厅，来寻菲利帕夫人。"我们必须与那苏格兰无赖讲和！——英格兰完了！——须派船去恳求爱德华陛下回师！"他们如此喊叫着。

"诸位大人，"菲利帕王后说道，"此间由谁做主？莫非我不过是个市井妇人？"

===Original===

Ensued a sudden silence. John Copeland, standing by the seaward window, had picked up a lute and was fingering the instrument half-idly. Now the Marquess of Hastings stepped from the throng. "Pardon, Highness. But the occasion is urgent."

"The occasion is very urgent, my lord," the Queen assented, deep in meditation.

John Copeland flung back his head and without prelude began to carol lustily.

===Chinese===

一阵突如其来的沉默。约翰·科佩兰站在面朝大海的窗边，随手拿起一把里拉琴，心不在焉地拨弄着琴弦。此时黑斯廷斯侯爵（Marquess of Hastings）从人群中跨步而出。"恕罪，殿下。但事态紧急。"

"事态确实紧急，我的勋爵，"王后表示同意，陷入沉思。

约翰·科佩兰仰起头，毫无铺垫地引吭高歌起来。

===Original===

Sang John Copeland:

"There are taller lads than Atys,

And many are wiser than he—

How should I heed them?—whose fate is

Ever to serve and to be

Ever the lover of Atys,

And die that Atys may dine,

Live if he need me—Then heed me,

And speed me, (the moment is thine!)

And let the heart of Atys,

At last, at last, be mine!

"Fair is the form unbeholden,

And golden the glory of thee

Whose voice is the voice of a vision

Whose face is the foam of the sea,

And the fall of whose feet is the flutter

Of breezes in birches and pine,

When thou drawest near me, to hear me,

And cheer me, (the moment is thine!)

And let the heart of Atys,

At last, at last, be mine!"

===Chinese===

约翰·科佩兰唱道：

"比阿提斯更高大的少年有的是，

比他更聪明的人也多——

我何必在意他们？——我的宿命

便是永远侍奉、永远做

阿提斯永恒的情人，

为他赴死，好让他安然进食，

他若需要我便活下去——那么请垂听，

成全我吧，（成败由你裁夺！）

让阿提斯的心，

终归、终归属于我！

"未见其形已觉其美，

你那辉煌的荣光如金——

你的声音是幻象之声，

你的面庞是海上泡沫，

你每一步轻落都如微风

拂过白桦与松林，

当你走近我，倾听我，

抚慰我，（成败由你裁夺！）

让阿提斯的心，

终归、终归属于我！"

===Original===

I must tell you that the Queen shivered, as if with extreme cold. She gazed toward John Copeland wonderingly. The secretary was fretting at his lutestrings, with his head downcast. Then in a while the Queen turned to Hastings.

"The occasion is very urgent, my lord," the Queen assented. "Therefore it is my will that tomorrow one and all your men be mustered at Blackheath. We will take the field without delay against the King of Scots."

The riot began anew. "Madness!" they shouted; "lunar madness! We can do nothing until our King returns with our army!"

===Chinese===

我须告诉你，王后打了个寒噤，仿佛冷到了极点。她惊异地望向约翰·科佩兰。那秘书正低着头，心不在焉地拨弄着里拉琴弦。过了一会儿，王后转向黑斯廷斯。

"事态确实紧急，我的勋爵，"王后表示同意。"因此我意已决：明日你们所有人马须在布莱克希思（Blackheath）集结。我们将即刻出征，迎击苏格兰国王。"

骚动再度爆发。"疯了！"他们喊道；"简直是疯了！在国王率大军回来之前我们什么也做不了！"

===Original===

"In his absence," the Queen said, "I command here."

"You are not Regent," the Marquess answered. Then he cried, "This is the Regent's affair!"

"Let the Regent be fetched," Dame Philippa said, very quietly. They brought in her son, Messire Lionel, now a boy of eight years, and, in the King's absence, Regent of England.

===Chinese===

"国王不在，"王后说，"由我在此号令。"

"您并非摄政王，"侯爵答道。随即他高声喊道："此事该由摄政王做主！"

"去请摄政王来，"菲利帕夫人平静地说。他们把她的儿子——里昂内尔大人（Messire Lionel）——带了进来。这孩子年方八岁，在国王出征期间担任英格兰的摄政王。

===Original===

Both the Queen and the Marquess held papers. "Highness," Lord Hastings began, "for reasons of state which I lack time to explain, this document requires your signature. It is an order that a ship be despatched to ask the King's return. Your Highness may remember the pony you admired yesterday?" The Marquess smiled ingratiatingly. "Just here, your Highness—a crossmark."

"The dappled one?" said the Regent; "and all for making a little mark?" The boy jumped for the pen.

===Chinese===

王后和侯爵各自手持文件。"殿下，"黑斯廷斯勋爵开口道，"因时间紧迫，恕臣无法细陈国事缘由，这份文件需要您签署。这是一道令旨，派船去请国王回銮。殿下可还记得昨日您看中的那匹小马？"侯爵谄媚地笑了笑。"只需在这里，殿下——画一个十字即可。"

"那匹花斑的？"摄政王说；"就为了画个小记号？"那孩子跳起来去够鹅毛笔。

===Original===

"Lionel," said the Queen, "you are Regent of England, but you are also my son. If you sign that paper you will beyond doubt get the pony, but you will not, I think, care to ride him. You will not care to sit down at all, Lionel."

The Regent considered. "Thank you very much, my lord," he said in the ultimate, "but I do not like ponies any more. Do I sign here, Mother?"

===Chinese===

"里昂内尔，"王后说，"你是英格兰的摄政王，但你也是我的儿子。你若签了那份文书，那匹小马固然归你，但我看你不会想骑它。你恐怕连坐都坐不住了，里昂内尔。"

摄政王想了想。"非常感谢，我的勋爵，"他最后说道，"但我再也不喜欢小马了。母亲，我是在这里签名吗？"

===Original===

Philippa handed the Marquess a subscribed order to muster the English forces at Blackheath; then another, closing the English ports. "My lords," the Queen said, "this boy is the King's vicar. In defying him, you defy the King. Yes, Lionel, you have fairly earned a pot of jam for supper."

===Chinese===

菲利帕将一份已签署的命令递给了侯爵——令英军在布莱克希思集结；接着又递上另一份——封闭英格兰各港口。"诸位勋爵，"王后说道，"这孩子是国王的代理人。违抗他便是违抗国王。是的，里昂内尔，你确实值得赏一罐果酱当晚饭。"

===Original===

Then Hastings went away without speaking. That night assembled at his lodgings, by appointment, Viscount Heringaud, Adam Frere, the Marquess of Orme, Lord Stourton, the Earls of Neville and Gage, and Sir Thomas Rokeby. These seven found a long table there littered with pens and parchment; to the rear of it, with a lackey behind him, sat the Marquess of Hastings, meditative over a cup of Bordeaux.

===Chinese===

黑斯廷斯一声不吭地走了。当晚，赫林戈子爵（Viscount Heringaud）、亚当·弗里尔（Adam Frere）、奥姆侯爵（Marquess of Orme）、斯图尔顿勋爵（Lord Stourton）、内维尔伯爵（Earl of Neville）、盖奇伯爵（Earl of Gage）和托马斯·罗克比爵士（Sir Thomas Rokeby）依约来到他的住所。这七个人发现一张长桌上堆满了鹅毛笔和羊皮纸；桌后坐着黑斯廷斯侯爵，身后站着一个仆从，他正对着一杯波尔多酒若有所思。

===Original===

Presently Hastings said: "My friends, in creating our womankind the Maker of us all was beyond doubt actuated by laudable and cogent reasons; so that I can merely lament my inability to fathom these reasons. I shall obey the Queen faithfully, since if I did otherwise Sire Edward would have my head off within a day of his return. In consequence, I do not consider it convenient to oppose his vicar. Tomorrow I shall assemble the tatters of troops which remain to us, and tomorrow we march northward to inevitable defeat. Tonight I am sending a courier into Northumberland. He is an obliging person, and would convey—to cite an instance—eight letters quite as blithely as one."

===Chinese===

黑斯廷斯随即说道："诸位朋友，造物主创造我们的女人时，无疑是出于值得赞颂且无可辩驳的理由；我只能遗憾自己参不透这些理由。我将忠实地服从王后，因为若不如此，爱德华陛下回来后不到一天便要砍我的脑袋。因此，我觉得违抗他的代理人并无益处。明日我将把我们残余的零散兵力集结起来，明天便挥师北上，迎接不可避免的败局。今晚我将派一名信使前往诺森伯兰。他是个热心肠的人，送八封信和送一封信一样乐意——举个例子。"

===Original===

Each man glanced furtively about. England was in a panic by this, and knew itself to lie before the Bruce defenceless. The all-powerful Countess of Salisbury had compounded with King David; now Hastings, too, their generalissimo, compounded. What the devil! loyalty was a sonorous word, and so was patriotism, but, after all, one had estates in the north.

===Chinese===

每个人都心虚地左右张望。英格兰此时已陷入恐慌，自知在布鲁斯面前毫无防备。权倾朝野的索尔兹伯里伯爵夫人已与戴维国王媾和；如今他们的总司令黑斯廷斯也跟着媾和了。见鬼！忠诚是个堂皇的词，爱国也是，但毕竟——你在北方还有产业呢。

===Original===

The seven wrote in silence. I must tell you that when they had ended, Hastings gathered the letters into a heap, and without glancing at the superscriptures, handed all these letters to the attendant lackey. "For the courier," he said.

The fellow left the apartment. Presently you heard a departing clatter of hoofs, and Hastings rose. He was a gaunt, terrible old man, gray-bearded, and having high eyebrows that twitched and jerked.

===Chinese===

七个人默默地写着。我须告诉你，写完之后，黑斯廷斯把信收拢成一摞，看都没看信封上的字，便统统交给了那个随侍的仆从。"交给信使，"他说。

那人出了房间。不一会儿你便听到一阵渐远的马蹄声，黑斯廷斯站了起来。他是个枯瘦而令人畏惧的老人，灰白的胡须，高挑的眉毛不住地抽动着。

===Original===

"We have saved our precious skins," said he. "Hey, you fidgeters, you ferments of sour offal! I commend your common sense, messieurs, and I request you to withdraw. Even a damned rogue such as I has need of a cleaner atmosphere in order to breathe comfortably." The seven went away without further speech.

===Chinese===

"我们保住了自己的小命，"他说。"嘿，你们这些坐立不安的家伙，你们这些发臭的脓水！我赞赏诸位的精明，诸位，请回吧。就连我这样该下地狱的无赖，也需要一口清新的空气才能舒坦地呼吸。"七人无言而退。

===Original===

They narrate that next day the troops marched for Durham, where the Queen took up her quarters. The Bruce had pillaged and burned his way to a place called Beaurepair, within three miles of the city. He sent word to the Queen that if her men were willing to come forth from the town he would abide and give them battle.

She replied that she accepted his offer, and that the barons would gladly risk their lives for the realm of their lord the King. The Bruce grinned and kept silence, since he had in his pocket letters from most of them protesting they would do nothing of the sort.

===Chinese===

据载次日军队向达勒姆进发，王后在那里驻扎下来。布鲁斯一路烧杀抢掠，已推进到距城不到三英里的一个叫博雷佩尔（Beaurepair）的地方。他传话给王后：若她的人马愿意出城迎战，他便在此等候。

她回复说接受他的挑战，男爵们将乐于为国王陛下的疆土拼死一战。布鲁斯咧嘴一笑，没有作声，因为他口袋里揣着他们当中大多数人的来信，信中保证他们绝不会做这种事。

===Original===

Here is comedy. On one side you have a horde of half-naked savages, a shrewd master holding them in leash till the moment be auspicious; on the other, a housewife at the head of a tiny force lieutenanted by perjurers, by men already purchased. God knows what dreams she had of miraculous victories, while her barons trafficked in secret with the Bruce. It is recorded that, on the Saturday before Michaelmas, when the opposing armies marshalled in the Bishop's Park, at Auckland, not a captain on either side believed the day to be pregnant with battle. There would be a decent counterfeit of resistance; afterward the little English army would vanish pell-mell, and the Bruce would be master of the island. The farce was prearranged, the actors therein were letter-perfect.

===Chinese===

这真是一出喜剧。一边是一群半裸的蛮族，由一个精明的主人攥在手中，只等时机成熟便松手出击；另一边是一位家主妇率领着一支小小的部队，麾下尽是已被收买的叛徒和背誓之徒。天知道她对奇迹般的胜利怀有怎样的梦想，而她的男爵们却在暗中与布鲁斯做着交易。据载在米迦勒节（Michaelmas）前的那个星期六，当两军在奥克兰（Auckland）主教的猎苑中列阵时，双方没有一位将领相信这天真会爆发一场战斗。不过是一场体面的假抵抗；之后那支小小的英军便会溃散而去，布鲁斯将成为这座岛屿的主人。这出闹剧早已排演好，戏中人都烂熟于心。

===Original===

That morning at daybreak John Copeland came to the Queen's tent, and informed her quite explicitly how matters stood. He had been drinking overnight with Adam Frere and the Earl of Gage, and after the third bottle had found them candid. "Madame and Queen, we are betrayed. The Marquess of Hastings, our commander, is inexplicably smitten with a fever. He will not fight today. Not one of your lords will fight today." Master Copeland laid bare such part of the scheme as yesterday's conviviality had made familiar. "Therefore I counsel retreat. Let the King be summoned out of France."

===Chinese===

那天清晨破晓时分，约翰·科佩兰来到王后的帐篷，将事态的原委一五一十地禀告了她。他前夜与亚当·弗里尔和盖奇伯爵饮酒，三瓶酒下肚后他们便对他坦诚相告了。"夫人、王后，我们遭到了背叛。我们的统帅黑斯廷斯侯爵莫名其妙地染上了寒热。他今日不会出战。您没有一位勋爵会出战。"科佩兰大师把昨日酒席上打探到的阴谋和盘托出。"因此臣建议撤退。召国王从法兰西回来吧。"

===Original===

Queen Philippa shook her head, as she cut up squares of toast and dipped them in milk for the Regent's breakfast. "Sire Edward would be vexed. He has always wanted to conquer France. I shall visit the Marquess as soon as Lionel is fed—do you know, John Copeland, I am anxious about Lionel; he is irritable and coughed five times during the night—and then I will attend to this affair."

===Chinese===

菲利帕王后摇摇头，一边把吐司切成小方块，蘸上牛奶，为摄政王准备早餐。"爱德华陛下会恼怒的。他一直想征服法兰西。等里昂内尔吃完早饭，我就去看望侯爵——你知道吗，约翰·科佩兰，我很担心里昂内尔；他很烦躁，夜里咳了五次——然后我再来处理这件事。"

===Original===

She found the Marquess in bed, groaning, the coverlet pulled up to his chin. "Pardon, Highness," said Lord Hastings, "but I am an ill man. I cannot rise from this couch."

"I do not question the gravity of your disorder," the Queen retorted, "since it is well known that the same illness brought about the death of Iscariot. Nevertheless, I bid you get up and lead our troops against the Scot."

Now the hand of the Marquess veiled his countenance. "I am an ill man," he muttered, doggedly. "I cannot rise from this couch."

There was a silence.

===Chinese===

她发现侯爵卧在床上，呻吟着，被子拉到了下巴。"恕罪，殿下，"黑斯廷斯勋爵说，"臣身染重病。无法从这榻上起身。"

"我不怀疑你病得不轻，"王后反驳道，"因为众所周知，同样的病症曾要了加略人（Iscariot）的命。尽管如此，我命令你起身，率领我们的军队迎击苏格兰人。"

侯爵用手遮住了脸。"臣病了，"他固执地嘟囔着。"无法从这榻上起身。"

一阵沉默。

===Original===

"My lord," the Queen presently began, "without is an army prepared—yes, and quite able—to defend our England. The one requirement of this army is a leader. Afford them that, my lord—ah, I know that our peers are sold to the Bruce, yet our yeomen at least are honest. Give them, then, a leader, and they cannot but conquer, since God also is honest and incorruptible. Pardieu! a woman might lead these men, and lead them to victory!"

Hastings answered: "I am ill. I cannot rise from this couch."

"There is no man left in England," said the Queen, "since Sire Edward went into France. Praise God, I am his wife!" She went away without flurry.

===Chinese===

"我的勋爵，"王后随即开口道，"外面有一支军队整装待发——是的，完全有能力——保卫我们的英格兰。这支军队所缺的只有一位统帅。给他们一个统帅吧，我的勋爵——啊，我知道我们的贵族已收了布鲁斯的贿赂，但至少我们的自耕农（yeomen）是诚实的。给他们一个统帅，他们必胜无疑，因为上帝也是诚实而不腐的。天哪！一个女人也能率领这些将士，领他们走向胜利！"

黑斯廷斯答道："臣病了。无法从这榻上起身。"

"英格兰已无男儿了，"王后说，"自爱德华陛下去了法兰西之后。赞美上帝，我是他的妻子！"她毫不慌乱地走了出去。

===Original===

Through the tent-flap Hastings beheld all that which followed. The English force was marshalled in four divisions, each commanded by a bishop and a baron. You could see the men fidgeting, puzzled by the delay; as a wind goes about a cornfield, vague rumors were going about those wavering spears. Toward them rode Philippa, upon a white palfrey, alone and perfectly tranquil. Her eight lieutenants were now gathered about her in voluble protestation, and she heard them out. Afterward she spoke, without any particular violence, as one might order a strange cur from his room. Then the Queen rode on, as though these eight declaiming persons had ceased to be of interest. She reined up before her standard-bearer, and took the standard in her hand. She began again to speak, and immediately the army was in an uproar; the barons were clustering behind her, in stealthy groups of two or three whisperers each; all were in the greatest amazement and knew not what to do; but the army was shouting the Queen's name.

===Chinese===

黑斯廷斯透过帐帘看到了接下来发生的一切。英军被编为四个方阵，各由一位主教和一位男爵统领。你可以看到士兵们焦躁不安，被这延误弄得莫名其妙；如风掠过麦田一般，隐约的流言在那些摇晃的长矛间四处传播。菲利帕骑着一匹白色矮种马向他们走来，独自一人，神色安详。她的八位副官此刻围了上来，喋喋不休地抗议着，她让他们把话说完。然后她开口了，并无多少激烈的言辞，就像吩咐一条陌生的野狗离开房间似的。接着王后策马前行，仿佛这八个慷慨陈词的人物已经不值得她关注了。她在旗手面前勒住马，亲手接过军旗。她又开始讲话，刹那间整个军队沸腾了；男爵们簇拥在她身后，三三两两地聚在一起窃窃私语；所有人都惊愕不已，不知所措；但全军将士齐声高呼王后的名号。

===Original===

"Now is England shamed," said Hastings, "since a woman alone dares to encounter the Scot. She will lead them into battle—and by God! there is no braver person under heaven than yonder Dutch Frau! Friend David, I perceive that your venture is lost, for those men would follow her to storm hell if she desired it."

He meditated, and shrugged. "And so would I," said Hastings.

===Chinese===

"英格兰羞愧了，"黑斯廷斯说，"因为只有一个女人敢于迎战苏格兰人。她要亲自率领他们上阵——天哪！这世上没有比那位荷兰夫人更勇敢的人了！大卫老兄，我看你的大业完了，因为只要她一声令下，这些人会追随她去攻打地狱。"

他沉思片刻，耸了耸肩。"我也会追随的，"黑斯廷斯说。

===Original===

A little afterward a gaunt and haggard old man, bareheaded and very hastily dressed, reined his horse by the Queen's side. "Madame and Queen," said Hastings, "I rejoice that my recent illness is departed. I shall, by God's grace, on this day drive the Bruce from England."

===Chinese===

过了不久，一个枯瘦憔悴的老人——没戴帽子，衣衫潦草——策马来到王后身边。"夫人、王后，"黑斯廷斯说，"臣欣喜地发现先前的病症已然消退。臣将蒙上帝的恩典，于今日将布鲁斯驱逐出英格兰。"

===Original===

Philippa was not given to verbiage. Doubtless she had her emotions, but none was visible upon the honest face. She rested one plump hand upon the big-veined hand of Hastings. That was all. "I welcome back the gallant gentleman of yesterday. I was about to lead your army, my friend, since there was no one else to do it, but I was hideously afraid. At bottom every woman is a coward."

"You were afraid to do it," said the Marquess, "but you were going to do it, because there was no one else to do it! Ho, madame! had I an army of such cowards I would drive the Scot not past the Border but beyond the Orkneys."

===Chinese===

菲利帕不善辞令。她内心无疑波澜起伏，但那诚实的面庞上却看不出分毫。她将一只圆润的小手放在黑斯廷斯青筋暴突的大手上。仅此而已。"我欢迎昨日那位英勇的绅士归来。我本打算亲自率领你的军队，我的朋友，因为无人可托，但我恐惧万分。归根结底，每个女人都是懦夫。"

"你害怕去做，"侯爵说，"但你还是要去做，因为无人可托！夫人啊！若我有一支由这样的'懦夫'组成的军队，我不仅要把苏格兰人赶过边境，还要一直追赶到奥克尼群岛（Orkneys）之外。"

===Original===

The Queen then said, "But you are unarmed."

"Highness," he replied, "it is surely apparent that I, who have played the traitor to two monarchs within the same day, cannot with either decency or comfort survive that day." He turned upon the lords and bishops twittering about his horse's tail. "You merchandise, get back to your stations, and if there was ever an honest woman in any of your families, the which I doubt, contrive to get yourselves killed this day, as I mean to do, in the cause of the honestest and bravest woman our time has known." Immediately the English forces marched toward Merrington.

===Chinese===

王后接着说："可你没有披甲。"

"殿下，"他答道，"显而易见，臣一日之内已对两位君主行了叛逆之事，无论体面还是良心，都已无法苟活到这一天结束。"他转身面向围在马尾后窃窃私语的贵族和主教们。"你们这群货物，回到你们的阵位上去！若你们家族中曾有过一个诚实的女人——虽然我深表怀疑——就学我的样，今日设法战死沙场，为我们这个时代最诚实最勇敢的女人献身！"英军随即向梅林顿（Merrington）进发。

===Original===

Philippa returned to her pavilion and inquired for John Copeland. She was informed that he had ridden off, armed, in company with five of her immediate retainers. She considered this strange, but made no comment.

===Chinese===

菲利帕回到自己的营帐，询问约翰·科佩兰的去向。她被告知他已全副武装，带着她的五名亲随策马离去了。她觉得有些蹊跷，但没有说什么。

===Original===

You picture her, perhaps, as spending the morning in prayer, in beatings upon her breast, and in lamentations. Philippa did nothing of the sort. She considered her cause to be so clamantly just that to expatiate to the Holy Father upon its merits would be an impertinence; it was not conceivable that He would fail her; and in any event, she had in hand a deal of sewing which required immediate attention. Accordingly she settled down to her needlework, while the Regent of England leaned his head against her knee, and his mother told him that ageless tale of Lord Huon, who in a wood near Babylon encountered the King of Faëry, and subsequently bereaved an atrocious Emir of his beard and daughter. All this the industrious woman narrated in a low and pleasant voice, while the wide-eyed Regent attended and at the proper intervals gulped his cough-mixture.

===Chinese===

你或许想象她整个上午都在祈祷，捶胸顿足，哀哭不已。菲利帕绝非如此。她认为自己所为之奋斗的事业正义昭彰，向圣父陈述其功绩反倒是一种僭越；她无法想象上帝会弃她于不顾；况且无论怎样，手头还有一堆针线活需要立刻处理。于是她坐下来做起女红，英格兰的摄政王把头靠在她膝上，母亲给他讲那个古老的故事：于翁勋爵（Lord Huon）在巴比伦附近的一片树林中遇到了仙王（King of Faëry），后来从一个残暴的埃米尔（Emir）手中夺走了他的胡须和女儿。这位勤勉的妇人用低沉悦耳的声音讲述着这一切，睁大眼睛的摄政王专心听着，每隔适当的时候便咽一口止咳药。

===Original===

You must know that about noon Master John Copeland came into the tent. "We have conquered," he said. "Now, by the Face!"—thus, scoffingly, he used her husband's favorite oath—"now, by the Face! there was never a victory more complete! The Scottish army is fled, it is as utterly dispersed from man's seeing as are the sands which dried the letters King Ahasuerus gave the admirable Esther!"

===Chinese===

须知约莫正午时分，约翰·科佩兰大师走进了帐篷。"我们胜了，"他说。"天哪！"——他戏谑地用了她丈夫最爱发的誓——"天哪！从未有过如此彻底的胜利！苏格兰军队溃逃了，其四散奔逃之状，正如当年洗刷亚哈随鲁王（King Ahasuerus）写给贤慧的以斯帖（Esther）的书信的沙粒一般，从人们视野中消失得干干净净！"

===Original===

"I rejoice," the Queen said, looking up from her sewing, "that we have conquered, though in nature I expected nothing else—Oh, horrible!" She sprang to her feet with a cry of anguish. Here in little you have the entire woman; the victory of her armament was to her a thing of course, since her cause was just, whereas the loss of two front teeth by John Copeland was a calamity.

===Chinese===

"我很欣慰，"王后从针线活中抬起头来说，"我们胜了，虽然这本在意料之中——噢，太可怕了！"她痛叫一声，猛地跳了起来。这里你便看到了这女人的全貌：她军队的胜利对她而言不过是理所当然之事，因为她的事业是正义的；而约翰·科佩兰掉了两颗门牙，那才是天大的灾难。

===Original===

He drew her toward the tent-flap, which he opened. Without was a mounted knight, in full panoply, his arms bound behind him, surrounded by the Queen's five retainers. "In the rout I took him," said John Copeland; "though, as my mouth witnesses, I did not find this David Bruce a tractable prisoner."

"Is that, then, the King of Scots?" Philippa demanded, as she mixed salt and water for a mouthwash. "Sire Edward should be pleased, I think. Will he not love me a little now, John Copeland?"

===Chinese===

他把她引向帐帘，掀了开来。外面有一位全副武装的骑士骑在马上，双手被反绑在身后，被王后的五名亲随团团围住。"在溃军中我擒住了他，"约翰·科佩兰说；"不过，从我的嘴便可看出，这位大卫·布鲁斯可不是个听话的俘虏。"

"这便是苏格兰国王？"菲利帕问道，一边调着盐水漱口液。"我想爱德华陛下会高兴的。他现在会不会爱我一点了，约翰·科佩兰？"

===Original===

John Copeland lifted both plump hands toward his lips. "He could not choose," John Copeland said; "madame, he could no more choose but love you than I could choose."

===Chinese===

约翰·科佩兰将两只胖乎乎的手举到唇边。"他别无选择，"约翰·科佩兰说；"夫人，他不可能不爱你，正如我不可能选择不爱。"

===Original===

Philippa sighed. Afterward she bade John Copeland rinse his gums and then take his prisoner to Hastings. He told her the Marquess was dead, slain by the Knight of Liddesdale. "That is a pity," the Queen said. She reflected a while, reached her decision. "There is left alive in England but one man to whom I dare entrust the keeping of the King of Scots. My barons are sold to him; if I retain Messire David by me, one or another lord will engineer his escape within the week, and Sire Edward will be vexed. Yet listen, John—" She unfolded her plan.

===Chinese===

菲利帕叹了口气。随后她吩咐约翰·科佩兰漱漱牙龈，然后把俘虏交给黑斯廷斯。他告诉她侯爵已经死了，被利德斯代尔骑士（Knight of Liddesdale）所杀。"可惜了，"王后说。她沉思片刻，拿定了主意。"英格兰如今只剩一个人，我敢把苏格兰国王托付给他看管。我的男爵们都被收买了；若我留大卫大人在身边，不出一周他们中便会有人设法让他逃跑，爱德华陛下要恼怒的。不过听我说，约翰——"她将自己的计划和盘托出。

===Original===

"I have long known," he said, when she had done, "that in all the world there was no lady more lovable. Twenty years I have loved you, my Queen, and yet it is only today I perceive that in all the world there is no lady more wise than you."

Philippa touched his cheek, maternally. "Foolish boy! You tell me the King of Scots has an arrow-wound in his nose? I think a bread poultice would be best." She told him how to make this poultice, and gave other instructions. Then John Copeland left the tent and presently rode away with his company.

===Chinese===

"我早就知道，"她说完之后他答道，"这世上没有比你更可爱的女子。我爱了你二十年，我的王后，然而直到今天我才发觉，这世上没有比你更聪慧的女子。"

菲利帕慈母般地抚了抚他的脸颊。"傻孩子！你是说苏格兰国王鼻子上中了一箭？我觉得用面包做个敷贴最好。"她教他如何制作这种敷贴，又嘱咐了其他事项。随后约翰·科佩兰离开了帐篷，带着他的随从策马而去。

===Original===

Philippa saw that the Regent had his dinner, and afterward mounted her white palfrey and set out for the battlefield. There the Earl of Neville, as second in command, received her with great courtesy. God had shown to her Majesty's servants most singular favor: despite the calculations of reasonable men—to which, she might remember, he had that morning taken the liberty to assent—some fifteen thousand Scots were slain. True, her gallant general was no longer extant, though this was scarcely astounding when one considered the fact that he had voluntarily entered the melee quite unarmed. A touch of age, perhaps; Hastings was always an eccentric man: in any event, as epilogue, this Neville congratulated the Queen that—by blind luck, he was forced to concede—her worthy secretary had made a prisoner of the Scottish King. Doubtless, Master Copeland was an estimable scribe, and yet—Ah, yes, Lord Neville quite followed her Majesty—beyond doubt, the wardage of a king was an honor not lightly to be conferred. Oh, yes, he understood; her Majesty desired that the office should be given some person of rank. And pardie! her Majesty was in the right. Eh? said the Earl of Neville.

===Chinese===

菲利帕看着摄政王用完午餐，然后跨上她的白色矮种马前往战场。在那里，内维尔伯爵作为副统帅，以极大的礼数迎接了她。上帝对她陛下的仆从们展现出了格外的恩典：尽管那些明智之人的算计——她或许还记得，他今早曾冒昧地附和了那些算计——约有一万五千名苏格兰人被歼灭。诚然，她英勇的统帅已经不在了，但考虑到他手无寸铁便自愿冲入混战这一事实，这也不足为奇。或许是年岁不饶人吧；黑斯廷斯向来是个怪人：无论如何，作为收尾，这位内维尔向王后道贺——尽管他不得不承认全凭运气——她能干的秘书已将苏格兰国王擒获。科佩兰大师无疑是一位令人敬佩的文书，不过——啊，是的，内维尔勋爵完全理解她陛下的意思——毫无疑问，看管一位国王乃是一项不可轻易授予的殊荣。哦，是的，他明白；她陛下希望将此职授予一位有爵位的人。天哪！她陛下说得对。是吗？内维尔伯爵说。

===Original===

Intently gazing into the man's shallow eyes, Philippa assented. Master Copeland had acted unwarrantably in riding off with his captive. Let him be sought at once. She dictated to Neville's secretary a letter, which informed John Copeland that he had done what was not agreeable in purloining her prisoner. Let him without delay deliver the King to her good friend the Earl of Neville.

===Chinese===

菲利帕直直盯着这人浅薄的双眼，表示赞同。科佩兰大师私自带着俘虏策马而去，实在越权。须即刻寻找他。她向内维尔的秘书口授了一封信，告知约翰·科佩兰他私自带走她的俘虏，此事令她不满。令他立即将国王交给她的好友内维尔伯爵。

===Original===

To Neville this was satisfactory, since he intended that once in his possession David Bruce should escape forthwith. The letter, I repeat, suited this smirking gentleman in its tiniest syllable, and the single difficulty was to convey it to John Copeland, for as to his whereabouts neither Neville nor anyone else had the least notion.

===Chinese===

内维尔对此十分满意，因为他打算一旦大卫·布鲁斯落入他的掌控便立即放跑他。这封信，我再说一遍，从头至尾每一个字都正合这位笑容可掬的绅士的心意，唯一的难题是把它送达约翰·科佩兰手中，因为他的行踪内维尔和其他人一样一无所知。

===Original===

This was immaterial, however, for they narrate that next day a letter signed with John Copeland's name was found pinned to the front of Neville's tent. I cite a passage therefrom: "I will not give up my royal prisoner to a woman or a child, but only to my own lord, Sire Edward, for to him I have sworn allegiance, and not to any woman. Yet you may tell the Queen she may depend on my taking excellent care of King David. I have poulticed his nose, as she directed."

===Chinese===

然而这无关紧要，因为据载次日一封署名约翰·科佩兰的信被发现钉在内维尔帐篷的正面。我摘引其中一段："我不会将我的王室俘虏交给一个女人或一个孩子，只交给我自己的君主爱德华陛下，因为我向他宣誓效忠，而非向任何女人。但你可以转告王后，她尽可放心，我会好好照看戴维国王的。我已按她的指示为他的鼻子做了敷贴。"

===Original===

Here was a nonplus, not without its comical side. Two great realms had met in battle, and the king of one of them had vanished like a soap-bubble. Philippa was in a rage—you could see that both by her demeanor and by the indignant letters she dictated; true, none of these letters could be delivered, since they were all addressed to John Copeland. Meanwhile, Scotland was in despair, whereas the traitor English barons were in a frenzy, because they did not know what had become of their fatal letters to the Bruce, or of him either. The circumstances were unique, and they remained unchanged for three feverish weeks.

===Chinese===

这真是一个令人哭笑不得的困境，而且不乏喜剧色彩。两个伟大的王国兵戎相见，其中一个王国的国王却像肥皂泡一样凭空消失了。菲利帕勃然大怒——你从她的举止和口授的那些愤慨的书信中便能看出；当然，这些信没有一封能送达，因为收件人全是约翰·科佩兰。与此同时，苏格兰陷入绝望，而那些叛国的英格兰男爵们则惶惶不可终日，因为他们不知道那些致命的投敌信函下落如何，也不知道布鲁斯本人去了哪里。事态前所未有，而且在紧张焦灼的三个星期里一成不变。

===Original===

We will now return to affairs in France, where on the day of the Nativity, as night gathered about Calais, John Copeland came unheralded to the quarters of King Edward, then besieging that city. Master Copeland entreated audience, and got it readily enough, since there was no man alive whom Sire Edward more cordially desired to lay his fingers upon.

===Chinese===

现在让我们回到法兰西的事务。在圣母诞生节（Nativity）那天，夜幕笼罩加莱（Calais）之际，约翰·科佩兰不请自来，来到了正在围攻该城的爱德华国王的营帐。科佩兰大师请求觐见，很快就得到了允许，因为世上没有第二个人比这位陛下更热切地想将他攥在手心。

===Original===

A page brought Master Copeland to the King, that stupendous, blond and incredibly big person. With Sire Edward were that careful Italian, Almerigo di Pavia, who afterward betrayed Sire Edward, and a lean soldier whom Master Copeland recognized as John Chandos. These three were drawing up an account of the recent victory at Créçi, to be forwarded to all mayors and sheriffs in England, with a cogent postscript as to the King's incidental and immediate need of money.

===Chinese===

一名侍从将科佩兰大师引到了国王面前——那是一个魁梧得令人难以置信的金发巨人。陪伴在爱德华陛下身旁的，有那位谨慎的意大利人阿尔梅里戈·迪·帕维亚（Almerigo di Pavia）——他日后背叛了爱德华陛下——以及一位瘦削的军人，科佩兰大师认出那是约翰·尚多斯（John Chandos）。三人正在起草一份关于最近克雷西（Créci）大捷的文书，准备下发给英格兰所有的市长和治安官，附带一段不容忽视的后记，说明国王因急需用钱而"顺便"提出的筹款要求。

===Original===

Now King Edward sat leaning far back in his chair, a hand on either hip, and with his eyes narrowing as he regarded Master Copeland. Had the Brabanter flinched, the King would probably have hanged him within the next ten minutes; finding his gaze unwavering, the King was pleased. Here was a novelty; most people blinked quite honestly under the scrutiny of those fierce big eyes, which were blue and cold and of an astounding lustre. The lid of the left eye drooped a little: this was Count Manuel's legacy, they whispered.

===Chinese===

此时爱德华国王深深地靠在椅背上，双手叉腰，眯起眼睛打量着科佩兰大师。倘若这布拉班特人退缩了，国王很可能在接下来的十分钟内便将他吊死；见他目光坚定不移，国王倒有些欣慰。这是个新鲜事；大多数人面对那双凌厉的蓝色巨眼——冰冷而闪烁着惊人的光芒——都老老实实地眨巴眼睛。他的左眼皮微微下垂：人们低声说，这是曼努埃尔伯爵（Count Manuel）留下的纪念。

===Original===

The King rose with a jerk and took John Copeland's hand. "Ha!" he grunted, "I welcome the squire who by his valor has captured the King of Scots. And now, my man, what have you done with Davie?"

===Chinese===

国王猛地站起身来，握住约翰·科佩兰的手。"哈！"他咕哝道，"我欢迎这位凭英勇擒获苏格兰国王的侍从。那么，我的好汉，你把戴维（Davie）怎么处置了？"

===Original===

John Copeland answered: "Highness, you may find him at your convenience safely locked in Bamborough Castle. Meanwhile, I entreat you, sire, do not take it amiss if I did not surrender King David to the orders of my lady Queen, for I hold my lands of you, and not of her, and my oath is to you, and not to her, unless indeed by choice."

===Chinese===

约翰·科佩兰答道："殿下，他此刻被妥善关押在班堡城堡（Bamborough Castle）中，殿下可随时提审。同时，臣恳请陛下勿怪臣未将戴维国王遵命交给王后，因为臣的封地受封于陛下，而非王后，臣的效忠誓言亦是向陛下宣的，而非向王后——除非出于臣自愿。"

===Original===

"John," the King sternly replied, "the loyal service you have done us is considerable, whereas your excuse for kidnapping Davie is a farce. Hey, Almerigo, do you and Chandos avoid the chamber! I have something in private with this fellow." When they had gone, the King sat down and composedly said, "Now tell me the truth, John Copeland."

"Sire," Copeland began, "it is necessary you first understand I bear a letter from Madame Philippa—"

"Then read it," said the King. "Heart of God! have I an eternity to waste on you slow-dealing Brabanters!"

===Chinese===

"约翰，"国王严厉地答道，"你对我们忠心耿耿，功绩不小，但你绑架戴维的辩解不过是一场闹剧。喂，阿尔梅里戈，你和尚多斯先出去！我有私事要和这家伙谈。"他们走后，国王坐下来，不慌不忙地说："现在把实情告诉我，约翰·科佩兰。"

"陛下，"科佩兰开口道，"臣以为陛下须先明白，臣身上带着菲利帕夫人的一封信——"

"那就念来听，"国王说。"天哪！难道我有无穷的时间来陪你们这些慢吞吞的布拉班特人耗着吗！"

===Original===

John Copeland read aloud, while the King trifled with a pen, half negligent, and in part attendant.

===Chinese===

约翰·科佩兰朗声读起来，国王漫不经心地摆弄着一支鹅毛笔，半是敷衍，半是倾听。

===Original===

Read John Copeland:

"My Dear Lord—I recommend me to your lordship with soul and body and all my poor might, and with all this I thank you, as my dear lord, dearest and best beloved of all earthly lords I protest to me, and thank you, my dear lord, with all this as I say before. Your comfortable letter came to me on Saint Gregory's day, and I was never so glad as when I heard by your letter that ye were strong enough in Ponthieu by the grace of God for to keep you from your enemies. Among them I estimate Madame Catherine de Salisbury, who would have betrayed you to the Scot. And, dear lord, if it be pleasing to your high lordship that as soon as ye may that I might hear of your gracious speed, which may God Almighty continue and increase, I shall be glad, and also if ye do continue each night to chafe your feet with a rag of woollen stuff, as your physician directed. And, my dear lord, if it like you for to know of my fare, John Copeland will acquaint you concerning the Bruce his capture, and the syrup he brings for our son Lord Edward's cough, and the great malice-workers in these shires which would have so despitefully wrought to you, and of the manner of taking it after each meal. I am lately informed that Madame Catherine is now at Stirling with Robert Stewart and has lost all her good looks through a fever. God is invariably gracious to His servants. Farewell, my dear lord, and may the Holy Trinity keep you from your adversaries and ever send me comfortable tidings of you. Written at York, in the Castle, on Saint Gregory's day last past, by your own poor

"Philippa.

"To my true lord."

===Chinese===

约翰·科佩兰读道：

"吾主大人——臣以灵魂、肉体与全部微薄之力向陛下的大人之位致敬，以此一切，臣感谢您，作为臣亲爱的大人，人间所有大人中最可敬、最可亲的，臣如此向您宣誓，并感谢您，吾主大人，如臣前述。陛下的慰藉之信于圣格列高利日（Saint Gregory's day）送达臣手，臣闻知陛下蒙上帝恩典在蓬蒂厄（Ponthieu）安然无恙，抵御敌人，其欣喜无以复加。臣以为其中当以凯瑟琳·德·索尔兹伯里夫人（Madame Catherine de Salisbury）为最险，她本欲将陛下出卖给苏格兰人。亲爱的主人，若能蒙陛下高恩垂允，一旦有机会便将陛下恩典的进展告知臣——愿全能的上帝使此恩典持续增长——臣将欣喜不已；若陛下每夜仍遵医嘱以粗毛呢布揉搓双脚，臣亦当欣慰。吾主大人，若陛下愿知臣之近况，约翰·科佩兰将禀告陛下布鲁斯被擒之事、他为我们儿子爱德华勋爵咳嗽带来的糖浆，以及此郡那些欲对陛下行恶的大阴谋家们的情况，以及每餐后服药之法。臣新近获悉凯瑟琳夫人现与罗伯特·斯图尔特（Robert Stewart）在斯特灵（Stirling），因一场热病已失去全部美貌。上帝对其仆从向来仁慈。别了，吾主大人，愿圣三位一体护佑陛下免遭敌人侵扰，并永远赐臣以陛下的佳音。写于约克城堡，上一个圣格列高利日，您卑微的

"菲利帕。

"致我真正的主人。"

===Original===

"H'm!" said the King; "and now give me the entire story."

John Copeland obeyed. I must tell you that early in the narrative King Edward arose and strode toward a window. "Catherine!" he said. He remained motionless while Master Copeland went on without any manifest emotion. When he had ended, King Edward said, "And where is Madame de Salisbury now?"

===Chinese===

"嗯！"国王说；"现在把事情的全部经过告诉我。"

约翰·科佩兰照办了。我须告诉你，在叙述过程中，爱德华国王早早便站起身来，大步走向一扇窗户。"凯瑟琳！"他说。他纹丝不动地站着，科佩兰大师继续讲述，脸上毫无表情。讲完后，爱德华国王说："那么索尔兹伯里夫人现在在哪里？"

===Original===

At this the Brabanter went mad. As a leopard springs he leaped upon the King, and grasping him by each shoulder, shook that monarch as one punishing a child.

"Now by the splendour of God—!" King Edward began, very terrible in his wrath. He saw that John Copeland held a dagger to his breast, and he shrugged. "Well, my man, you perceive I am defenceless."

"First you will hear me out," John Copeland said.

"It would appear," the King retorted, "that I have little choice."

===Chinese===

听到这话，这位布拉班特人疯了。他像一头猎豹般扑向国王，双手揪住他的双肩，像教训一个孩子那样猛摇这位君主。

"以上帝的光辉起誓——！"爱德华国王怒吼道，极为骇人。他看到约翰·科佩兰手持一把匕首抵在自己胸前，便耸了耸肩。"好嘛，我的好汉，你看我手无寸铁。"

"你须先听我说完，"约翰·科佩兰说。

"看来，"国王反驳道，"我别无选择。"

===Original===

At this time John Copeland began: "Sire, you are the mightiest monarch your race has known. England is yours, France is yours, conquered Scotland lies prostrate at your feet. Today there is no other man in all the world who possesses a tithe of your glory; yet twenty years ago Madame Philippa first beheld you and loved you, an outcast, an exiled, empty-pocketed prince. Twenty years ago the love of Madame Philippa, great Count William's daughter, got for you the armament with which England was regained. Twenty years ago but for Madame Philippa you had died naked in some ditch."

"Go on," the King said presently.

===Chinese===

约翰·科佩兰此时开口说道："陛下，您是您的血脉中所出的最强大的君主。英格兰是您的，法兰西是您的，被征服的苏格兰匍匐在您脚下。今日举世之中，没有任何一个人拥有您十分之一的荣耀；然而二十年前，菲利帕夫人初见您时，您不过是一个被驱逐、流亡在外、身无分文的落魄王子，她便爱上了您。二十年前，大威廉伯爵之女菲利帕夫人的爱，为你赢得了收复英格兰的军队。二十年前，若无菲利帕夫人，你已赤条条地死在了某条沟渠之中。"

"说下去，"国王过了一会儿说。

===Original===

"Afterward you took a fancy to reign in France. You learned then that we Brabanters are a frugal people: Madame Philippa was wealthy when she married you, and twenty years had quadrupled her private fortune. She gave you every penny of it that you might fit out this expedition; now her very crown is in pawn at Ghent. In fine, the love of Madame Philippa gave you France as lightly as one might bestow a toy upon a child who whined for it."

The King fiercely said, "Go on."

===Chinese===

"后来你突发奇想要去统治法兰西。那时你才知道我们布拉班特人是节俭的民族：菲利帕夫人嫁给你时身家丰厚，二十年间她的私产翻了四倍。她将每一分钱都给了你，好让你装备这次远征；如今她的王冠都典押在根特（Gent）了。总而言之，菲利帕夫人的爱将法兰西轻而易举地给了你，就像大人随手给一个哭闹的孩子一个玩具。"

国王厉声说道："接着说。"

===Original===

"Eh, sire, I intend to. You left England undefended that you might posture a little in the eyes of Europe. And meanwhile a woman preserves England, a woman gives you Scotland as a gift, and in return asks nothing—God have mercy on us!—save that you nightly chafe your feet with a bit of woollen. You hear of it—and inquire, 'Where is Madame de Salisbury?' Here beyond doubt is the cock of Aesop's fable," snarled John Copeland, "who unearthed a gem and grumbled that his diamond was not a grain of corn."

"You shall be hanged at dawn," the King replied. "Meanwhile spit out your venom."

===Chinese===

"嗯，陛下，臣正打算如此。你将英格兰弃之不顾，只为在欧洲人面前摆摆架子。与此同时一个女人保卫了英格兰，一个女人将苏格兰作为礼物送给你，而她所求的回报不过是——上帝怜悯我们！——让你每夜用一小块毛呢布揉揉脚。你听闻此事——却只问：'索尔兹伯里夫人在哪里？'毫无疑问，这就是伊索寓言里那只公鸡，"约翰·科佩兰咆哮道，"挖出了一颗宝石，却抱怨他的钻石不是一粒谷子。"

"天亮就将你绞死，"国王答道。"不过在这之前，把你的毒液吐完。"

===Original===

"I say to you, then," John Copeland continued, "that today you are master of Europe. I say to you that, but for this woman whom for twenty years you have neglected, you would today be mouldering in some pauper's grave. Eh, without question, you most magnanimously loved that shrew of Salisbury! because you fancied the colour of her eyes, Sire Edward, and admired the angle between her nose and her forehead. Minstrels unborn will sing of this great love of yours. Meantime I say to you"—now the man's rage was monstrous—"I say to you, go home to your too-tedious wife, the source of all your glory! sit at her feet! and let her teach you what love is!" He flung away the dagger. "There you have the truth. Now summon your attendants, my très beau sire, and have me hanged."

===Chinese===

"那么我告诉你，"约翰·科佩兰继续说道，"今日你是欧洲的主宰。我告诉你，若非这个你二十年来一直忽视的女人，你今天早已化为某个穷鬼坟墓中的朽骨。嗯，毫无疑问，你那宽宏大量的爱献给了索尔兹伯里的那个泼妇！因为你迷恋她眼睛的颜色，爱德华陛下，还欣赏她鼻梁和额头之间的角度。尚未出生的吟游诗人将会传唱你这伟大的爱情。而我现在告诉你"——此刻这人的愤怒已然滔天——"我告诉你，回到你那令人厌倦的妻子身边去吧，她是你一切荣耀的源泉！跪在她的脚下！让她教你什么叫爱！"他掷出了匕首。"真相便是如此。现在召你的侍从来吧，我英俊的陛下，让他们把我绞死。"

===Original===

The King made no movement. "You have been bold—" he said at last.

"But you have been far bolder, sire. For twenty years you have dared to flout that love which is God's noblest heritage to His children."

===Chinese===

国王纹丝不动。"你胆子不小——"他终于说道。

"但陛下胆子更大。二十年来，你竟敢藐视那爱——那是上帝赐予子女们最崇高的遗产。"

===Original===

King Edward sat in meditation for a long while. The squinting of his left eye was now very noticeable. "I consider my wife's clerk," he drily said, "to discourse of love in somewhat too much the tone of a lover." And a flush was his reward.

===Chinese===

爱德华国王沉思了很久。他左眼的斜视此刻格外明显。"我觉得，"他干巴巴地说，"我妻子的秘书谈起爱情来，语气未免太像一个情人了。"他的报偿是一阵脸红。

===Original===

But when this Copeland spoke he was like one transfigured. His voice was grave and very tender, and he said:

"As the fish have their life in the waters, so I have and always shall have mine in love. Love made me choose and dare to emulate a lady, long ago, through whom I live contented, without expecting any other good. Her purity is so inestimable that I cannot say whether I derive more pride or sorrow from its preeminence. She does not love me, and she will never love me. She would condemn me to be hewed in fragments sooner than permit her husband's finger to be injured. Yet she surpasses all others so utterly that I would rather hunger in her presence than enjoy from another all which a lover can devise."

===Chinese===

然而这位科佩兰开口时，仿佛变了一个人。他的声音深沉而极其温柔，他说道：

"鱼儿以水为生，我亦以爱为生，且永远如此。爱使我选择并敢于追随一位淑女，那已是很久以前的事了，我因她而心满意足地活着，不期望其他任何好处。她的纯洁如此不可估量，我不知从中得到的究竟是骄傲更多还是忧伤更多。她不爱我，也永远不会爱我。她宁可将我碎尸万段，也不愿她丈夫的一根手指受伤。然而她远超众人，我宁愿在她面前挨饿，也不愿从另一个人那里享受爱情所能给予的一切。"

===Original===

Sire Edward stroked the table through this while, with an inverted pen. He cleared his throat. He said, half-fretfully:

"Now, by the Face! it is not given every man to love precisely in this troubadourish fashion. Even the most generous person cannot render to love any more than that person happens to possess. I have read in an old tale how the devil sat upon a cathedral spire and white doves flew about him. Monks came and told him to begone. 'Do not the spires show you, O son of darkness' they clamoured, 'that the place is holy?' And Satan (in this old tale) replied that these spires were capable of various interpretations. I speak of symbols, John. Yet I also have loved, in my own fashion—and, it would seem, I win the same reward as you."

===Chinese===

爱德华陛下一边听一边用倒转的鹅毛笔抚弄桌面。他清了清嗓子，半带烦闷地说道：

"天哪！并非每个人都注定以这种吟游诗人的风格去爱。即便是最慷慨的人，能给予爱情的也不过是自己恰好拥有的东西。我在一个古老的故事里读到，魔鬼坐在一座大教堂的尖顶上，白鸽在他周围飞来飞去。僧侣们过来叫他滚开。'难道这些尖顶没有告诉你，黑暗之子啊，'他们喊道，'这地方是神圣的吗？'而撒旦（在这则古老的故事中）回答说，这些尖顶可以有各种不同的解释。我说的是象征，约翰。但我也曾以自己的方式爱过——而且看来，我得到了和你一样的报偿。"

===Original===
