"是命运将我引至此处，"阿兰断言，眼中透着征服一切的渴望。"命运待我不薄；我将向她祈祷，愿她一如既往。"但当凯瑟琳追问这祈祷是什么时，阿兰摇了摇他黄褐色的头。"您很快就会知道的，殿下，但不是现在。我回沙特尔诺夫处理些必要的事务；明日鸡鸣时分启程去米兰，投效维斯孔蒂家族（Visconti）。再会！"他策马扬鞭，在八月的金色阳光中离去，猎犬在周围嬉戏。狐尾系在他的帽上。特里斯坦·德·利奥尼斯（Tristran de Léonois）或许也曾如此骑行在沉没的康沃尔（Cornwall）——凯瑟琳目送他远去，如此想着，如此庄严而从容。她回到自己的房间，哼着一首关于春天那恋爱而欢乐时光的无意义小曲，那时万物欢腾、人人幸福——

"El tems amoreus plein de joie,

El tems où tote riens s'esgaie——"

却突然爆发出一阵痛哭。她想到，每天都有那么多女子降生，她们不是公主，因此不得不嫁给令人憎恶的国王。

===Original===

Dawn found her in the orchard. She was to remember that it was a cloudy morning, and that mist-tatters trailed from the more distant trees. In the slaty twilight the garden's verdure was lustreless, the grass and foliage were uniformly sombre save where dewdrops showed like beryls. Nowhere in the orchard was there absolute shadow, nowhere a vista unblurred; in the east, halfway between horizon and zenith, two belts of coppery light flared against the gray sky like embers swaddled by ashes. The birds were waking; there were occasional scurryings in treetops and outbursts of peevish twittering to attest as much; and presently came a singing, less musical than that of many a bird perhaps, but far more grateful to the girl who heard it, heart in mouth. A lute accompanied the song demurely.

Sang Alain:

"O Madam Destiny, omnipotent,

Be not too obdurate to us who pray

That this our transient grant of youth be spent

In laughter as befits a holiday,

From which the evening summons us away,

From which tomorrow wakens us to strife

And toil and grief and wisdom—and today

Grudge us not life!

O Madam Destiny, omnipotent,

Why need our elders trouble us at play?

We know that very soon we shall repent

The idle follies of our holiday,

And being old, shall be as wise as they:

But now we are not wise, and lute and fife

Plead sweetlier than axioms—so today

Grudge us not life!

O Madam Destiny, omnipotent,

You have given us youth—and must we cast away

The cup undrained and our one coin unspent

Because our elders' beards and hearts are gray?

They have forgotten that if we delay

Death claps us on the shoulder, and with knife

Or cord or fever flouts the prayer we pray—

'Grudge us not life!'

Madam, recall that in the sun we play

But for an hour, then have the worm for wife,

The tomb for habitation—and today

Grudge us not life!"

===Chinese===

黎明时分，她已在果园。她记得那是个阴沉的清晨，远处树梢拖着薄雾的残缕。灰蒙蒙的暮色中，花园的绿意毫无光彩，草木一律暗沉，唯有露珠如绿宝石般闪烁。果园里没有绝对的阴影，也没有清晰的远景；东方，地平线与天顶之间，两道铜色的光带如灰烬包裹的余烬，在灰蒙蒙的天空中燃烧。鸟儿正在醒来；树梢不时传来窸窣的响动和恼怒的啾鸣作为证明；接着传来一阵歌声，或许不如许多鸟儿那般悦耳，但对那颗悬着心倾听的少女来说，却远为动听。鲁特琴（lute）温顺地伴着歌声。

阿兰唱道：

"噢，命运夫人，全能者，

莫对我们祈祷者过于严酷，

莫让我们这短暂的青春年华

在假日应有的欢笑中虚度，

黄昏将我们从那里召回，

明日将我们从那里唤醒，

投入争斗、劳苦、忧伤与智慧——而今日

莫吝啬我们的生命！

噢，命运夫人，全能者，

何必让长辈在我们嬉戏时烦扰？

我们深知不久便会悔恨

假日中那些懒散的荒唐，

年老之后，也将如他们一般睿智：

但此刻我们并不睿智，鲁特琴与笛

比公理更动听地恳求——所以今日

莫吝啬我们的生命！

噢，命运夫人，全能者，

你赐予我们青春——难道我们须将

未饮尽的杯与未花尽的钱币弃掷，

只因长辈们的须发与心已灰白？

他们忘了若我们迟延

死神便拍我们的肩，以刀

或绳索或热病嘲弄我们的祈求——

'莫吝啬我们的生命！'

夫人，请记起我们在阳光下嬉戏

不过一个时辰，此后蛆虫为妻，

坟墓为居所——而今日

莫吝啬我们的生命！"

===Original===

Candor in these matters is best. Katharine scrambled into the crotch of the apple-tree. The dew pattered sharply about her, but the Princess was not in a mood to appraise discomfort.

"You came!" this harper said, transfigured; and then again, "You came!"

She breathed, "Yes."

So for a long time they stood looking at each other. She found adoration in his eyes and quailed before it; and in the man's mind not a grimy and mean incident of the past but marshalled to leer at his unworthiness: yet in that primitive garden the first man and woman, meeting, knew no sweeter terror.

===Chinese===

坦率在此事上最是上策。凯瑟琳爬上苹果树的枝杈。露珠噼啪地打在她身上，但公主此刻无心计较不适。

"您来了！"这位琴师说道，容光焕发；继而又说，"您来了！"

她轻声道："是的。"

于是他们久久伫立，彼此凝望。他在她眼中看到了倾慕，不禁退缩；而在男人心中，过往的每一件卑劣往事都排着队，嘲笑着他的不堪；但在那原始的花园里，初遇的男女不知比这更甜蜜的恐惧。

===Original===

It was by the minstrel that a familiar earth and the grating speech of earth were earlier regained. "The affair is of the suddenest," Alain observed, and he now swung the lute behind him. He indicated no intention of touching her, though he might easily have done so as he sat there exalted by the height of his horse. "A meteor arrives with more prelude. But Love is an arbitrary lord; desiring my heart, he has seized it, and accordingly I would now brave hell to come to you, and finding you there, would esteem hell a pleasure-garden. I have already made my prayer to Destiny that she concede me love. Now of God, our Father and Master, I entreat quick death if I am not to win you. For, God willing, I shall come to you again, even if in order to do this I have to split the world like a rotten orange."

"Madness! Oh, brave, sweet madness!" Katharine said. "You are a minstrel and I am a king's daughter."

"Is it madness? Why, then, I think sane persons are to be commiserated. And indeed I spy in all this some design. Across half the earth I came to you, led by a fox. Hey, God's face!" Alain swore; "the foxes which Samson, that old sinewy captain, loosed among the corn of heathenry kindled no disputation such as this fox has set afoot. That was an affair of standing corn and olives spoilt, a bushel or so of disaster; now poised kingdoms topple on the brink of ruin. There will be martial argument shortly if you bid me come again."

"I bid you come," said Katharine; and after they had stared at each other for a long while, he rode away in silence. It was through a dank and tear-flawed world that she stumbled conventward, while out of the east the sun came bathed in mists, a watery sun no brighter than a silver coin.

===Chinese===

是琴师先将这片熟悉的大地和那刺耳的人间言语重新唤回。"此事真是突如其来，"阿兰评论道，此时他将鲁特琴甩到身后。他端坐于高大的马背之上，虽然轻易就能触到她，却无意如此。"流星的降临也比这更有铺垫。但爱是专横的君主；它欲得我心，便攫住了它，因此我愿为你直赴地狱，而若在地狱找到你，便视地狱为乐土。我已向命运祈祷，求她赐我爱情。现在我向天主，我们的天父和主宰，恳求速死，若我不能赢得你。因为，天主若准许，我必再回到你身边，哪怕为此得将这世界劈成两半，如同劈一只腐烂的橙子。"

"疯了！哦，勇敢而甜美的疯狂！"凯瑟琳说。"您是个琴师，我是国王的女儿。"

"这是疯狂吗？那么，我以为理智之人才该被怜悯。而且我确实在其中窥见某种设计。我跨过半个世界来到你身边，由一只狐狸引领。嘿，天主的面容！"阿兰咒道，"参孙（Samson）那老筋肉队长在异教徒的谷物中放出的狐狸，引起的纷争也不及这只狐狸挑起的这番。那不过是毁了些谷物和橄榄，一两蒲式耳的灾祸；如今摇摇欲坠的王国在毁灭的边缘倾覆。若您命我再来，战事不久便会重燃。"

"我命您再来，"凯瑟琳说；他们彼此凝视良久之后，他默然策马离去。当她跌跌撞撞走向修道院时，穿过的是一个潮湿而涕泪纵横的世界，而东方的太阳浴在薄雾中升起，如同一枚银币般苍白无光。

===Original===

And for a month the world seemed no less dreary, but about Michaelmas the Queen-Regent sent for her. At the Hôtel de Saint-Pol matters were much the same. Katharine found her mother in foul-mouthed rage over the failure of a third attempt to poison the Dauphin of Vienne, as Queen Isabeau had previously poisoned her two elder sons; I might here trace out a curious similitude between the Valois and that dragon-spawned race which Jason very anciently slew at Colchis, since the world was never at peace so long as any two of them existed. But King Charles greeted his daughter with ampler deference, esteeming her to be the wife of Presbyter John, the tyrant of Aethiopia. However, ingenuity had just suggested card-playing for King Charles' amusement, and he paid little attention nowadays to anyone save his opponent at this new game.

So the French King chirped his senile jests over the card-table, while the King of England was besieging the French city of Rouen sedulously and without mercy. In late autumn an armament from Ireland joined Henry's forces. The Irish fought naked, it was said, with long knives. Katharine heard discreditable tales of these Irish, and reflected how gross are the exaggerations of rumor.

In the year of grace 1419, in January, the burgesses of Rouen, having consumed their horses, and finding frogs and rats unpalatable, yielded the town. It was the Queen-Regent who brought the news to Katharine.

"God is asleep," the Queen said; "and while He nods, the Butcher of Agincourt has stolen our good city of Rouen." She sat down and breathed heavily. "Never was any poor woman so pestered as I! The puddings today were quite uneatable, as you saw for yourself, and on Sunday the Englishman entered Rouen in great splendor, attended by his chief nobles; but the Butcher rode alone, and before him went a page carrying a fox-brush on the point of his lance. I put it to you, is that the contrivance of a sane man? Euh! euh!" Dame Isabeau squealed on a sudden; "you are bruising me."

===Chinese===

此后一月，世界似乎同样阴郁，但米迦勒节（Michaelmas）前后，摄政王后遣人来召她。在圣波尔宫（Hôtel de Saint-Pol），情形大同小异。凯瑟琳发现母亲正为第三次毒杀维埃纳（Vienne）王太子（Dauphin of Vienne）的企图失败而暴跳如雷，此前伊莎博王后已毒杀了她另外两个儿子；我在此或可追溯瓦卢瓦家族与伊阿宋（Jason）古老时在科尔喀斯（Colchis）所屠之龙裔之间惊人的相似，只要他们中有任何两个并存，世界便永无宁日。但查理国王（King Charles）以更隆重的礼节迎接女儿，视她为祭司王约翰（Presbyter John）——埃塞俄比亚（Aethiopia）的暴君——之妻。不过，有人刚为查理国王发明了打牌消遣，他如今除了牌桌上的对手，对谁都不大理会。

于是法国国王在牌桌旁咿呀讲着老迈的笑话，而英格兰国王正在围困法国城市鲁昂，不遗余力、毫不留情。深秋时节，一支来自爱尔兰的军队加入了亨利的部队。据说爱尔兰人赤身裸体作战，手持长刀。凯瑟琳听到了关于这些爱尔兰人的不光彩传闻，感叹谣言的夸张何等粗鄙。

公元一四一九年一月，鲁昂市民在吃光了马匹、发现青蛙和老鼠难以下咽后，献城投降。是摄政王后将这消息带给凯瑟琳的。

"天主打瞌睡了，"王后说，"趁他打盹，阿金库尔的屠夫（Butcher of Agincourt）偷走了我们美好的鲁昂城。"她坐下，重重喘着气。"从没有哪个可怜的女人像我这样被烦扰！今日的布丁简直难以下咽，您亲眼看到了；星期天，英格兰人浩浩荡荡地进入鲁昂，由他们的贵族首领簇拥；但那屠夫独自骑行，前方一名侍从在长矛尖上挑着一支狐尾。我倒要问您，这是神智正常的人想出来的主意吗？哎！哎！"伊莎博夫人（Dame Isabeau）突然尖叫起来，"您弄疼我了。"

===Original===

Katharine had gripped her by the shoulder. "The King of England—a tall, fair man? with big teeth? a tiny wen upon his neck—here—and with his left cheek scarred? with blue eyes, very bright, bright as tapers?" She poured out her questions in a torrent, and awaited the answer, seeming not to breathe at all.

"I believe so," the Queen said, "and they say, too, that he has the damned squint of old Manuel the Redeemer."

"O God!" said Katharine.

"Ay, our only hope now. And may God show him no more mercy than has this misbegotten English butcher shown us!" the good lady desired, with fervor. "The hog, having won our Normandy, is now advancing on Paris itself. He repudiated the Aragonish alliance last August; and until last August he was content with Normandy, they tell us, but now he swears to win all France. The man is a madman, and Scythian Tamburlaine was more lenient. And I do not believe that in all France there is a cook who understands his business." She went away whimpering, and proceeded to get tipsy.

===Chinese===

凯瑟琳抓住了她的肩膀。"英格兰国王——一个高大、金发的男人？牙齿很大？脖子上——这里——有一颗小肉瘤？左脸颊有疤痕？蓝眼睛，非常明亮，亮如烛火？"她连珠炮似地抛出问题，屏息等待答案。

"我想是的，"王后说，"而且他们还说，他有老救世主曼努埃尔（Manuel the Redeemer）那该死的斜眼。"

"哦，天主！"凯瑟琳说。

"唉，他现在是我们唯一的希望。愿天主对他毫无怜悯，正如这个杂种英格兰屠夫对我们毫无怜悯！"这位好夫人热切地说道。"这头猪猡赢了我们的诺曼底（Normandy），如今正向巴黎本身进发。去年八月他断绝了与阿拉贡的联盟；直到去年八月，他还满足于诺曼底，他们告诉我们，但现在他发誓要征服整个法国。这人是个疯子，斯基泰人（Scythian）的帖木儿（Tamburlaine）也比他宽宏。我就不信全法国有一位厨师懂得自己的本行。"她呜咽着走了，接着便去喝得醉醺醺。

===Original===

The Princess remained quite still, as Dame Isabeau had left her; you may see a hare crouch so at sight of the hounds. Finally the girl spoke aloud. "Until last August!" Katharine said. "Until last August! 'Poised kingdoms topple on the brink of ruin, now that you bid me come to you again.' And I bade this devil's grandson come to me, as my lover!" Presently she went into her oratory and began to pray.

In the midst of her invocation she wailed: "Fool, fool! How could I have thought him less than a king!"

You are to imagine her breast thus adrum with remorse and hatred of herself, the while that town by town fell before the invader like card-houses. Every rumor of defeat—and the news of some fresh defeat came daily—was her arraignment; impotently she cowered at God's knees, knowing herself a murderess, whose infamy was still afoot, outpacing her prayers, whose victims were battalions. Tarpeia and Pisidicé and Rahab were her sisters; she hungered in her abasement for Judith's nobler guilt.

===Chinese===

公主一动不动地留在原地，如伊莎博夫人离开她时那样；你或许见过野兔在猎犬面前这般蜷伏。终于，少女大声说道："直到去年八月！"凯瑟琳说。"直到去年八月！'摇摇欲坠的王国在毁灭的边缘倾覆，既然你命我再来。'而我却命这魔鬼的孙子来见我，做我的情人！"不久她走进祈祷室，开始祈祷。

在祈祷中，她哀号道："傻瓜，傻瓜！我怎能认为他不如一个国王！"

你尽可想象，她胸中充满了懊悔和自厌，与此同时，城镇在入侵者面前如纸牌屋般一座座陷落。每一次败绩的传闻——而每日都有新的败绩消息传来——都是对她的控诉；她无力地蜷伏在天主的膝头，自知是个凶手，她的罪孽仍在蔓延，超越了她的祈祷，她的受害者是成群结队的。塔尔佩娅（Tarpeia）、皮西狄克（Pisidicé）和喇合（Rahab）是她的姐妹；她在卑屈中渴望着朱迪丝（Judith）那更高贵的罪孽。

===Original===

In May he came to her. A truce was patched up, and French and English met amicably in a great plain near Meulan. A square space was staked out and on three sides boarded in, the fourth side being the river Seine. This enclosure the Queen-Regent, Jehan of Burgundy, and Katharine entered from the French side. Simultaneously the English King appeared, accompanied by his brothers the Dukes of Clarence and Gloucester, and followed by the Earl of Warwick. Katharine raised her eyes with I know not what lingering hope; but it was he, a young Zeus now, triumphant and uneager. In his helmet in place of a plume he wore a fox-brush spangled with jewels.

These six entered the tent pitched for the conference—the hanging of blue velvet embroidered with fleurs-de-lys of gold blurred before the girl's eyes—and there the Earl of Warwick embarked upon a sea of rhetoric. His French was indifferent, his periods were interminable, and his demands exorbitant; in brief, the King of England wanted Katharine and most of France, with a reversion at the French King's death of the entire kingdom. Meanwhile Sire Henry sat in silence, his eyes glowing.

===Chinese===

五月，他来到她身边。双方达成停战协议，法国人和英格兰人在默兰（Meulan）附近的大平原上友好会晤。一片方形场地被标出，三面围板，第四面是塞纳河（Seine）。摄政王后、勃艮第的让（Jehan of Burgundy）和凯瑟琳从法国一侧进入这片围场。与此同时，英格兰国王现身，由他的兄弟们——克拉伦斯公爵（Duke of Clarence）和格洛斯特公爵（Duke of Gloucester）陪同，身后跟着沃里克伯爵（Earl of Warwick）。凯瑟琳抬起目光，带着我不知什么样的依稀希望；但那正是他，如今如年轻的宙斯（Zeus），凯旋而意犹未尽。他的头盔上，本该插羽翎的地方，插着一支点缀珠宝的狐尾。

这六人进入为会议搭起的帐篷——那绣着金百合花（fleurs-de-lys）的蓝丝绒帷幔在少女眼前模糊了——沃里克伯爵在那里展开了长篇大论。他的法语平平，辞藻冗长，要求苛刻；简言之，英格兰国王想要凯瑟琳和大半个法国，外加法国国王死后整个王国的继承权。而亨利陛下（Sire Henry）则静坐一旁，目光炯炯。

===Original===

"I have come," he said, under cover of Warwick's oratory—"I have come again, my lady."

Katharine's gaze flickered over him. "Liar!" she said, very softly. "Has God no thunders remaining in His armory that this vile thief still goes unblasted? Would you steal love as well as kingdoms?"

His ruddy face was now white. "I love you, Katharine."

"Yes," she answered, "for I am your pretext. I can well believe, messire, that you love your pretext for theft and murder."

Neither spoke after this, and presently the Earl of Warwick having come to his peroration, the matter was adjourned till the next day. The party separated. It was not long before Katharine had informed her mother that, God willing, she would never again look upon the King of England's face uncoffined. Isabeau found her a madwoman. The girl swept opposition before her with gusts of demoniacal fury, wept, shrieked, tore at her hair, and eventually fell into a sort of epileptic seizure; between rage and terror she became a horrid, frenzied beast. I do not dwell upon this, for it is not a condition in which the comeliest maid shows to advantage. But, for the Valois, insanity always lurked at the next corner, and they knew it; to save the girl's reason the Queen was forced to break off all discussion of the match. Accordingly, the Duke of Burgundy went next day to the conference alone. Jehan began with "ifs," and over these flimsy barriers Henry, already fretted by Katharine's scorn, presently vaulted to a towering fury.

===Chinese===

"我来了，"他说，声音淹没在沃里克的雄辩之下——"我再次来到你身边，我的女士。"

凯瑟琳的目光在他身上游移。"骗子！"她轻声说道。"天主的武库中难道没有雷霆了吗，以致这卑鄙的窃贼仍未遭天谴？您不仅要窃取王国，还要窃取爱情吗？"

他红润的脸如今变得苍白。"我爱你，凯瑟琳。"

"是的，"她答道，"因为我是您的借口。我完全相信，先生，您爱的是您盗窃和谋杀的借口。"

此后双方皆不言语，不久沃里克伯爵讲完结语，此事被推迟到次日再议。众人散去。不久，凯瑟琳告知母亲，天主若准许，她将永不再见英格兰国王的容颜，除非在棺中。伊莎博视她为疯子。少女以恶魔般的狂怒横扫一切反对，哭泣、尖叫、扯头发，最终陷入某种癫痫般的发作；在暴怒与惊恐之间，她成了一头可怖而狂乱的野兽。我不愿详述，因为这不是最美丽的少女展现优势的状态。但对于瓦卢瓦家族而言，疯狂总是潜伏在下一个转角，他们心知肚明；为了挽救少女的神智，王后被迫中止了所有关于这门婚事的讨论。于是，次日勃艮第公爵独自前往会晤。让以"若"字开头，而亨利在这些脆弱的障碍上，早已被凯瑟琳的蔑视激怒，不久便勃然大怒。

===Original===

"Fair cousin," the King said, after a deal of vehement bickering, "we wish you to know that we will have the daughter of your King, and that we will drive both him and you out of this kingdom."

The Duke answered, not without spirit, "Sire, you are pleased to say so; but before you have succeeded in ousting my lord and me from this realm, I am of the opinion that you will be very heartily tired."

At this the King turned on his heel; over his shoulder he flung: "I am tireless; also, I am agile as a fox in the pursuit of my desires. Say that to your Princess." Then he went away in a rage.

It had seemed an approvable business to win love incognito, according to the example of many ancient emperors, but in practice he had tripped over an ugly outgrowth from the legendary custom. The girl hated him, there was no doubt about it; and it was equally certain he loved her. Particularly caustic was the reflection that a twitch of his finger would get him Katharine as his wife, for before long the Queen-Regent was again attempting secret negotiations to bring this about. Yes, he could get the girl's body by a couple of pen-strokes, and had he been older that might have contented him: as it was, what he wanted was to rouse the look her eyes had borne in Chartres orchard that tranquil morning, and this one could not readily secure by fiddling with seals and parchments. You see his position: this high-spirited young man now loved the Princess too utterly to take her on lip-consent, and this marriage was now his one possible excuse for ceasing from victorious warfare. So he blustered, and the fighting recommenced; and he slew in a despairing rage, knowing that by every movement of his arm he became to her so much the more detestable.

Then the Vicomte de Montbrison, as you have heard, betrayed France, and King Henry began to strip the French realm of provinces as you peel the layers from an onion. By the May of the year of grace 1420 France was, and knew herself to be, not beaten but demolished. Only a fag-end of the French army lay entrenched at Troyes, where King Charles and his court awaited Henry's decision as to the morrow's action. If he chose to destroy them root and branch, he could; and they knew such mercy as was in the man to be quite untarnished by previous using. Sire Henry drew up a small force before the city and made no overtures toward either peace or throat-cutting.

===Chinese===

"堂弟，"国王在一番激烈的争吵后说道，"我们要你知道，我们要娶你们国王的女儿，并且要把他和你都赶出这个王国。"

公爵不甘示弱地答道："陛下，您乐意这么说；但依我之见，在您成功将我们赶出这片领土之前，您会非常厌倦。"

闻此，国王转身就走；他回头抛下一句："我不知疲倦；而且，我追逐所欲之物时，敏捷如狐。把这话告诉您的公主。"说罢他怒气冲冲地走了。

以匿名方式赢得爱情，效仿众多古皇帝的先例，本是个值得称道的做法，但在实践中，他被这传奇习俗的丑陋衍生物绊倒了。少女恨他，这一点毫无疑问；同样确定的是，他爱她。尤其令人苦涩的反思是，他只需动动手指就能娶凯瑟琳为妻，因为不久后摄政王后又在试图秘密谈判促成此事。是的，他只需签几笔就能得到这少女的身子，若他年长些，或许这已足够满足他；但如今，他想要的是唤起那日清晨她在沙特尔果园中的神情，而这无法靠摆弄印章和羊皮纸轻易获得。你看出他的处境：这位意气风发的青年如今过于爱慕公主，无法仅凭唇上应允便娶她，而这场婚姻如今是他停止胜战的唯一可能的借口。于是他虚张声势，战事重燃；他在绝望的暴怒中杀戮，深知自己每一举臂，在她眼中便更添一分可憎。

随后，如你所闻，蒙布里松子爵（Vicomte de Montbrison）背叛了法国，亨利国王开始像剥洋葱一样层层剥去法国的行省。公元一四二〇年五月，法国已沦为，也自知沦为，不被击败而是被摧毁。只有法国军队的残部驻守在特鲁瓦（Troyes），查理国王和他的宫廷在那里等待亨利决定次日的行动。若他选择斩草除根，他可以做到；而他们深知此人的仁慈此前未曾动用，因而毫发无损。亨利陛下（Sire Henry）在城前列出一小队人马，对和平或屠杀均无表示。

===Original===

This was the posture of affairs on the evening of the Sunday after Ascension day, when Katharine sat at cards with her father in his apartments at the Hôtel de Ville. The King was pursing his lips over an alternative play, when somebody began singing below in the courtyard.

Sang the voice:

"I can find no meaning in life,

That have weighed the world—and it was

Abundant with folly, and rife

With sorrows brittle as glass,

And with joys that flicker and pass

Like dreams through a fevered head;

And like the dripping of rain

In gardens naked and dead

Is the obdurate thin refrain

Of our youth which is presently dead.

"And she whom alone I have loved

Looks ever with loathing on me,

As one she hath seen disproved

And stained with such smirches as be

Not ever cleansed utterly;

And is both to remember the days

When Destiny fixed her name

As the theme and the goal of my praise;

And my love engenders shame,

And I stain what I strive for and praise.

"O love, most perfect of all,

Just to have known you is well!

And it heartens me now to recall

That just to have known you is well,

And naught else is desirable

Save only to do as you willed

And to love you my whole life long;—

But this heart in me is filled

With hunger cruel and strong,

And with hunger unfulfilled.

"Fond heart, though thy hunger be

As a flame that wanders unstilled,

There is none more perfect than she!"

===Chinese===

这是圣体瞻礼节（Ascension day）后那个星期日晚间的情势，凯瑟琳正与父亲在市政厅（Hôtel de Ville）他的房间里打牌。国王正为一步棋犹豫不决，突然有人在下方的庭院里唱起歌来。

歌声唱道：

"我在生命中寻不到意义，

我已掂量过这世界——它满是

愚蠢，充斥着

脆弱如玻璃的悲伤，

还有闪烁而逝的欢愉，

如发烧者头中的幻梦；

而赤裸死寂的花园中

雨滴淅沥，

恰如我们行将消逝的青春

那执拗而细微的副歌。

"我唯一爱过的女子

总以厌恶的目光看我，

如同看一个被揭穿、

被无法彻底洗刷的污点

玷染之人；

她既记得那些岁月——

当命运定下她的名字

作为我赞美的主题与目标；

我的爱却催生羞耻，

我玷污了我所追求与赞美的。

"哦，爱，万美之冠，

认识你便已足够！

此刻我忆起此言，心中振奋：

认识你便已足够，

此外别无所求，

唯愿如你所愿行事，

终生爱你；——

但此心充盈着

残酷而强烈的饥渴，

以及未被满足的饥渴。

"痴心啊，纵使你的饥渴

如游移不止的火焰，

世间也无人比她更完美！"

===Original===

Malise now came into the room, and, without speaking, laid a fox-brush before the Princess.

Katharine twirled it in her hand, staring at the card-littered table. "So you are in his pay, Malise? I am sorry. But you know that your employer is master here. Who am I to forbid him entrance?" The girl went away silently, abashed, and the Princess sat quite still, tapping the brush against the table.

"They do not want me to sign another treaty, do they?" her father asked timidly. "It appears to me they are always signing treaties, and I cannot see that any good comes of it. And I would have won the last game, Katharine, if Malise had not interrupted us. You know I would have won."

"Yes, Father, you would have won. Oh, he must not see you!" Katharine cried, a great tide of love mounting in her breast, the love that draws a mother fiercely to shield her backward boy. "Father, will you not go into your chamber? I have a new book for you, Father—all pictures, dear. Come—" She was coaxing him when Sire Henry appeared in the doorway.

"But I do not wish to look at pictures," Charles said, peevishly; "I wish to play cards. You are an ungrateful daughter, Katharine. You are never willing to amuse me." He sat down with a whimper and began to pluck at his dribbling lips.

Katharine had moved a little toward the door. Her face was white. "Now welcome, sire!" she said. "Welcome, O great conqueror, who in your hour of triumph can find no nobler recreation than to shame a maid with her past folly! It was valorously done, sire. See, Father; here is the King of England come to observe how low we sit that yesterday were lords of France."

===Chinese===

此时马利斯（Malise）走进房间，不发一言，将一支狐尾放在公主面前。

凯瑟琳在手中转动着它，凝视着散落纸牌的桌子。"那么您受雇于他，马利斯？我很遗憾。但您知道您的主人在此地是主宰。我算什么，竟敢禁止他入内？"少女默默走开，满面羞惭，公主则静静地坐着，用狐尾轻叩桌面。

"他们不是要我再签一份条约吧？"她父亲怯生生地问道。"在我看来，他们总在签约，我看不出有什么好处。要不是马利斯打扰我们，我本可以赢下那盘棋的，凯瑟琳。您知道我本可以赢的。"

"是的，父亲，您本可以赢。哦，他不能见您！"凯瑟琳喊道，心中涌起一股强烈的爱意，那爱如母亲护佑怯懦的幼子般猛烈。"父亲，您不想回房间去吗？我有本新书给您，父亲——全是图画，亲爱的。来——"她正哄着他，亨利陛下（Sire Henry）出现在门口。

"但我不想看图画，"查理任性地说，"我想打牌。你是个忘恩负义的女儿，凯瑟琳。你从来不愿逗我开心。"他呜咽着坐下，开始揪扯流涎的嘴唇。

凯瑟琳已向门口挪了几步。她面色苍白。"那么，欢迎您，陛下！"她说。"欢迎您，伟大的征服者，在您凯旋的时刻，竟寻不到更高尚的消遣，只能以女子往日的愚蠢羞辱她！您做得真英勇，陛下。看，父亲；英格兰国王来了，看看我们昨日身为法国的主人，今日却沦落到何等境地。"

===Original===

"The King of England!" echoed Charles, and he rose now to his feet. "I thought we were at war with him. But my memory is treacherous. You perceive, brother of England, I am planning a new mousetrap, and my mind is somewhat preempted. I recall now that you are in treaty for my daughter's hand. Katharine is a good girl, a fine upstanding girl, but I suppose—" He paused, as if to regard and hear some invisible counsellor, and then briskly resumed: "Yes, I suppose policy demands that she should marry you. We trammelled kings can never go free of policy—ey, my compère of England? No; it was through policy I wedded her mother; and we have been very unhappy, Isabeau and I. A word in your ear, son-in-law: Madame Isabeau's soul formerly inhabited a sow, as Pythagoras teaches, and when our Saviour cast it out at Gadara, the influence of the moon drew it hither."

Henry did not say anything. Steadily his calm blue eyes appraised Dame Katharine. And King Charles went on, very knowingly:

"Oho, these Latinists cannot hoodwink me, you observe, though by ordinary it chimes with my humor to appear content. Policy again, son-in-law: for once roused, I am terrible. Today in the great hall-window, under the bleeding feet of Lazarus, I slew ten flies—very black they were, the black shrivelled souls of parricides—and afterward I wept for it. I often weep; the Mediterranean hath its sources in my eyes, for my daughter cheats at cards. Cheats, sir!—and I her father!" The incessant peering, the stealthy cunning with which Charles whispered this, the confidence with which he clung to his destroyer's hand, was that of a conspiring child.

"Come, Father," Katharine said. "Come away to bed, dear."

"Hideous basilisk!" he spat at her; "dare you rebel against me? Am I not King of France, and is it not blasphemy for a King of France to be mocked? Frail moths that flutter about my splendor," he shrieked, in an unheralded frenzy, "beware of me, beware! for I am omnipotent! I am King of France, Heaven's regent. At my command the winds go about the earth, and nightly the stars are kindled for my recreation. Perhaps I am mightier than God, but I do not remember now. The reason is written down and lies somewhere under a bench. Now I sail for England. Eia! eua! I go to ravage England, terrible and merciless. But I must have my mousetraps, Goodman Devil, for in England the cats of the middle-sea wait unfed." He went out of the room, giggling, and in the corridor began to sing:

"A hundred thousand times goodbye!

I go to seek the Evangelist,

For here all persons cheat and lie..."

===Chinese===

"英格兰国王！"查理应道，此刻站起身来。"我以为我们正与他交战。但我的记忆靠不住。您瞧，英格兰兄弟，我正在设计一个新捕鼠器，我的心思被占了些。我记起来了，您正在洽谈娶我女儿的事。凯瑟琳是个好姑娘，一个挺拔的好姑娘，但我想——"他顿住，仿佛在倾听某个无形的谋士，接着轻快地继续道："是的，我想政策要求她嫁给您。我们这些被束缚的国王永远无法摆脱政策——咦，我的英格兰伙计？不；正是出于政策，我娶了她的母亲；而我们过得很不幸福，伊莎博和我。附耳过来，女婿：伊莎博夫人（Madame Isabeau）的灵魂从前寄居在一头母猪身上，正如毕达哥拉斯（Pythagoras）所教导的，当我们的救主在迦达拉（Gadara）将其驱出时，月亮的引力把它引到了这里。"

亨利什么也没说。他平静的蓝眼睛稳稳地打量着凯瑟琳夫人（Dame Katharine）。查理国王非常精明地继续说：

"哦，这些拉丁学者骗不了我，您瞧，尽管我通常乐于装出心满意足的样子。政策，女婿：一旦被激怒，我便可怕。今日在大厅的窗下，在拉撒路（Lazarus）流血的双脚下，我杀了十只苍蝇——非常黑，是弑亲者枯黑的灵魂——之后我为之哭泣。我常哭泣；地中海的源头就在我的眼中，因为我的女儿打牌作弊。作弊，先生！——而我是她的父亲！"查理不断窥视着，狡黠地低语，紧握着他毁灭者的手，那是一个阴谋家的孩子般的模样。

"来，父亲，"凯瑟琳说。"来，上床睡觉，亲爱的。"

"丑陋的蛇蝎！"他唾弃道，"你胆敢反抗我？我不是法国国王吗？对法国国王不敬岂非亵渎？扑向我光辉的飞蛾们，"他突然无缘无故地尖叫道，"当心，当心！因为我无所不能！我是法国国王，上天的摄政。我一声令下，风遍行大地，星辰每夜为我的消遣而点燃。或许我比天主更强大，但我不记得了。原因被写下来，放在某张长凳底下。现在我要航往英格兰。咿呀！咿呀！我要去蹂躏英格兰，可怕而无情。但我需要我的捕鼠器，好魔鬼（Goodman Devil），因为在英格兰，地中海的猫儿正等着喂食呢。"他咯咯笑着走出房间，在走廊里唱起歌来：

"千万次告别！

我要去寻找福音书作者，

因为这里人人作弊撒谎……"


===Original===

All this while Henry remained immovable, his eyes fixed upon Katharine. Thus (she meditated) he stood among Frenchmen; he was the boulder, and they the waters that babbled and fretted about him. But she turned and met his gaze squarely. She noted now for the first time how oddly his left eyebrow drooped. Katharine said: "And that is the king whom you have conquered! Is it not a notable conquest to overcome so wise a king? to pilfer renown from an idiot? There are cutthroats in Troyes, rogues doubly damned, who would scorn the action. Now shall I fetch my mother, sire? the commander of that great army which you overcame? As the hour is late, she is by this time tipsy, but she will come. Or perhaps she is with some paid lover, but if this conqueror, this second Alexander, wills it she will come. O God!" the girl wailed, on a sudden; "O just and all-seeing God! are not we of Valois so contemptible that in conquering us it is the victor who is shamed?"

===Chinese===

在此期间，亨利一动不动，目光锁定凯瑟琳。她沉思着，他就是这样站在法国人中间；他是岩石，而他们是围绕他潺潺流淌的溪水。但她转身直面他的目光。她此刻初次注意到，他左眉古怪地低垂着。凯瑟琳说道："这就是你们所征服的国王！征服如此睿智的国王，岂非了不起的成就？从一个白痴那里窃取声名？特鲁瓦的凶手们，那些双重诅咒的恶棍，也会鄙视这种行为。现在，陛下，我要去请我母亲吗？那支被您征服的大军的统帅？时候不早了，她这会儿已喝得醉醺醺，但她会来。或者她正与某个花钱买来的情人在一起，但只要这征服者，这第二个亚历山大（Alexander）愿意，她就会来。哦，天主！"少女突然哀号道，"哦，公正而全知的天主！我们瓦卢瓦家族难道如此可鄙，以致征服我们的人反受羞辱吗？"

===Original===

"Flower of the marsh!" he said, and his voice pulsed with tender cadences—"flower of the marsh! it is not the King of England who now comes to you, but Alain the harper. Henry Plantagenet God has led hither by the hand to punish the sins of this realm, and to reign in it like a true king. Henry Plantagenet will cast out the Valois from the throne they have defiled, as Darius cast out Belshazzar, for such is the desire and the intent of God. But to you comes Alain the harper, not as a conqueror but as a suppliant—Alain who has loved you wholeheartedly these two years past, and who now kneels before you entreating grace."

Katharine looked down into his countenance, for to his speech he had fitted action. Suddenly and for the first time she understood that he believed France to be his by Divine favor and Heaven's peculiar intervention. He thought himself God's factor, not His rebel. He was rather stupid, this huge, handsome, squinting boy; and as she comprehended this, her hand went to his shoulder, half maternally.

"It is nobly done, sire. But I understand. You must marry me in order to uphold your claim to France. You sell, and I with my body purchase, peace for France. There is no need of a lover's posture when hucksters meet."

===Chinese===

"沼泽之花！"他说，声音里透着温柔的韵律——"沼泽之花！此刻来到你身边的不是英格兰国王，而是琴师阿兰。亨利·金雀花（Henry Plantagenet）被天主引领至此，惩罚这王国的罪孽，并如真正的国王般统治此地。亨利·金雀花将把瓦卢瓦家族从他们玷污的王座上驱逐出去，正如大流士（Darius）驱逐伯沙撒（Belshazzar），因为这是天主的意愿和旨意。但来到你身边的是琴师阿兰，不是作为征服者，而是作为恳求者——阿兰这两年来全心全意爱着你，此刻跪在你面前恳求恩典。"

凯瑟琳俯视着他的面容，因为他的言语伴随着动作。她骤然初次领悟，他相信法国是蒙天主恩典和天国特殊干预而属于他的。他视自己为天主的代理人，而非叛逆者。这个魁梧、英俊、斜眼的男孩，相当愚蠢；当她领悟这一点时，她的手半是慈母般地搭上了他的肩。

"您做得很高尚，陛下。但我明白了。您必须娶我以维护您对法国的宣称。您出售，而我以我的身体购买，法国的和平。商贩会面时，无需摆出情人的姿态。"

===Original===

"So changed!" he said, and he was silent for an interval, still kneeling. Then he began: "You force me to point out that I do not need any pretext for holding France. France lies before me prostrate. By God's singular grace I reign in this fair kingdom, mine by right of conquest, and an alliance with the house of Valois will neither make nor mar me." She was unable to deny this, unpalatable as was the fact. "But I love you, and therefore as man wooes woman I sue to you. Do you not understand that there can be between us no question of expediency? Katharine, in Chartres orchard there met a man and a maid we know of; now in Troyes they meet again—not as princess and king, but as man and maid, the wooer and the wooed. Once I touched your heart, I think. And now in all the world there is one thing I covet—to gain for the poor king some portion of that love you would have squandered on the harper." His hand closed upon her hand.

At his touch the girl's composure vanished. "My lord, you woo too timidly for one who comes with many loud-voiced advocates. I am daughter to the King of France, and next to my soul's salvation I esteem the welfare of France. Can I, then, fail to love the King of England, who chooses the blood of my countrymen as a judicious garb to come a-wooing in? How else, since you have ravaged my native land, since you have besmirched the name I bear, since yonder afield every wound in my dead and yet unburied Frenchmen is to me a mouth which shrieks your infamy?"

He rose. "And yet, for all that, you love me."

She could not at the first effort find words with which to answer him, but presently she said, quite simply, "To see you lying in your coffin I would willingly give up my hope of heaven, for heaven can afford no sight more desirable."

"You loved Alain."

"I loved the husk of a man. You can never comprehend how utterly I loved him."

===Chinese===

"变化如此之大！"他说，沉默片刻，仍跪着。然后他开口道："你迫使我指出，我无需任何借口来占据法国。法国在我面前俯首帖耳。蒙天主特殊恩典，我在此美丽的王国为王，以征服权属于我，与瓦卢瓦家族结盟既不能成就也不能损害我。"她无法否认这一点，尽管事实令人不快。"但我爱你，因此我以男人追求女人的方式向你求婚。你不明白我们之间不存在任何利害关系吗？凯瑟琳，在沙特尔果园里，我们所知的一男一女相遇了；如今在特鲁瓦，他们再次相遇——不是作为公主与国王，而是作为男人与女人，追求者与被追求者。我曾触及你的心，我想。如今全世界我只渴求一事——为这可怜的国王赢得你本要挥霍在琴师身上的那份爱。"他的手握住了她的手。

在他触碰的瞬间，少女的镇定消失了。"我的大人，您求爱得太怯懦了，您身后跟着那么多大声疾呼的拥护者。我是法兰西国王的女儿，仅次于灵魂的救赎，我最看重法兰西的福祉。那么，我能不爱英格兰国王吗？他选择以我同胞的鲜血作为求爱的得体伪装？还能怎样呢，既然您蹂躏了我的故土，既然您玷污了我所承之名，既然远方旷野上，我那死去而尚未掩埋的法兰西人身上的每一道伤口，都朝我尖叫着您的恶名？"

他站起身。"然而，即便如此，你还是爱我。"

她一时语塞，但随即相当平静地说："看着您躺在棺材里，我愿放弃对天国的期望，因为天国无法呈现更可期的景象。"

"你爱过阿兰。"

"我爱的是一个人的躯壳。您永远无法理解我曾多么彻底地爱过他。"

===Original===

"You are stubborn. I shall have trouble with you. But this notion of yours is plainly a mistaken notion. That you love me is indisputable, and this I propose to demonstrate. You will observe that I am quite unarmed except for this dagger, which I now throw out of the window—" with the word it jangled in the courtyard below. "I am in Troyes alone among some thousand Frenchmen, any one of whom would willingly give his life for the privilege of taking mine. You have but to sound the gong beside you, and in a few moments I shall be a dead man. Strike, then! For with me dies the English power in France. Strike, Katharine! If you see in me but the King of England."

She was rigid; and his heart leapt when he saw it was because of terror.

"You came alone! You dared!"

He answered, with a wonderful smile, "Proud spirit! How else might I conquer you?"

"You have not conquered!" Katharine lifted the baton beside the gong, poising it. God had granted her prayer—to save France. Now the past and the ignominy of the past might be merged in Judith's nobler guilt. But I must tell you that in the supreme hour, Destiny at her beck, her main desire was to slap the man for his childishness. Oh, he had no right thus to besot himself with adoration! This dejection at her feet of his high destiny awed her, and pricked her, too, with her inability to understand him. Angrily she flung away the baton. "Go! Ah, go!" she cried, like one strangling. "There has been enough of bloodshed, and I must spare you, loathing you as I do, for I cannot with my own hand murder you."

But the King was a kindly tyrant, crushing independence from his associates as lesser folk squeeze water from a sponge. "I cannot go thus. Acknowledge me to be Alain, the man you love, or else strike upon the gong."

"You are cruel!" she wailed, in her torture.

"Yes, I am cruel."

===Chinese===

"你很固执。我会与你费一番周折。但你这想法显然大错特错。你爱我是无可争辩的，我打算证明这一点。你会注意到，我完全手无寸铁，除了这把匕首，我现在把它扔出窗外——"话音未落，匕首在下方庭院里铿然落地。"我在特鲁瓦孤身一人，周围是千余法国人，其中任何一个都愿以性命换取杀死我的特权。你只需敲响身旁的锣，片刻之后我便是一具尸体。那就敲吧！因为与我同逝的还有英格兰在法兰西的权势。敲吧，凯瑟琳！如果你眼中我只是一个英格兰国王。"

她僵住了；当他看出那是出于恐惧时，心为之一跃。

"你独自前来！你竟敢！"

他带着奇妙的微笑答道："骄傲的灵魂！否则我怎能征服你？"

"你没有征服！"凯瑟琳举起锣旁的鼓槌，悬停着。天主已准她的祈求——拯救法兰西。如今，往昔及其耻辱可融入朱迪丝那更高贵的罪孽。但我必须告诉你，在这至高的时刻，命运俯首听命，而她的主要愿望却是给这男人一巴掌，为他的孩子气。哦，他无权如此痴迷于崇拜！他那高贵的宿命竟委顿于她的脚下，这景象震慑了她，也刺痛了她，因她无法理解他。她愤怒地扔掉鼓槌。"走吧！啊，走吧！"她喊道，仿佛在窒息。"血腥已经够多了，我必须放过您，尽管我厌恶您，因为我无法亲手杀死您。"

但国王是个仁慈的暴君，从他的同伴身上碾碎独立，正如常人从海绵中挤水。"我不能这样走。承认我是阿兰，你爱的那个男人，否则就敲锣。"

"您太残忍了！"她在痛苦中哀号。

"是的，我残忍。"

===Original===

Katharine raised straining arms above her head in a hard gesture of despair. "You have conquered. You know that I love you. Oh, if I could find words to voice my shame, to shriek it in your face, I could better endure it! For I love you. With all my body and heart and soul I love you. Mine is the agony, for I love you! and presently I shall stand quite still and see little Frenchmen scramble about you as hounds leap about a stag, and afterward kill you. And after that I shall live! I preserve France, but after I have slain you, Henry, I must live. Mine is the agony, the enduring agony." She stayed motionless for an interval. "God, God! Let me not fail!" Katharine breathed; and then: "O fair sweet friend, I am about to commit a vile action, but it is for the sake of the France that I love next to God. As Judith gave her body to Holofernes, I crucify my heart for the preservation of France." Very calmly she struck upon the gong.

If she could have found any reproach in his eyes during the ensuing silence, she could have borne it; but there was only love. And with all that, he smiled like one who knew the upshot of this matter.

A man-at-arms came into the room. "Germain—" said Katharine, and then again, "Germain—" She gave a swallowing motion and was silent. When she spoke it was with crisp distinctness. "Germain, fetch a harp. Messire Alain here is about to play for me."

At the man’s departure she said: "I am very pitiably weak. Need you have dragged my soul, too, in the dust? God heard my prayer, and you have forced me to deny His favor, as Peter denied Christ. My dear, be very kind to me, for I come to you naked of honor." She fell at the King’s feet, embracing his knees. "My master, be very kind to me, for there remains only your love."

He raised her to his breast. "Love is enough," he said.

She was conscious, as he held her thus, of the chain mail under his jerkin. He had come armed; he had his soldiers no doubt in the corridor; he had tricked her, it might be from the first. But that did not matter now.

"Love is enough," she told her master docilely.

Next day the English entered Troyes and in the cathedral church these two were betrothed. Henry was there magnificent in a curious suit of burnished armor; in place of his helmet-plume he wore a fox-brush ornamented with jewels, which unusual ornament afforded great matter of remark among the busybodies of both armies.

===Chinese===

凯瑟琳将绷紧的双臂高举过头，做出绝望的决绝姿态。"您征服了。您知道我爱您。哦，若我能找到言辞来表达我的耻辱，来对着您的脸尖叫它，我便能更好地忍受！因为我爱您。我以我的整个身体、心灵和灵魂爱您。痛苦是我的，因为我爱您！不久我将静静地站着，看着小法国人们围着您奔忙，如猎犬扑向雄鹿，然后杀死您。而在那之后，我将活下去！我拯救了法兰西，但在我杀死您之后，亨利，我必须活下去。痛苦是我的，持久的痛苦。"她一动不动地停留了片刻。"天主，天主！莫让我失败！"凯瑟琳低语；接着："哦，美好而甜蜜的朋友，我即将犯下一桩卑劣的行径，但这是为了我爱仅次于天主的法兰西。正如朱迪丝将身体献给荷罗孚尼（Holofernes），我为拯救法兰西而钉死我的心。"她极为平静地敲响了锣。

如果她在接下来的沉默中能从他眼中找到任何责备，她本可以承受；但那里只有爱。即便如此，他仍微笑着，仿佛知晓此事的结局。

一名武装士兵走进房间。"热尔曼（Germain）——"凯瑟琳说，继而又说，"热尔曼——"她做了个吞咽的动作，沉默了。当她再次开口时，语调清晰而干脆。"热尔曼，拿一把琴来。阿兰爵士（Messire Alain）要在此为我弹奏。"

士兵离开后，她说："我可怜地软弱。您何必也将我的灵魂拖入尘土？天主听到了我的祈求，而您迫使我否认他的恩宠，正如彼得（Peter）否认基督。我的爱人，请待我仁慈，因为我来到你身边时，已无荣誉可言。"她倒在国王脚下，抱住他的双膝。"我的主人，请待我仁慈，因为只剩下您的爱了。"

他将她扶起，拥入怀中。"爱已足够，"他说。

在他如此拥抱她时，她感到了他紧身短上衣（jerkin）下的锁子甲（chain mail）。他是武装而来的；毫无疑问，他的士兵就在走廊里；他或许从一开始就欺骗了她。但这如今已无关紧要。

"爱已足够，"她顺从地告诉她的主人。

次日，英格兰人进入特鲁瓦，在大教堂里，两人订了婚。亨利身着一套奇特的抛光盔甲，华美异常；头盔羽翎的位置，他戴着一支点缀珠宝的狐尾，这不寻常的装饰在两军好事者中引起了广泛的议论。

谨献

				安妮·布兰奇·卡贝尔（Anne Branch Cabell）

===Original===
"Ainsi a vous, Madame, a ma très haulte et très noble dame, a qui j'ayme a devoir attachement et obéissance, j'envoye ce livret."

===Chinese===
"谨以此小书奉上，夫人，吾之至高至贵之女主人，吾甘愿以忠诚与顺从相报。"

## Endnotes / 尾注

===Original===

For this perplexing matter the curious may consult Paul Verville’s *Notice sur la vie de Nicolas de Caen*, p. 93 et seq. The indebtedness to Antoine Riczi is, of course, conceded by Nicolas in his “Epilogue.” ↩︎

===Chinese===

关于此困惑之处，好奇者可查阅保罗·维尔维尔（Paul Verville）的《尼古拉斯·德·卡昂生平考》（*Notice sur la vie de Nicolas de Caen*）第93页及以下。尼古拉斯在其“尾声”中当然承认了对安托万·里齐（Antoine Riczi）的借鉴。↩︎

===Original===

She was the daughter of King Ferdinand of Leon and Castile, whose conversion to sainthood the inquisitive may find recorded elsewhere. ↩︎

===Chinese===

她是莱昂与卡斯蒂利亚国王费迪南德（Ferdinand）之女，其封圣之事，好奇者可另寻记载。↩︎

===Original===

Not without indulgence in anachronism. But Nicolas, be it repeated, was no Gradgrindian. ↩︎

===Chinese===

不无时代错置之嫌。但须再次强调，尼古拉斯绝非葛擂梗式（Gradgrindian）的刻板之人。↩︎

===Original===

Nicolas gives this ballad in full, but, for obvious reasons, his translator would prefer to do otherwise. ↩︎

===Chinese===

尼古拉斯全文收录了这首民谣，但出于显而易见的原因，其译者宁愿另作处理。↩︎

===Original===

Nicolas unaccountably omits to mention that during the French wars she had ruled England as Regent with signal capacity﻿—although this fact, as you will see more lately, is the pivot of his chronicle. ↩︎

===Chinese===

尼古拉斯莫名地省略了提及，在法兰西战争期间，她曾以摄政王身份卓越地统治英格兰——尽管此事实，如您稍后将见，乃其编年史之关键。↩︎

## The Epilogue / 跋

===Original===
"Et je fais sçavoir à tous lecteurs de ce Livret que les choses que je dis avoir vues et sues sont enregistrés icy, afin que vous pouviez les regarder selon vostre bon sens, s'il vous plaist."

Here is appended the epilogue that Messire Nicolas de Caen affixed to the book which he had made according to the best of his ability; and which (in consequence) he dared not appraise.

===Chinese===
"我谨告知本书所有读者，我所述亲身所见所知之事均已记录于此，以便诸位凭各自的明鉴予以审视——若诸位愿意的话。"

此乃尼古拉·德·卡昂阁下（Messire Nicolas de Caen）附于其竭尽所能编成之书后的跋文；此书（因此）他不敢妄加品评。

===Original===
*A Son Livret*

Intrepidly depart, my little book, into the presence of that most illustrious lady who bade me compile you. Bow down before her judgment. And if her sentence be that of a fiery death, I counsel you not to grieve at what cannot be avoided.

But, if by any miracle that glorious, strong fortress of the weak consider it advisable that you remain unburned, pass thence, my little book, to every man who may desire to purchase you, and live out your little hour among these very credulous persons; and at your appointed season perish and be forgotten. Thus may you share your betters' fate, and be at one with those famed comedies of Greek Menander and all the poignant songs of Sappho. *Et quid Pandoniae*—thus, little book, I charge you to poultice your more-merited oblivion—*quid Pandoniae restat nisi nomen Athenae*?

===Chinese===
*致我的小书*

勇敢地出发吧，我的小书，去到那位最显赫的夫人面前——正是她命我编纂了你。在她的审判前俯首。若她的判决是火刑，我劝你不必为那不可避免之事悲伤。

然而，倘若奇迹发生，那弱者之光荣而坚固的堡垒认为将你留存不焚更为妥当，那就去吧，我的小书，去到每一个愿意购买你的人身边，在这些极度轻信的人中间度过你短短的时辰；然后在注定的时节消亡并被遗忘。如此你便可与那些更伟大的作品共享命运，与希腊剧作家米南德（Menander）那些著名的喜剧、与萨福（Sappho）所有凄婉的歌谣合为一体。*Et quid Pandoniae*——小书啊，我命你以此敷贴你那更应得的遗忘——*quid Pandoniae restat nisi nomen Athenae*（潘多尼亚还剩下什么，除了雅典之名）？

===Original===
Yet even in your brief existence you may chance to meet with those who will affirm that the stories you narrate are not true and protest assertions which are only fables. To these you will reply that I, your maker, was in my youth the quite unworthy servant of the most high and noble lady, Dame Jehane, and in this period, at and about her house of Havering-Bower, conversed in my own person with Dame Katharine, then happily remarried to a private gentleman of Wales; and so obtained the matter of the ninth story and of the tenth authentically. You will say also that Messire de Montbrison afforded me the main matter of the sixth and seventh stories, and many of the songs which this book contains; and that, moreover, I once journeyed to Caer Idion and talked for some two hours with Richard Holland (whom I found a very old and garrulous and cheery person), and got of him the matter of the eighth tale in this dizain, together with much information as concerns the sixth and the seventh. And you will add that the matter of the fourth and fifth tales was in every detail related to me by my most illustrious mistress, Madame Isabella of Portugal, who had this information from her mother, an equally veracious and immaculate lady, and one that was in youth Dame Philippa's most dear associate. For the rest you must admit, unwillingly, the first three stories in this book to be a thought less solidly confirmed; although (as you will say) even in these histories I have not ever deviated from what was at odd times narrated to me by the aforementioned persons, and have always endeavored honestly to piece together that which they told me.

===Chinese===
然而即便在你短暂的存在中，你或许会遇见那些声称你所叙述的故事不真实、抗议那些不过是寓言之说的人。对这些人你将如此回应：我，你的制造者，在年轻时曾是最高贵的珍夫人（Dame Jehane）——一位极其尊崇而高尚的女士——的卑微仆从，在那段时期，在她的哈弗林-鲍尔（Havering-Bower）庄园内外，我曾亲自与凯瑟琳夫人（Dame Katharine）交谈——她当时已幸福地改嫁给一位威尔士的乡绅——由此如实获得了第九则与第十则故事的素材。你还将说道，蒙布里松阁下（Messire de Montbrison）向我提供了第六则与第七则故事的主要素材，以及本书所收的许多歌谣；而且，我曾前往卡尔·伊迪恩（Caer Idion），与理查·霍兰（Richard Holland）——我发现他是一位极为年迈、健谈而快活的人——交谈了约两个小时，从他那里获得了本韵集中第八则故事的素材，以及大量关于第六则与第七则故事的信息。你还将补充道，第四则与第五则故事的每一个细节均由我最显赫的女主人葡萄牙的伊莎贝拉夫人（Madame Isabella of Portugal）亲口讲述给我，她从她的母亲——一位同样诚实而纯洁的女士，年轻时是菲莉帕夫人（Dame Philippa）最亲密的伴侣——那里获得了这些信息。至于其余的，你必须不情愿地承认，本书前三则故事的依据稍欠扎实；尽管（正如你所言）即便在这些历史中，我也从未偏离前述诸人不时向我叙述的内容，始终诚实地努力将他们告诉我的东西拼合在一起。

===Original===
I have pieced together these tales about the women who intermarried, not very enviably, with the demon-tainted blood of Edward Longshanks, because it seems to me that these tales, when they are rightly considered, compose the initial portion of a troubling history. Whether (as some declare) the taint came from Manuel of Poictesme, or whether (as yet others say) this poison was inherited from the demon wife whom Foulques Plantagenet fetched out of hell, the blood in these men was not all human. These men might not tread equally with human beings: their wives suffered therefor, just as they that had inherited this blood suffered therefor, and all England suffered therefor. And the upshot of it I have narrated elsewhere, in the book called and entitled *The Red Cuckold*, which composes the final portion of this history, and tells of the last spilling and of the extinction of this blood.

===Chinese===
我已将这些关于那些女子的故事拼合起来——她们与爱德华·长腿（Edward Longshanks）那被恶魔污染的血脉联姻，并不十分令人艳羡——因为在我看来，这些故事若加以正确的审视，便构成了一段令人不安的历史的开篇。无论这污染是（如某些人所言）来自普瓦图的曼努埃尔（Manuel of Poictesme），还是（如另一些人所说）这毒液是继承自富尔克·金雀花（Foulques Plantagenet）从地狱中娶回的恶魔妻子，这些人血管中的血液并不全然是人的。这些人不能与常人平等相处：他们的妻子因此受苦，正如那些继承了这血脉的人因此受苦，整个英格兰也因此受苦。而这一切的结局我已在别处记述，在名为《红帽者》（*The Red Cuckold*）的书中，它构成了这段历史的终章，讲述了这血脉最后的流溢与灭绝。

===Original===
Also, my little book, you will encounter more malignant people who will jeer at you, and will say that you and I have cheated them of your purchase-money. To these you will reply, with Plutarch, *Non mi aurum posco, nec mi pretium*. Secondly you will say that, of necessity, the tailor cuts the coat according to his cloth; and that he cannot undertake to robe an Ephialtes or a towering Orion suitably when the resources of his shop amount to only a few yards of cambric. Indeed had I the power to make you better, my little book, I would have exercised that power to the utmost. A good conscience is a continual feast, and I summon high Heaven to be my witness that had I been Homer you had awed the world, another Iliad. I lament your inability to do this, as heartily as any person living; yet Heaven willed it; and it is in consequence to Heaven these aforementioned cavillers should rightfully complain.

===Chinese===
此外，我的小书，你还会遇见更多刻薄之人，他们将嘲弄你，说你和我骗了他们的购书钱。对这些人你将如此回应，援引普鲁塔克（Plutarch）之言：*Non mi aurum posco, nec mi pretium*（我不索取金子，也不索取代价）。其次你将说道，裁缝量体裁衣乃必然之理；当店铺的全部家当仅有几码细麻布时，他无法承诺为巨人厄菲阿尔特斯（Ephialtes）或高耸的猎户座（Orion）合身地裁制衣裳。事实上，若我有能力将你做得更好，我的小书，我本会竭尽全力运用这种能力。问心无愧乃无尽的盛宴，我召请上天作证——倘若我是荷马，你本可令世界敬畏，成为又一部《伊利亚特》。我对此无能为力的痛惜，不亚于任何一个在世之人；然而天意如此；因此前述那些吹毛求疵者理当向天意申诉。

===Original===
So to such impious people do you make no answer at all, unless indeed you should elect to answer them by repetition of this song which I now make for you, my little book, at your departure from me. And the song runs in this fashion:

Depart, depart, my book! and live and die

Dependent on the idle fantasy

Of men who cannot view you, quite, as I.

For I am fond, and willingly mistake

My book to be the book I meant to make,

And cannot judge you, for that phantom's sake.

Yet pardon me if I have wrought too ill

In making you, that never spared the will

To shape you perfectly, and lacked the skill.

Ah, had I but the power, my book, then I

Had wrought in you some wizardry so high

That no man but had listened…

They pass by,

And shrug—as we, who know that unto us

It has been granted never to fare thus,

And never to be strong and glorious.

Is it denied me to perpetuate

What so much loving labor did create?—

I hear Oblivion tap upon the gate,

And acquiesce, not all disconsolate.

For I have got such recompense

Of that high-hearted excellence

Which the contented craftsman knows,

Alone, that to loved labor goes,

And daily does the work he chose,

And counts all else impertinence!

===Chinese===
所以对那些不虔敬之人，你一个字也不必回应，除非你真想以这首歌作为回答——我在你离我而去之际为你谱写的这首歌。歌是这样唱的：

去吧，去吧，我的书！去活去死，

寄望于那些闲人异想天开

——他们无法像我这般将你审视。

因为我痴愚，心甘情愿地误认

我写的书便是我本想写成的那本，

因此无法公正地评判你。

但请宽恕我若将你造得太拙劣，

我从未吝惜要把你塑得尽善尽美，

只是力有不逮。

唉，若我有那力量，我的书，我便

在你身上施以那般高妙的魔法

令世人无一不侧耳倾听……

他们路过，

耸耸肩——正如我们，深知命运从不曾

赐予我们如此际遇，

令我们强大而荣耀。

难道我不配让倾注了那么多爱的

劳动成果得以永存？——

我听见遗忘之手在门上轻叩，

我默然应允，并非全然心灰意冷。

因为我已获得那份报偿——

那心满意足的匠人才知的

崇高而纯粹的报偿：

他独自走向心爱的劳作，

日复一日做着他选择的活计，

视余者皆为无关紧要之事！

Explicit Decas Reginarum / 《十女王集》终

## Precautional / 预防性声明

===Original===
I

Imprimis, as concerns the authenticity of these tales perhaps the less debate may be the higher wisdom, if only because this Nicolas de Caen, by common report, was never a Gradgrindian. And in this volume in particular, writing it (as Nicolas is supposed to have done) in 1470, as a dependant on the Duke of Burgundy, it were but human nature should he, in dealing with the putative descendants of Dom Manuel and Alianora of Provence, be niggardly in his ascription of praiseworthy traits to any member of the house of Lancaster or of Valois. Rather must one in common reason accept old Nicolas as confessedly a partisan writer, who upon occasion will recolor an event with such nuances as will be least inconvenient to a Yorkist and Burgundian bias.

===Chinese===
一

首先，关于这些故事的真实性，或许少一些争论方为上策——只因这位尼古拉·德·卡昂（Nicolas de Caen），据普遍传闻，从来就不是一个格拉金迪式的刻板之人。尤其在本卷中，据称尼古拉写作于一四七〇年，身为勃艮第公爵的附庸，在涉及多姆·曼努埃尔（Dom Manuel）与普罗旺斯的阿丽亚诺拉（Alianora of Provence）的虚构后裔时，要他慷慨地将值得称颂的品性赋予兰开斯特家族或瓦卢瓦家族的任何成员，实属人之常情。理性的读者理当承认老尼古拉是一位坦率的党派作家，偶尔会以最不碍于约克派与勃艮第派立场的微妙色彩重新渲染某一事件。

===Original===
The reteller of these stories needs in addition to plead guilty of having abridged the tales with a free hand. Item, these tales have been a trifle pulled about, most notably in "The Story of the Satraps," where it seemed advantageous, on reflection, to put into Gloucester's mouth a history which in the original version was related ab ovo, and as a sort of bungling prologue to the story proper.

===Chinese===
这些故事的转述者还须坦承，自己曾大刀阔斧地删节了这些故事。其二，这些故事也做了些许调整，最明显的是在《总督的故事》（The Story of the Satraps）中——事后回想，将原版中从头道来、笨拙地充当故事引子的一段历史，改由格洛斯特（Gloucester）之口说出，似更为妥当。

===Original===
Item, the reteller of these stories desires hereby to tender appropriate acknowledgment to Mr. R. E. Townsend for his assistance in making an English version of the lyrics included hereinafter; and to avoid discussion as to how freely, in these lyrics, Nicolas has plagiarized from Raimbaut de Vaqueiras and other elder poets.1

And—"sixth and lastly"—should confession be made that in the present rendering a purely arbitrary title has been assigned this little book; chiefly for commercial reasons, since the word "dizain" has been adjudged both untranslatable and, in its pristine form, repellantly outré.

===Chinese===
其三，这些故事的转述者谨此向R. E. 汤森先生（Mr. R. E. Townsend）致以恰当的谢意，感谢他协助将本书所附抒情诗译成英文；同时也意在回避一个话题——尼古拉在这些诗作中从雷姆博·德·瓦凯拉（Raimbaut de Vaqueiras）及其他前辈诗人处借鉴了多少。

其六也是最后一项——须坦白承认，在目前这个译本中，这本小书被赋予了一个纯粹武断的书名；这主要是出于商业考量，因为"dizain"一词已被判定既无法翻译，其原始形式又令人望而生畏地不合时宜。

===Original===
II

You are to give my titular makeshift, then, a wide interpretation; and are always to remember that in the bleak, florid age these tales commemorate this Chivalry was much the rarelier significant of any personal trait than of a worldwide code in consonance with which all estimable people lived and died. Its root was the assumption (uncontested then) that a gentleman will always serve his God, his honor and his lady without any reservation; nor did the many emanating bylaws ever deal with special cases as concerns this triple, fixed, and fundamental homage.

Such is the trinity served hereinafter. Now about lady-service, or domnei, I have written elsewhere. Elsewhere also I find it recorded that "the cornerstone of Chivalry is the idea of vicarship: for the chivalrous person is, in his own eyes at least, the child of God, and goes about this world as his Father's representative in an alien country."

===Chinese===
二

因此，读者当对我这个权宜的书名作宽泛的理解；并须始终铭记，在这些故事所纪念的那个荒凉而华丽的年代，"骑士精神"（Chivalry）极少指涉个人品性，而更多指一套普世的准则——所有可敬之人都据此准则生与死。其根基在于一个当时无人质疑的预设：一位绅士将毫无保留地侍奉他的上帝、他的荣誉和他的女士；由此衍生的诸多细则，从未对这三重、固定且根本的敬奉之礼涉及的特殊情形作过任何规定。

下文所展现的，正是这一三重侍奉。关于侍奉女士之礼，即"多姆内"（domnei），我在别处已有论述。在别处我亦找到这样的记载："骑士精神的基石是代理的理念：因为骑士——至少在他自己眼中——是上帝之子，在这个陌生的国度行走于世，充当他天父的代言人。"

===Original===
I believe the definition holds: it certainly tends to explain the otherwise puzzling pertinacity with which the characters in these tales talk about God and act upon an assured knowledge as to Heaven's private intentions and preferences. These people are the members of one family engrossed, as all of us are apt to be when in the society of our kin, by family matters and traditions and bywords. It is not merely that they are all large children consciously dependent in all things upon a not foolishly indulgent Father, Who keeps an interested eye upon the least of their doansings, and punishes at need—not merely that they know themselves to act under surveillance and to speak within earshot of a divine eavesdropper. The point is, rather, that they know this observation to be as tender, the punishment to be as unwilling, as that which they themselves extend to their own children's pranks and misdemeanors. The point is that to them Heaven is a place as actual and tangible as we consider Alaska or Algiers to be, and that their living is a conscious journeying toward this actual place. The point is that the Father is a real father, and not a word spelt with capital letters in the Church Service; not an abstraction, not a sort of a something vaguely describable as "the Life Force," but a very famous kinsman, of whom one is naively proud, and whom one is on the way to visit. … The point, in brief, is that His honor and yours are inextricably blended, and are both implicated in your behavior on the journey.

===Chinese===
我相信这个定义是成立的：它无疑有助于解释——这些故事中的人物何以会以那种令人费解的执拗谈论上帝，并依据对上天隐秘旨意和偏好的确信行事。这些人是同一个家族的成员，正如我们与亲人相处时往往沉浸于家事、传统和谚语之中，他们同样如此。这不仅仅是说他们全都是些长不大的孩子，清醒地知道自己事事依赖一位并非愚蠢纵容的天父——这位天父密切关注他们最细微的举动，并在必要时施以惩罚；也不仅仅意味着他们知道自己身处监视之下，在一位神圣的窃听者的听力范围之内说话。关键毋宁在于，他们知道这种注视是温柔的，这种惩罚是不情愿的，正如他们自己对待儿女们的恶作剧和过失时所展现的那样。关键在于，对他们而言，天堂是一个与我们眼中的阿拉斯加或阿尔及尔同样真实可触的地方，而他们的生活是朝着这个真实之地的一场自觉的旅程。关键在于，这位天父是一位真实的父亲，而非礼拜仪式中以大写字母拼写的一个词语；不是某种抽象概念，不是某种可模糊描述为"生命力"的东西，而是一位非常显赫的亲人——人们天真地为之自豪，并正在前去探访的路上。……简言之，关键在于，祂的荣誉与你的荣誉不可分割地交织在一起，而两者都牵涉到你在旅途中的行为。

===Original===
We nowadays can just cloudily imagine this viewing of life as a sort of boarding-school from which one eventually goes home, with an official report as to progress and deportment: and in retaliation for being debarred from the comforts of this view, the psychoanalysts have no doubt invented for it some opprobrious explanation. At all events, this Chivalry was a pragmatic hypothesis: it "worked," and served society for a long while, not faultlessly of course, but by creating, like all the other codes of human conduct which men have yet tried, a tragicomic melee wherein contended "courtesy and humanity, friendliness, hardihood, love and friendship, and murder, hate, and virtue, and sin."

===Chinese===
我们如今只能朦胧地将这种人生观想象为一种寄宿学校——人最终会回家去，带着一份关于学业与品行的官方报告：而精神分析学家们无疑为此种美好想象之丧失发明了一个可耻的解释作为报复。无论如何，这骑士精神乃是一个实用主义的假说：它"行之有效"，长久地服务于社会——当然并非完美无缺——但正如人类迄今尝试过的所有其他行为准则一样，它创造了一出悲喜交集的混战，其中"礼仪与人性、友善、刚毅、爱与友谊，以及谋杀、仇恨、美德与罪孽"彼此交锋。

===Original===
III

For the rest, since good wine needs no bush, and an inferior beverage is not likely to be bettered by arboreal adornment, I elect to piece out my exordium (however lamely) with "The Printer's Preface." And it runs in this fashion:

"Here begins the volume called and entitled the Dizain of Queens, composed and extracted from divers chronicles and other sources of information, by that extremely venerable person and worshipful man, Messire Nicolas de Caen, priest and chaplain to the right noble, glorious and mighty prince in his time, Philippe, Duke of Burgundy, of Brabant, etc., in the year of the Incarnation of our Lord God a thousand four hundred and seventy: and imprinted by me, Colard Mansion, at Bruges, in the year of our said Lord God a thousand four hundred and seventy-one; at the commandment of the right high, mighty and virtuous Princess, my redoubted Lady, Isabella of Portugal, by the grace of God Duchess of Burgundy and Lotharingia, of Brabant and Limbourg, of Luxembourg and of Gueldres, Countess of Flanders, of Artois, and of Burgundy, Palatine of Hainault, of Holland, of Zealand and of Namur, Marquesse of the Holy Empire, and Lady of Frisia, of Salins and of Mechlin; whom I beseech Almighty God less to increase than to continue in her virtuous disposition in this world, and after our poor fleet existence to receive eternally. Amen."

===Chinese===
三

其余事项，既然好酒无需招牌，劣饮亦非树木装饰所能增色，我便选择以"印刷商前言"来补全我的开场白（无论多么力不从心）。其文如下：

"兹开始此卷，题名为《女王十韵集》（Dizain of Queens），由极其可敬的尊者尼古拉·德·卡昂——当世尊贵、光荣而强大的王子、勃艮第与布拉班特等公爵菲利普殿下之神父与随堂牧师——采撷多种编年史及其他资料来源编撰而成，时值我主上帝道成肉身之年一千四百七十年：由我科拉尔·曼西翁（Colard Mansion）于布鲁日印制，时值我上述主上帝之年一千四百七十一年；奉至尊、强大而贤德的公主殿下——我所敬畏的夫人、葡萄牙的伊莎贝拉（Isabella of Portugal）之命，蒙上帝恩典为勃艮第与洛林、布拉班特与林堡、卢森堡与海尔德公爵夫人，佛兰德斯、阿图瓦与勃艮第女伯爵，埃诺、荷兰、泽兰与那慕尔的帕拉丁女伯爵，神圣帝国侯爵夫人，弗里斯兰、萨兰与梅赫伦女领主；我恳求全能的上帝，与其在此世增添她的美德，不如延续她美好的性情，并在我们短暂的尘世生命之后永远接纳她。阿门。"

## The Prologue / 序章

===Original===
"Afin que les entreprises honorables et les nobles aventures et faicts d'armes soyent noblement enregistrés et conservés, je vais traiter et raconter et inventer ung galimatias."

The Dizain of Queens of that noble maker in the French tongue, Messire Nicolas de Caen, dedicated to the most illustrious Isabella of Portugal, of the house of the indomitable Alfonso Henriques, and Duchess Dowager of Burgundy. Here begins in auspicious wise the prologue.

===Chinese===
"为使光荣的事业、高贵的冒险与赫赫战功得以庄重地记录与保存，我将论述、讲述并编造一篇杂谈。"

此乃法兰西语之高贵作者尼古拉·德·卡昂阁下（Messire Nicolas de Caen）所著《女王十韵集》（Dizain of Queens），谨献于最显赫的葡萄牙的伊莎贝拉（Isabella of Portugal）——不可征服的阿方索·恩里克斯（Alfonso Henriques）家族之裔，勃艮第太妃殿下。序章于此吉庆开篇。

===Original===
*A Sa Dame*

Inasmuch as it was by your command, illustrious and exalted lady, that I have gathered together these stories to form the present little book, you should the less readily suppose I have presumed to dedicate to your Serenity this trivial offering because of my esteeming it to be not undeserving of your acceptance. The truth is otherwise: your postulant approaches not spurred toward you by vainglory, but rather by equity, and equity's plain need to acknowledge that he who seeks to write of noble ladies must necessarily implore at outset the patronage of her who is the light and mainstay of our age. I humbly bring my book to you as Phidyle approached another and less sacred shrine, *farre pio et saliente mica*, and lay before you this my valueless mean tribute not as appropriate to you but as the best I have to offer.

===Chinese===
*献予我的夫人*

正因奉了您的谕命，尊贵而崇高的夫人，我才将这些故事搜集整理成眼前这本小书，故而您当不致轻易以为，我冒昧将此微末之礼呈献于您的御前，乃因自认它配得上您的垂纳。实情并非如此：我这个恳求者趋近您前，并非受虚荣心驱策，而是出于公义——公义那明白无误的需要，即凡欲书写高贵女士之人，必当在开篇之初恳请我们这个时代之光与支柱的庇佑。我恭谨地将此书呈至您前，如同菲迪勒（Phidyle）走近另一座不甚神圣的庙宇，献上"虔诚的麦粉与一撮盐"（farre pio et saliente mica），将这鄙陋的微薄敬意呈于您面前——并非因为它配得上您，而是因为这是我所能献出的最好的东西。

===Original===
It is a little book wherein I treat of divers queens and of their love-business; and with necessitated candor I concede my chosen field to have been harvested, and scrupulously gleaned, by many writers of innumerable conditions. Since Dares Phrygius wrote of Queen Heleine, and Virgil (that shrewd necromancer) of Queen Dido, a preponderating mass of clerks, in casting about for high and serious matter, have chosen, as though it were by common instinct, to dilate upon the amours of royal women. Even in romance we scribblers must contrive it so that the fair Nicolete shall be discovered in the end to be no less than the King's daughter of Carthage, and that Sir Doön of Mayence shall never sink in his love affairs beneath the degree of a Saracen princess; and we are backed in this old procedure not only by the authority of Aristotle but, oddly enough, by that of reason.

===Chinese===
这是一本小书，我在其中谈及若干女王及其情爱之事；我不得不坦然承认，我选定的这个领域已被无数身份各异的作家反复耕耘、精细拾穗。自达雷斯·弗里基乌斯（Dares Phrygius）书写了海伦女王（Queen Heleine），维吉尔（那个精明的巫术师）书写了狄多女王（Queen Dido）以来，数量庞大的文人在寻觅崇高而严肃的题材时，仿佛出于本能一般，都选择了详述王室女性的爱情。即便在传奇故事中，我们这些涂鸦之辈也须设法让美丽的妮可莱特（Nicolete）最终被发现竟是迦太基国王之女，让美因茨的杜恩爵士（Sir Doön of Mayence）在情场上永不低于一位撒拉逊公主的身份；我们沿袭这一古老做法，不仅有亚里士多德的权威为靠山，而且——说来也怪——还有理性的支持。

===Original===
Kings have their policies and wars wherewith to drug each human appetite. But their consorts are denied these makeshifts; and love may rationally be defined as the pivot of each normal woman's life, and in consequence as the arbiter of that ensuing life which is eternal. Because—as anciently Propertius demanded, though not, to speak the truth, of any woman—

===Chinese===
国王有其权谋与战争，足以麻醉人间种种欲望。但他们的配偶却被剥夺了这些权宜之计；爱——理性地讲——可以被定义为每一个正常女性生命的核心枢纽，因而也是那随之而来的永恒生命的裁决者。因为——正如古罗马诗人普罗佩提乌斯（Propertius）当年所追问的，尽管平心而论，他并非在向任何女性发问——

===Original===
Quo fugis? ah demens! nulla est fuga, tu licet usque

Ad Tanaim fugias, usque sequetur amor.

===Chinese===
Quo fugis? ah demens! nulla est fuga, tu licet usque

Ad Tanaim fugias, usque sequetur amor.

===Original===
And a dairymaid, let us say, may love whom she will, and nobody else be a penny the worse for her mistaking of the preferable nail whereon to hang her affections; whereas with a queen this choice is more portentous. She plays the game of life upon a loftier table, ruthlessly illuminated, she stakes by her least movement a tall pile of counters, some of which are, of necessity, the lives and happiness of persons whom she knows not, unless it be by vague report. Grandeur sells itself at this hard price, and at no other. A queen must always play, in fine, as the vicar of destiny, free to choose but very certainly compelled in the ensuing action to justify that choice: as is strikingly manifested by the authentic histories of Brunhalt, and of Guenevere, and of swart Cleopatra, and of many others that were born to the barbaric queenhoods of extinct and dusty times.

===Chinese===
比方说，一个挤奶女工可以随心所欲地爱谁便爱谁，不会因为错把感情挂在了不够理想的钉子上而让旁人遭受哪怕一文钱的损失；然而对一位女王而言，这个选择便意味深长了。她是在一张更高处的牌桌上进行人生的游戏，被无情地照亮；她最微不足道的举动也押上了一大摞筹码，其中必定有一些，是她素不相识之人的生命与幸福——除非经由模糊的传闻。显赫以此高昂的代价出售自身，别无他途。总之，一位女王必须始终以命运代理人的身份出场，有选择的自由，但在随后的行动中必定被迫为这个选择作出辩白：布伦希尔特（Brunhalt）、格尼薇儿（Guenevere）与黝黑的克利奥帕特拉（Cleopatra），以及许多其他生于往昔蛮荒年代、蒙尘已久的野蛮女王，她们的真实历史对此已有触目惊心的印证。

===Original===
All royal persons are (I take it) the immediate and the responsible stewards of Heaven; and since the nature of each man is like a troubled stream, now muddied and now clear, their prayer must ever be, *Defenda me, Dios, de me*! Yes, of exalted people, and even of their near associates, life, because it aims more high than the aforementioned Aristotle, demands upon occasion a more great catharsis, which would purge any audience of unmanliness, through pity and through terror, because, by a quaint paradox, the players have been purged of humanity. For a moment Destiny has thrust her scepter into the hands of a human being and Chance has exalted a human being to decide the issue of many human lives. These two—with what immortal chucklings one may facilely imagine—have left the weakling thus enthroned, free to direct the heavy outcome, free to choose, and free to evoke much happiness or age-long weeping, but with no intermediate course unbarred. "Now prove thyself!" saith Destiny; and Chance appends: "Now prove thyself to be at bottom a god or else a beast, and now eternally abide that choice. And now (*O crowning irony!*) we may not tell thee clearly by which choice thou mayst prove either."

===Chinese===
一切王室成员——我如此认为——都是天庭直接而负责的管家；既然人性如同一条浑浊的河流，时而浑浊时而清澈，他们的祈祷必定永远是：*Defenda me, Dios, de me*（上帝啊，救我于我自身）！是的，对于显贵之人，甚至对于他们身边亲近的人，生命——因为它比前述亚里士多德所追求的更为崇高——有时要求一种更为伟大的净化，一种借由怜悯与恐惧来涤除一切怯懦之气的净化，因为——说来悖论颇妙——剧中人自身已丧失了人性的温存。在某一刻，命运将权杖塞入一个凡人的手中，而偶然将一个凡人提升到裁决众生命运的位置。这二者——人们不难想象它们那不朽的窃笑——将这个被如此扶上宝座的懦弱之人留在那里，自由地指引沉重的结局，自由地选择，自由地唤起无尽的幸福或亘古的泪水，但绝无中间道路可走。"现在证明你自己吧！"命运如是说；偶然补充道："现在证明你骨子里究竟是个神还是头野兽，并永远承担那个选择的后果。而现在（*啊，最登峰造极的讽刺！*）我们不能清楚地告诉你，哪一个选择能证明其中哪一面。"

===Original===
In this little book about the women who intermarried, not very enviably, with an unhuman race (a race predestinate to the red ending which I have chronicled elsewhere, in *The Red Cuckold*), it is of ten such moments that I treat.

===Chinese===
在这本关于与一个非人类种族联姻——这联姻并不十分令人艳羡——的小书中，我所讲述的正是十个这样的时刻（这个种族命中注定要迎来我在他处、在《红帽者》（*The Red Cuckold*）中所记述的那个血色结局）。

===Original===
You alone, I think, of all persons living, have learned, as you have settled by so many instances, to rise above mortality in such a testing, and unfailingly to merit by your conduct the plaudits and the adoration of our otherwise dissentient world. You have often spoken in the stead of Destiny, with nations to abide your verdict; and in so doing have both graced and hallowed your high vicarship. If I forbear to speak of this at greater length, it is because I dare not couple your well-known perfection with any imperfect encomium. Upon no plea, however, can anyone forbear to acknowledge that he who seeks to write of noble ladies must necessarily implore at outset the patronage of her who is the light and mainstay of our age.

===Chinese===
我想，在所有在世之人中，唯独您——正如您以无数实例所证明的那样——已经学会了在这种考验中超越凡人的局限，始终以您的品行赢得我们这个本不协调的世界的喝彩与崇拜。您常常代替命运发言，万国臣服于您的裁决；在如此作为中，您既荣耀又神圣化了您崇高的代理人之职。我之所以不再详述，是因为我不敢将您那举世皆知的完美与任何不完美的颂词并列。然而，无论以何种理由，任何人都无法回避这样一个承认：凡欲书写高贵女士之人，必当在开篇之初恳请我们这个时代之光与支柱的庇佑。

===Original===
*Therefore to you, madame—most excellent and noble lady, to whom I love to owe both loyalty and love—I dedicate this little book.*

===Chinese===
*因此，夫人——最卓越而高贵的女士，我乐于将忠诚与爱一并献于您前——谨将这本小书献给您。*
